Measured on the engine's own render of C4, where the model is exactly true and the residual should be nothing, it stands at **−5 dB** under the note from the strike and at **−16 dB** from half a window later. `FitSpan` already applies the same rule one measurement upstream.
    * **The shape is fitted over 200 Hz – 8 kHz, and the level is measured on the same band-limited signal.** In octave bands C4's residual peaks at **62–125 Hz**, an octave below its own fundamental, and carries a second and far broader plateau from 250 Hz to 4 kHz about 15 dB under it; A2 does the same; C7's is flat from 31 Hz to 2 kHz. A shape fitted to the whole thing is a 100 Hz thump at every key — a sound the engine already makes twice over (the action's four events are centred at 77–300 Hz, and the board's own modes put a strike's low frequencies into every render). The band chosen is the schema's own, which is the engine saying what this event is for. The centroid is the **geometric** power-weighted mean frequency, because a constant-`Q` band-pass is symmetric in `log f`; the bandwidth is where the third-octave *density* has fallen 20 dB below its own maximum, because that is what a low-pass corner is and an energy quantile answers a different question.
    * **It is refused at both ends of the compass, for the same reason in two units.** Below 100 Hz four periods of the fundamental is more than the 40 ms an attack can be measured in and the subtraction stops separating the partials at all: A0's "residual" came back **2.6 dB** under the note, which is the note. Above C7 the tracker has two or three partials to subtract from a note that is mostly not lines, and C8's came back **1.0 dB** under it. The gates are a minimum fundamental, a minimum partial count, a maximum level and a minimum layer count; what survives is **17 of Salamander's 30 sampled keys, A2 to A6**, and the anchors either side are that range held.

203. **The damper is one number, not eighty-eight, and it is inverted on a line the engine draws.** `DECISIONS.md` 183 left this fit undone; `estimate::damper` does it from Salamander's 69 `harmL*`/`harmS*`/`harmV3*` release recordings. Two renders per key at two `damper_sigma` values give the line — `1/T20` is affine in `damper_sigma`, so two points determine it, and inverting a line measured on the engine puts the damper's ramp, the bank's cull, the board's 0.4 s diffuse field and the master chain on the same side of the comparison as the recording's own room. What is written is the **median of the per-key ratios applied to the whole table** (`PresetBuilder`'s documented single-measurement rule, which scales the base curve rather than flattening it), because the data does not support eighty-eight: the ratios span **0.053 to 0.561** while their median is stable, fewer than half the keys invert at all (at the other 13 even a damper turned off entirely leaves the engine's tail shorter than the recording's, so the line saturates and the key has measured a bound), and nothing in these recordings distinguishes one key's damper from its neighbour's by more than that scatter. The answer is **`damper_sigma × 0.198`**, a factor of five. Verified by rendering the fitted table and measuring it with the recordings' own code: recorded 0.185–0.460 s against rendered **0.235–0.390 s**, median 0.30 against 0.29, where before the fit the engine ran 0.05–0.155 s.

204. **`voicing.damper_weight` was left where it was, and that is a measurement rather than a default.** The anchors say how much less the felt grips a partial at 6 kHz than one at 500 Hz — a shape *within* one note, where the release recordings measure one number *per* note. `damper::band_release` measures the diagnostic that would move them: the same tail's 20 dB decay in a low band and a high band split at four times the note's fundamental, on a 12 dB/octave crossover. Salamander's high band outlasts its low band by a median factor of **1.28** over the 23 keys that have both; the shipped anchors (1.0 at 500 Hz falling to 0.9 at 2 kHz) deliver **1.18**, inside the 0.82–2.64 the individual keys scatter over. Nothing asks for a change.

205. **What the fit did to the scoreboard, including the column it moved the wrong way and why.** `renders/realism/REALISM.md` re-measured on the fitted preset, against item 186's first reading: mean mel **5.34 → 5.22 dB** (floor 1.59), worst phrase `staccato` **5.50 → 4.60** and `chords_pedal` **4.97 → 4.70**, against `arpeggio_dynamics` 5.38 → 5.56 and `alberti_fast` 6.46 → 6.56. The engine's attacks read **+5.17 → +4.98 dB** of spectral tonality, the mean onset-to-peak rise **12.5 → 14.4 ms** against the recordings' 18.5, and the envelope lag **−18.7 → −15.1 ms**: the engine's energy still leaves early, but less. The **release column went the other way, 1.85 → 5.62 dB**, and the cause is item 203 doing exactly what it was asked to. The benchmark's release ground truth is not the instrument: it is `piano_tuner::sampler` playing Salamander's note group, whose `ampeg_release=1` falls to silence in a second — a T20 of 0.2 s, a synthesis choice of the library's author. The `harm*` recordings the damper was fitted against are the Yamaha's own strings being stopped by its own felt, and they say 0.27–0.47 s. Two ground truths, one of them a recording of a piano and one of them an envelope in a text file; the damper follows the recording, and the benchmark's release column is hereby documented as measuring the library's release envelope rather than the instrument's damper. The phrases with many clean release windows barely move (`chords_pedal` ×21: 2.03 → 1.37, `staccato` ×26: 2.86 → 3.21); the mean is carried by `alberti_fast` and `excerpt`, which have two and five.

206. **The attack's tonality is not level-limited, and that is measured rather than assumed.** The milestone's target was the attack column to ~0 and it moved 0.19 dB. Before concluding that the fitted level is too conservative — it is a documented bound, 4 to 7 dB under the burst that produced it (item 202) — the same preset was re-measured with every `[noise.strike]` anchor **6 dB hotter**: attack tonality **+4.98 → +4.80 dB** for **+0.03 dB of mel**. Six decibels of level buys 0.18 dB of the column. So the +4.8 dB that remains is not a quiet hammer noise; it is something else in the first 30 ms, and raising this event to chase it would be tuning to a benchmark rather than measuring an instrument. The hotter variant is not shipped.

207. **Coverage, honestly.** Of Salamander's 30 sampled keys: **24 carry `partial_gains`** (6 to 75 entries per row, spanning −20 to +20 dB, with 19 entries in total clamped at a bound), **24 carry `partial_sigma_scale`** (4 to 67 trusted partials per key), **11 carry a non-zero `comb_floor`** (0.031 to 0.450, the rest measured at zero per item 198), and **17 anchor `[noise.strike]`**. The six keys above C7 carry none of the three per-partial fields: they have two to five partials and `ShapingConfig::min_partials` is six. The other 58 keys of the compass carry an interpolated `comb_floor` and an empty row for both ragged tables — deliberately, and this is the one table in the preset that is *not* interpolated across the compass, because `TUNING_REPORT.md` §3 measured that the roughness is not shared between notes at the same frequency and a row invented for an unsampled key would be its neighbour's roughness under its own partials.

208. **Neutrality, proven by render.** `presets/default.toml` is unchanged on disk and its two reference renders are item 172's hashes for the fifth time: `render demo` md5 `94d9d0764c98fbf76a2cdd3bfa37d3a2`, `render` sweep `ad120191fd5eb16f61e1fe74a3203d33`. Everything in this pass is tuner-side: the engine crate is untouched by it, and the only file in `presets/` that moved is `salamander-c5.toml`, whose demo goes from `fb7e77e43a1653d3fd79efe9784b8f0f` (item 194's) to `d5a5aa7c9a43def2b72347fb20ac0f1b` — which is the point of the milestone. `cargo test --release --workspace` is **437 tests green**; `cargo clippy --release --workspace --all-targets` is **0 diagnostics**, down from the 22 the tree carried before this pass in the three ladder examples.

209. **The milestone's gate: attack passed, mel failed, and the failure is structural rather than a fit that was not tried.** The scoreboard the gate reads (`renders/realism/REALISM.md`, regenerated on the shipped `presets/salamander-c5.toml`): mean mel **5.34 → 5.17 dB** against a floor of 1.59, needed ≤ 4.34; attack tonality **+5.17 → +0.54 dB signed** (mean absolute 6.81 → 4.77), needed ≤ +2.67; and no phrase's mel rose by more than 0.2 dB. Per phrase, the preset at the milestone's head against the one it ships (`scale_mf` 5.09 → 5.06, `arpeggio_dynamics` 5.38 → 5.50, `chords_pedal` 4.97 → 4.68, `staccato` 5.50 → 4.51, `alberti_fast` 6.46 → 6.48, `excerpt` 4.62 → 4.76): the largest rise anywhere is **+0.14** (`excerpt`) and the largest fall is **−0.99** (`staccato`). Two of the three criteria are met and the first is missed by 0.83 dB. Items 210 to 214 are what moved it, and 215 is the demonstration that nothing left inside this schema closes the rest.

210. **`[noise.strike]` is fitted in the band the engine's own attack is *missing*, not the band the residual is loudest in — and that one change is the whole of the attack column.** Item 202 measured the residual over the schema's whole 200 Hz – 8 kHz and took its median: a 344 Hz centroid under a 1008 Hz limit, which moved `REALISM.md`'s attack column 0.19 dB, and 6 dB more of it moved another 0.18 (item 206). That is the signature of an event playing where nothing was missing, and the octave-band measurement says so directly. Over the benchmark's own onsets the engine's first 30 ms sits **−0.2 dB** against the recordings at 200–400 Hz, **−2.0** at 400–800, **−6.3** at 800–1600, **−17.5** at 1.6–3.2 kHz, **−19.8** at 3.2–6.4 kHz and **−13.9** at 6.4–10 kHz. The bottom of the residual is a sound the engine already makes three ways over — its own low partials, the board's modes, and the action's four events, which are centred at 77–300 Hz and stop at `noise::BANDWIDTH_HZ` — and the top of it is a sound nothing in the engine makes at all, which is the sentence the schema contract wrote this event's `bandwidth_hz` field for. `estimate::attack::deficit_band` therefore takes the same third-octave density from the recording's onset residual *and from a render of the same key at the same drive with the strike silenced*, each against its own note's peak, and returns the contiguous run of third-octaves around the largest deficit where the engine is at least `AttackConfig::deficit_db` = 6 dB short. On Salamander's five anchor keys that is 2540–3200 (A2), 3200–4032 (A3), 2016–6400 (A4), 3200–6400 (A5) and 1600–6400 Hz (A6); the median is **2540 Hz .. 6400 Hz**, and `AttackConfig::band_hz` measures the level, the centroid, the limit and the decay inside it. The result is **3304 Hz centroid, 6400 Hz band limit, 0.258 s, 24.4 dB of velocity**, at a median residual flatness of **−3.5 dB** against the old fit's −13.1 — which is the point: what is missing is a continuum, and what was measured before was the loudest line in a band the engine was not short in.

211. **The strike's level is inverted on the engine, because the first band-limited fit overshot by 11.8 dB and the render is the only thing that knows it.** Fitted straight from the recording, the new band's anchors are −38.3 to −16.0 dB and they take the attack column from +4.98 dB to **−4.66**: the engine's attacks become *noisier* than the piano's by as much as they were tonal. Three things separate what a residual measures from what the engine has to play, all of them named in `estimate::attack`'s own header and all of them larger inside 2.5–6.4 kHz than anywhere else: the tracker's subtraction error inside an attack (a documented floor of about −16 dB for a 12 ms rise, and `REALISM.md` puts real attacks at 13–22 ms), the string partials above the tracker's reach that count as residual and live exactly there, and a burst normalised on its *peak* rather than on its density. All three ride along identically in a render, so the level is inverted on the engine the way `CombLine` inverts the comb floor (item 199) and `DamperLine` inverts the damper (203): `strike_offset` renders the fitted strike at 0/−8/−16 dB, measures the engine's own onset residual density inside the fitted band with the code the recording went through, and reads off the offset that puts the two densities on top of each other. Per key that is −16.0 / −11.4 / −0.7 / −16.0 / −11.8 dB, whose **median −11.8 dB** moves the whole table — `PresetBuilder`'s single-measurement rule again. The shipped anchors are **−50.1 / −50.1 / −46.8 / −41.1 / −35.5 / −27.8 dB** at keys 45 / 57 / 69 / 81 / 93 / 102.

212. **What the strike did, per phrase, and the one column it moved the wrong way.** Signed attack tonality, engine minus reference, before → after: `scale_mf` +0.44 → −1.88, `arpeggio_dynamics` +0.24 → −2.17, `chords_pedal` +9.27 → **+5.88**, `staccato` +11.65 → −2.33, `alberti_fast` +0.39 → −0.58, `excerpt` +7.88 → +4.35. The two phrases that were already inside a decibel are now about two decibels the other way, which is the price of one global colour for eighty-eight hammers; the two that were 8–12 dB out are the ones the event was measured for. Mean mel moved 5.22 → **5.17** and the modulation distance 5.76 → 5.77, so the colour was bought without paying for it anywhere else. The `mod` column of `chords_pedal` is the one number that moves about: it reads 3.62 → 3.35 here and 1.60 at a level 11.8 dB hotter, which is the modulation metric responding to a broadband bed under a sustained chord rather than to anything about a hammer.

213. **The ladder says the excitation is now the recording's, and the attack residual is closed.** `renders/timbre-ladder/ANALYSIS.md`, re-rendered and re-measured on the shipped preset, source `00` / engine `07`: spectral-envelope roughness **7.63 / 8.56 dB** at C4 (was 4.81), **5.98 / 6.33** at A2 (was 9.66) and **6.02 / 6.85** at C6 (was 11.06) — the engine's excitation scatter was 2.8 dB too smooth at C4 and 3.7 and 5.0 dB too rough at A2 and C6, and is now 0.4 to 0.9 dB from the recording at all three keys, on the metric §7 ranks second-most diagnostic. §4a's holes are gone with it: the engine's deepest partial reads **17.8 dB (k=19) against the recording's 17.7 (k=22)** at C4, **15.5 against 13.0** at A2 and **11.0 against 9.3** at C6, where before the fit `sin(k pi x)`'s exact zeros put 35.7 and 25.9 dB holes at A2 and C6 that no recording has anywhere. The attack closes at the same time: attack flatness **−44.4 / −47.8 dB** at C4 (was −57.1), **−47.4 / −47.0** at A2 (was −58.4), **−39.0 / −40.4** at C6 (was −50.9) — a 12.7, 11.1 and 11.8 dB gap reduced to 3.4, 0.4 and 1.4 — and rung `07` now reads what rung `06`, the engine with the *recording's own* residual mixed in, reads: −47.8 against −44.1 at C4. The attack *level* lands at −5.0 dB against the source's −5.1 where it stood at +0.9. What did not close is the one §8 called most diagnostic: envelope modulation is 2.58 / 4.64 dB at C4 (was 4.90), 2.38 / 0.77 at A2 (was 1.41) and 3.51 / 4.84 at C6 (was 5.08) — still too much movement at C4 and C6 and now too little at A2, and item 200's per-partial rates did not fix it.

214. **Nothing else in the preset was touched, and only the strike section was re-fitted.** `fit_partials` is not re-entrant — run again over its own output it re-measures the gains against a comb that already contains the floors it wrote, and the second pass moves `comb_floor` at 41 keys — so the run that produced this milestone's `[noise.strike]` was taken with `--out` to a scratch file and *only that section* was spliced into `presets/salamander-c5.toml`. The strike fit is independent of the base's own strike by construction (the deficit probe silences it), so the splice is the same preset the pipeline would have written from the survey base. The rest of the file is byte-identical to item 208's.

215. **Why the mel gate cannot be met inside this schema, measured four ways rather than argued.** The gate asked for −1.0 dB of mean log-mel distance and the milestone delivered −0.17. What follows is the evidence that the remaining 3.6 dB above the 1.59 dB floor is not reachable by any table this schema holds.
    * **It is not a spectral tilt.** Removing, as an oracle, each phrase's *own* per-band mean difference — a perfect 64-band EQ fitted per phrase, which no preset could deliver — takes the mean from 5.27 to **4.99 dB**. One global 64-band EQ over all six phrases makes it **worse** (5.42), and a single global gain is neutral (5.30). The signed per-band curve is real and large (−3.2 dB at 56 Hz, +6.0 at 413 Hz, −5.8 at 3.4 kHz) but it is a small part of the distance.
    * **It is not the envelope or the alignment.** An oracle per-frame broadband gain — a perfect dynamic correction — makes it worse (5.32). The best whole-phrase time shift buys 0.14 dB.
    * **It is flat in time, so it is not the attack and not the decay.** Binned by time since onset on `scale_mf`, the mean absolute difference is 6.30 dB over the first 50 ms, 5.94 from 150–400 ms, 5.85 from 0.4–1 s and 5.82 from 1–2.5 s. On `alberti_fast` it is 7.63 / 6.99 / 7.08 / 6.74. Whatever it is, it is there the whole time the note is.
    * **It is per-partial amplitude, and it survives the fit.** Measured partial by partial on isolated `staccato` notes 20–90 ms after the strike, the engine stands at 5.7 to 8.0 dB RMS from the reference *on the keys that carry a fitted `partial_gains` row*, against a reference-versus-itself floor of 1.59. Per note over the same phrase, the keys the library actually sampled score **5.17 dB** of mel and the ones it did not score **4.79** — the fitted keys are the *worse* half. Twenty decibels of measured per-partial gain is worth 0.36 dB of mel (the preset without them reads 5.58), which is the whole of what this mechanism has to give.
    * **And the union of everything left, including three regressions, still misses.** A preset carrying the new strike *and* `partial_sigma_scale` deleted (−0.29) *and* the damper returned to its pre-fit value (−0.15) *and* `partial_gains` propagated to each recording's neighbouring semitones (−0.12) reads **4.88 dB** — still 0.54 above the gate, and bought by undoing three measurements. That is the demonstration the milestone owed.

216. **Two measured errors that are larger than anything the five fields hold, and are the next milestone's, not this one's.** Both were found while looking for the missing decibel and neither has a preset field that can carry it.
    * **The velocity law is compressed by 14 dB at the bottom, and it is global where the library's is per key.** On `arpeggio_dynamics`, level against the same phrase's velocity-120 sweep: the engine spans **−26.4 dB** from velocity 20 while the recordings span **−40.6**. `hammer.velocity_min`/`velocity_max` are still `0.2`/`6.0` — the defaults; `--velocity-map` is opt-in and was never run into this preset. Running it does not fix it: `pooled_velocity_map` over 80 layers returns **0.261..3.478 m/s**, a *narrower* range than the default, at a residual of 0.674 in `ln v` (a factor of two), and the per-key fits it pools scatter from 0.100..1.723 m/s at key 33 to 0.905..7.760 at key 81. The library's velocity scaling is a per-key fact and the schema holds one global pair. Setting `velocity_min` to the 0.031 that would match the span is both unphysical for a hammer and worse on the benchmark (mel 5.17 → **6.10**), so it is not shipped and the finding is recorded instead.
    * **The per-key level balance scatters by ±5 dB.** Over `staccato`'s twenty-six notes the engine's level in the first 120 ms of each note, after the whole-phrase RMS match, runs from **+5.8 dB** at key 33 to **−10.5 dB** at key 88, standard deviation 4.1 dB and the same shape in both dynamic passes. A per-note broadband error of that size is by itself most of a 5 dB mean absolute log-mel distance. `notes.bridge_gain` is the field that would hold it and no stage fits it against the library's own per-key output level.

217. **Neutrality, proven by render, for the sixth time.** `presets/default.toml` is unchanged on disk; `render demo` md5 `94d9d0764c98fbf76a2cdd3bfa37d3a2` and the compass sweep `ad120191fd5eb16f61e1fe74a3203d33`, item 172's hashes exactly. The only file in `presets/` that moved is `salamander-c5.toml`, whose demo goes from item 208's `d5a5aa7c9a43def2b72347fb20ac0f1b` to `80111529eebf1c4e0c1967f8533328a4`. `cargo test --release --workspace` is **441 tests green**, `cargo clippy --release --workspace --all-targets` **0 diagnostics**. The performance budget is untouched because the engine is: the four acceptance worst cases read **45.5 %**, **47.1 %** (the measured preset's duplex), **46.2 %** (a thousand events with the mechanism and the hammer's noise both voiced, +0.43 points over the same material with the action silenced) and **46.4 %** of one core against the 50 % goal.

218. **The felt limiter clicked at four block boundaries on every note-off, and the fix is that the damper's landing is a curve rather than a staircase.** Reported as a review finding on `voice.rs`, reproduced, and larger than it was reported: the mechanism is not the seat alone. `felt_threshold_for` read `damper_current` — which advances **once per block** — and held the result for all 128 samples, while the threshold is *exponential* in that position (`FELT_CLEARANCE.powf`). At the nominal release the position moves 0.267 per block, so the threshold stepped **13.3 dB at each block boundary of the ramp**, and a fourth and larger step landed where `felt_is_limiting`'s `damper_current < 1.0` switched the limiter off at the seat and the output snapped from `soft_limit(x, 0.01 · reference)` back to `x` between two adjacent samples. Measured on a single note-off, worst single-sample step in the second after the release, against the RMS single-sample step of the block either side of it: **12.4–14.4×** at keys 33/45/60/72 at velocities 12 and 90, at −17 to −23 dB under the note's own peak, on **`presets/default.toml` as well as the measured preset** — this was never a `partial_gains` phenomenon, and the milestone's +20 dB rows only made an existing defect louder. In the six shipped benchmark phrases: **252 isolated block-boundary steps** (staccato 30, alberti_fast 72, scale_mf 42, arpeggio_dynamics 78, chords_pedal 8, excerpt 22), worst −12.6 to −15.4 dB under the phrase peak, against **zero** in all six reference renders. Nothing in the model is synchronised to the block grid, so a step that stands out from its own neighbourhood *at* a boundary is an artefact of the block loop by construction, and that is the assertion `a_release_does_not_click_at_the_block_boundary` makes (five times the local slope, on both velocities of four keys, action silenced so a key-off thump is not measured as a step). It fails on the old code at 12.5× and passes at ≤ 3.4×. After the fix the six phrases carry **one** boundary step, at −90 dB under the phrase peak.

219. **What replaced it: a per-sample geometric walk, and an interaction that retires instead of switching off.** `Voice::felt_ramp` is called once per rendered block, after the damper has moved, and returns the threshold at the block's first sample, the factor that walks it to the last, and the lower of the two ends. Three decisions in it, two of them forced by measurement rather than by taste:
    * **The walk is geometric, at one `powf` per block and one multiply per sample.** The position enters the threshold through `powf`, so a geometric walk in the threshold is a *linear* walk in the damper's position — the ramp the engine already believes in, now sampled at 44.1 kHz instead of at 344 Hz. The `lowest` field is the cheap early-out the old code had as `peak <= threshold`: a block whose peak is under both ends cannot reach the felt anywhere, which is how `PHYSICS.md` §6's third interval (free vibration again) still falls out rather than being scheduled.
    * **Holding the limit at a seated damper was tried first, and it is worse than the click.** It is the physically tidy statement — the string cannot swing past a felt resting on it — and it failed two acceptance tests by doing exactly what it says: an absolute limit anchored to the pre-release level clamps the whole tail to 2 % of it, and the release stopped answering the release *velocity* at all (**−80.8 / −81.0 / −81.6 dB** at MIDI 1 / 64 / 127, where `a_slow_release_rings_on_where_a_fast_one_stops` wants 2 dB between the first two), while `a_half_pedalled_note_is_not_merely_a_faster_decay` lost its control (1.5 dB of brightness against the 3 dB it asserts, because the *linear* control's fully-seated damper had now acquired a felt too). What ends at the seat is the **interaction**, not the contact: engagement walks back to zero at `damper_step` per block, so the note is free again after the damper has been down for as long as it took to land, and every sample in between is on a continuous curve. Both tests pass, and the shipped preset's numbers barely move — release velocity **−78.7/−82.2/−82.8 → −79.0/−81.8/−82.7 dB**, half-pedal brightness **5.6 → 5.1 dB** against a 3.0 dB gate.
    * **The direction test stays, and the one discontinuity it leaves is documented rather than removed.** A damper *leaving* the string is still not limiting at all (item 116: the engine lifts the damper and throws the hammer on the same instant, and limiting through that spends the whole attack transient clamped against the previous note's level — 27–68 dB of choke, and `a_restrike_attacks_like_a_strike_from_silence` asserts the 0.5 dB it must not exceed). A gesture that lifts a damper *during its own descent* — a re-strike or a pedal within the ~12 ms of the ramp, with the limiter still biting — therefore releases the limit in one step. Ramping that release instead was rejected because it re-introduces exactly the choke item 116 measured; it is the one place on this path where a discontinuity is the lesser fault, and it is reachable only inside a 12 ms window on a key that is being released and re-taken at once.

220. **What the fix did to the scoreboard, which is more than the milestone that preceded it.** `renders/realism/REALISM.md`, re-run on the same preset with only the engine changed (item 209's reading → this one): mean log-mel **5.17 → 4.91 dB** against the 1.59 floor, and **no phrase rose** — `scale_mf` 5.06 → 4.71, `arpeggio_dynamics` 5.50 → 5.29, `chords_pedal` 4.68 → 4.68, `staccato` 4.51 → 4.42, `alberti_fast` 6.48 → **5.69**, `excerpt` 4.76 → 4.67. The **modulation distance moved 5.77 → 3.84 dB** (floor 0.96), which is the largest single move any change has made to that column and is the metric `ANALYSIS.md` §8 ranks most diagnostic: a click on the note grid is broadband amplitude modulation at the note rate, and it was being measured as one. The worst modulation band is no longer `scale_mf` at 8.9 kHz / 2.8 Hz with 22.24 dB but `alberti_fast` at 7.3 kHz / 0.6 Hz with 8.95. Onset-to-peak **13.5 → 14.0 ms** against the recordings' 18.5. The one column that went the other way is signed attack tonality, **+0.54 → +1.48 dB** (mean absolute 4.77 → **4.70**, i.e. better): the steps were broadband energy inside the first blocks after an event and their removal takes noise out of the attack window too. `renders/timbre-ladder` is **bit-identical**, every rung of all three keys, because the ladder holds its notes and never releases one — which is the cleanest available statement that the change reaches the damper and nothing else.

221. **Neutrality, and the one contract this deliberately breaks.** `presets/default.toml` is byte-identical on disk and still character for character what the engine writes; nothing in `presets/` moved at all. `engine/tests/preset.rs`'s sounding-path fingerprint — ten strikes across the compass with no release and no pedal move, taken from the engine at `16307c4` — is **unchanged at `63686423443ec4d3`**, so no change reached the hammer, the string, the unison, the resonance bus, the soundboard or the master chain. What did change is the audio of every render that contains a note-off, and that includes the two reference renders: `render demo` **`94d9d0764c98fbf76a2cdd3bfa37d3a2` → `76edc431ea498e8e88bfa054376e527b`** and the compass sweep **`ad120191fd5eb16f61e1fe74a3203d33` → `84ffcc303af6091faa063e6d306d9c7e`**, item 172's hashes for the first time since they were taken; the salamander demo goes `80111529eebf1c4e0c1967f8533328a4` → `978a6d0f307c943764cc16854eb909df`. This is stated rather than avoided. Item 103's rule is that *a preset without a new field renders what it always rendered* — a schema contract, and it is intact, because no field was added. A bug fix on the audio path is the other kind of change, the kind `engine/tests/preset.rs`'s own header already anticipates ("a damper on its way down soft-limits the string it is landing on … both are *wanted*, and both are audible"), and a hash that could never move would mean an engine that could never be corrected. The difference is **22.9 dB below the demo's own RMS** (25.4 on the sweep, 24.9 on the salamander demo) and it is confined to the release intervals. `cargo test --release --workspace` is **442 tests green** (441 plus the new gate); `cargo clippy --release --workspace --all-targets` **0 diagnostics**. The performance budget is unmoved: **45.6 %**, **47.1 %**, **46.3 %**, **46.4 %** of one core against item 217's 45.5 / 47.1 / 46.2 / 46.4 — one multiply per sample, on the handful of voices whose dampers are moving.

222. **`fit_partials` now refuses the invocation its own header used to demonstrate.** Item 214 documented that the tool is not re-entrant and then left the hazard entirely in prose — in a file whose usage example was `--preset presets/salamander-c5.toml … --out presets/salamander-c5.toml`, the destructive form. Re-verified by running it: over the shipped preset, `comb_line` renders a comb whose nulls the preset's own `partial_gains` have already filled, the engine's line goes flat (key 45: **−31.0 / −11.5 / −11.5 / −11.9 dB** across the four probe floors) and `floor_for` **saturates to 0.450** where a run from the survey base reproduces the shipped 0.200 and 0.0 exactly. Three changes, no measurement moved: the header now writes to `/tmp` and states the non-re-entrancy with the numbers above; `refuse_self_overwrite` **fails the run** when `--out` canonicalises to the file `--preset` was read from, because there is no run for which that is right — the *next* run would have no base left to measure against; and `warn_if_already_fitted` prints, before anything is measured, which of this tool's own answers the base already carries (on the shipped preset: `notes.partial_gains` (24), `notes.partial_sigma_scale` (24), `notes.comb_floor` (41), `[noise.strike]` (6)). The warning covers the case the refusal cannot see — copying the fitted preset to a new name first — and neither guard changes what a legitimate run from the survey base produces.

223. **The unison is now the `2N` coupled eigenmodes of one bridge point, solved at construction, and it is cheaper than what it replaces.** `FUNDAMENTALS.md` §5's construction, built in `engine/src/string.rs`. Per partial `k` of a key with `N` strings: `a' = A_k a`, `A_k = i Omega_k − sigma_int I − C_k`, with `Omega_k = diag(omega_k detune_j)` and `C_k` block diagonal in the two polarizations, each block `c_p` times an all-ones matrix. That block is **rank one**, so the characteristic equation factorises into two degree-`N` complex polynomials — [`block_solve`] forms `prod_j (lambda − d_j) + c prod'`, which is `c` times the derivative of the first term, and takes its roots by Durand–Kerner after shifting to the block's centre so they are `O(detune spread + N|c|)` instead of `O(omega)`. `D − cJ` is complex **symmetric**, so the row of `V^-1` the strike projection needs is `v_m / (v_m . v_m)` and not `v_m^H`: one division per mode, no LAPACK, no matrix inversion, and 260 lines of `f64` arithmetic that never runs on the audio thread. The `2N` eigenmodes go straight into two `ModalBank`s — the vertical block's into one, the horizontal block's into the other, which is what keeps `voicing.polarization_pan_spread` working — at **exactly the mode count the free-running construction used** (C4: 3 strings x 55 partials x 2 = 330 modes, both ways). What they need that free modes do not is a **complex input gain**: the strike projects onto a non-orthogonal eigenbasis, so every mode starts at its own phase, and `ModalBank::push_mode_complex` and one more FMA in `Chunk::step` are the whole of the runtime change. `PianoString` loses its `N` excitation buffers for one, because the per-string shares live in the modes' gains now; `couple()`, `Polarizations::previous`, `group_previous` and `MAX_UNISON_COUPLING` are deleted with the feedback loop they belonged to. Construction costs **68–82 ms for all 88 keys** on the two shipped presets, which is preset-load work: nothing in `A_k` depends on velocity, so a note-on costs exactly what it did.

224. **What the construction produces, measured on the default preset.** C4's fundamental, six modes sorted by radiated amplitude: `df` −0.123 / +0.128 / −0.132 / +0.309 / +0.138 / +0.317 Hz against nominal, `sigma` 0.798 / 0.612 / 0.469 / 0.455 / 0.419 / 0.381 s⁻¹, levels 0.0 / −5.3 / −12.8 / −14.9 / −18.0 / −25.0 dB. Three things in that table are the milestone: **the decay rates are split by a factor of 2.1** where the free-running construction's three strings were identical to the last bit; **the loudest mode is the fastest**, which is Weinreich's prompt sound and aftersound arriving out of one coupling constant instead of out of a hand-set `horizontal_gain_db`; and **the frequency spread has contracted** from the tuning's 0.74 Hz to 0.43 Hz, which is Woodhouse's anti-veering. The polarization split — mode `j` of the vertical block against mode `j` of the horizontal — is **0.0072 to 0.0109 Hz** at C4 k=1 against the shipped field's flat 0.35, and it grows with the partial (0.038 Hz by k=8) because it is `N gamma_v beta eps / 2 pi` and follows the loss it comes out of.

225. **The three fields the construction obsoletes stay in the schema, inert, and say so on load.** `voicing.unison_coupling`, `voicing.horizontal_offset_hz` and `voicing.unison_sigma_scale` are still parsed and still validated; none is read. `FUNDAMENTALS.md` §5.4 says to delete them, and deleting them from the *engine's* struct would break every preset already written and force `tuner/src/preset.rs` — a deliberately independent copy of the schema, item 57 — to move in the same commit, for no gain: what makes them harmless is that nothing reads them, not that nothing parses them. `Preset::inert_fields` names the ones a file actually sets and `Preset::load` prints them, **on the file path only** and not in `from_toml`, because a probe preset the tuner builds in memory is not a user asking for a field and a warning printed once per render is not a warning. What each of them used to assert is now derived: the coupling **is** `radiated_share × sigma_k`, the same coefficient as the radiation damping (`FUNDAMENTALS.md` §1.1, Capleton Eq. 2); the polarization split is the bridge's reactive anisotropy times the partial's own frequency; and the per-string decay split is what the eigenproblem produces on its own. `MAX_UNISON_COUPLING` leaves `engine::string` — it was a loop-gain ceiling and there is no loop — and survives as `preset::LEGACY_MAX_UNISON_COUPLING` at the same 0.05 so that every file already written still validates. Two constants replace the three fields and are **not** preset fields: `REACTIVE_RATIO = 1.0` (Weinreich/Capleton §III.B, order one; the prototype re-solved and re-rendered everything at 0, 0.25, 1 and 3) and `REACTIVE_ANISOTROPY = 0.075` (Capleton's 1 : 0.925 example). §7.6 asked for a field; nothing in the tuner can fit either of them until the strike vector gets a velocity dependence (§7.5 step 3), and a schema field nobody can fit is a knob, not a measurement.

226. **`horizontal_decay_ratio` is inverted, not re-read — and that inversion is worth 8 dB of treble attack.** §5.4 says to re-read the field as the bridge's resistive anisotropy `Re Y_h / Re Y_v` directly, "same number, now a property of the bridge instead of a decay law". A decibel too far, because the horizontal plane **still radiates**. For one string, whose two polarizations are the whole eigenproblem, `sigma_v = sigma_int + gamma_v = sigma` and `sigma_h = sigma_int + rho gamma_v = rho(2 − rho) sigma`, so the *decay* ratio the field was fitted to is `rho(2 − rho)` and not `rho`: reading it as `rho` makes every note's aftersound decay `(2 − rho)` times too fast — 0.496 against the fitted 0.29 on `presets/default.toml`. Inverting instead, `rho = 1 − sqrt(1 − horizontal_decay_ratio)` (0.157 for the default voicing, 0.090 for the measured one, against the prototype's 0.828 share) puts the single-string aftersound back exactly on the fitted rate, and it is what takes the rendered note's peak and attack level from **+0.4 / +1.1 dB** of the free-running construction to **+0.4 / +0.5 dB** across the compass. `radiated_share = 1 − rho` follows, which is `FUNDAMENTALS.md` §2.6's contradiction with the shipped `voicing.bridge.radiated_share = 0.5` resolved in favour of the field that was fitted to recordings; `bridge.radiated_share` keeps its own narrower job, the *fluctuation* of the board's mobility on each partial's decay.

227. **The timing skew cannot be a phase, and the migration sketch is wrong about it: measured, −8.4 dB at C6 and −9.5 dB at C7 of attack peak.** §5.1 expected the per-string delay `d_j = j MAX_SKEW_SAMPLES / N` to survive as `e^{−i omega_k d_j}` in the strike vector — "which is why it collapses into one complex scalar per mode instead of needing `N` input buffers". A delay is a phase rotation *for a steady sinusoid*. The hammer's force is a pulse about a millisecond long and the delay spread is 0.31 ms, so for the length of the blow the three copies overlap almost completely and add coherently, while the phase form applies the partial's steady-state cancellation from `t = 0`; at C7 the spread is 0.65 of a period, so the phase form cancels the strike itself. Exactly: the modal amplitude is `sum_j c_m^(j) F_m(t − d_j)`, and only for `t >> d_j`, where `F_m(t) -> A_m e^{lambda_m t}`, does that factor into one scalar. Measured on the rendered string against the free-running construction, key by key: with the skew as a phase, peak −0.4 / −8.4 / −9.5 / −1.2 dB at C4 / C6 / C7 / C8 with the **sustained partial magnitudes unchanged to a few decibels** — the error is entirely inside the first millisecond. With the skew dropped, peak +0.5 / +0.4 / +0.8 / +3.0 dB. So it is dropped, not approximated: what it stood for — the hammer is not square to the strings — is the same fact `unison_layout.share` states, and under this construction the shares are what break the group's symmetry and set how loudly the antisymmetric modes radiate, which is a far larger effect than 0.31 ms of arrival spread.

228. **The T60 normalisation is a bisection of a staircase, because the thing it has to land on jumps by a beat period.** `Voicing::vertical_decay_factor` is deleted: it is a closed form for a two-exponential sum and exact for that, and there is no closed form for `2N` coupled modes. `decay_scale` solves for the one factor on `sigma_int` and `gamma_v` that puts the composite's −60 dB crossing on the fitted anchor `6.91 / sigma_k`, and the statistic it solves against is the **coherent** modulus `|sum_m G_m e^{lambda_m t}|` — the signal, beats and all, with the *last* crossing taken because a double-decay envelope with a beat on it can dip below and come back. That statistic is not continuous in the mode set: move the damping infinitesimally and a trough that was just above the threshold drops just below it, and the crossing jumps by a whole beat period. A Newton step on it lands anywhere inside a tread — **measured at up to 48 % of the anchor** before the solve was made a bisection. So it is two stages: the **beat-free** envelope `sum_m |G_m| e^{−sigma_m t}` first, which is strictly decreasing, needs no grid at all, is the closed form it replaces term for term when there is one string, and lands within about 20 % (the two statistics differ by however much the tail's modes cancel); then eleven bisections of the real one inside a factor of 2.5 either side. Stage 2 is skipped for a single string — two modes with real positive gains a few thousandths of a hertz apart, where the beat-free envelope *is* the coherent one — and for partials more than 50 dB under the note's loudest, of which a bass note has sixty out of eighty. The grid is 192 points over three anchors against the prototype's 4000 over ten, because the crossing is interpolated in log amplitude between its two grid points: what the grid sets is the cost and not the answer, and the cost is real — `render_to_buffer` builds the engine inside the window the performance acceptance tests measure, so the prototype's 1.3 s of preset load is ten points of the budget. Result over all 88 keys of `presets/default.toml`, partials 1–4 with an anchor over half a second, 302 cells: **median 0.0 %, p90 8.0 %, worst 22.9 %** against the anchor, the residual being the beat jump.

229. **Neutrality, and the contract that replaced bit-exactness.** `presets/default.toml` is byte-identical on disk and still character for character what the engine writes; nothing in `presets/` moved at all. The sounding-path fingerprint of `engine/tests/preset.rs` — ten strikes across the compass, no release, no pedal — moves for the first time since it was taken at `16307c4`: **`63686423443ec4d3` → `61e29d4abaa316f9`**. That is the point rather than a regression: the poles are the eigenvalues of a matrix where they used to be the diagonal of it, and no construction change can hold a hash. `render demo` goes `76edc431ea498e8e88bfa054376e527b` → `b018cad36f011833c4de7a64888c4360`, the compass sweep `84ffcc303af6091faa063e6d306d9c7e` → `a8dcb61a0fa697d026e9a3f888782573`, the measured preset's demo `978a6d0f307c943764cc16854eb909df` → `97da50f66b517a75db14e4011146fe23`; the differences are **−7.7, −8.8 and −12.1 dB** under each render's own RMS, against item 221's −22.9 for the click fix. What is held instead, all on `presets/default.toml` and all pinned by tests: **pitch** — every partial's radiated centre within **0.166 cents** of the frequency the preset's own formula asks for, over every partial of every key, where the free-running construction read +0.87 to +1.72 cents *sharp* on the same rendered cells because `horizontal_offset_hz` added a fixed number of hertz to every horizontal mode (22 cents of it at A0); **decay** — item 228's table, and the `fundamental_t60_matches_the_spec_anchors` claim the old closed form made, now made by the solve; **level** — the strike's peak within **0.5 dB** from A0 to C6 (+1.0 at C7, +3.0 at C8) and the first 0.1 s within **0.6 dB** to C6. What is *not* held, and is the documented break: the **sustain**. The 0.2–2.0 s RMS moves **+2.3 dB at A0 and A1, +1.7 at A2, and −1.0 at C6**, because the prompt decay is now derived from the bridge instead of asserted — the free-running construction had to make its vertical bank decay 2.6× the whole-note rate to reach the anchor with two exponentials and a hand-set 12 dB offset, and the coupled one reaches the same anchor with a prompt at 1.4× and an aftersound underneath it. The note is the same length and holds more of itself in the middle.

230. **The metronome is gone, the double decay is not, and the modal loop got 36 % faster on the way.** *The metronome*: over every partial of every key of `presets/default.toml` — **4048 cells**, every pairwise beat rate of every partial's `2N` modes — each of the three shipped constants now appears in **0.22 % (0.270 Hz), 0.30 % (0.350) and 0.15 % (0.520)** of cells where it appeared in **all** of them, and the most-shared rate anywhere in the audible band is shared by **0.69 %** of cells to within half a millihertz (`string::tests::no_beat_rate_is_shared_across_the_compass`). On the render, over C4 / A2 / C6 × k=1..4, no rate is the dominant modulation line of more than a third of the cells and the fastest cell moves at more than twice the rate of the slowest (`tests/partials.rs`); the literal three numbers are deliberately *not* tested on the render, because on the default preset the unison's own detune beats live at 0.4–1.1 Hz and land within a few hundredths of 0.520 by coincidence. *The double decay*: at C4 and A2, every tracked partial's tail is the slower of its two straight lines — prompt/tail ratios 7.1 / 4.7 / 3.7 / 1.7 at C4 and 7.6 / 1.1 / 6.4 / 5.8 at A2 — and C6's fundamental keeps one too (−27.2 dB/s prompt against −15.2 tail), which is the cell `FUNDAMENTALS.md` §7.3 measured the prototype breaking on and the errata softens with the corrected `mu ≈ 1.05`. *The cost*: the worst cases read **28.5 %, 29.8 %, 29.4 % and 29.1 %** of one core against item 221's 45.6 / 47.1 / 46.3 / 46.4 and a 50 % goal. Three things paid for the extra FMA and then some, and two of them are independent of this milestone. `Chunk::step` was writing each lane's new state to its array and reading it straight back to accumulate; giving it a local costs nothing and was worth **5.7 points** with six arrays live where it had been free with five. `LANES` goes 4 → 8: one horizontal reduction per eight modes instead of per four, and 16 points. And `process_add` gained a `step_free` for the case that decides the budget — a piano is mostly *ringing*, not being driven, and a mode with no input is four multiplies and three adds against the driven six and five. One threshold moved with the construction: `CULL_AMPLITUDE` is divided by `2 × MAX_UNISON`, because one partial is now `2N` eigenmodes that **add coherently at the output**, so `2N` modes each a hair under a per-mode threshold are together `2N` times over it — measured on `a_prepared_string_rings_on_after_the_note_that_excited_it`, where a sympathetically prepared C3 holding 3.2e-11 of energy was zeroed outright while the free-running string holding *less* (1.8e-11) survived because all of it was in one mode.

231. **`partial_gains` was fitted to half the excitation, and the half it threw away is 6.5 dB at C4's fundamental.** Reported from listening, before it was measured: the C4 jitter A/B pair reads as two different pitches, because at velocity 90 the recording's strongest partial is **k = 2** and the engine's is **k = 1** — a note whose fundamental leads where the recording's second partial does is a note the ear places an octave differently at the attack. `estimate::shaping::partial_gains` fits a polynomial in `ln k` to each layer's spectrum and writes only what is left over, on the reasoning that everything smooth in `ln k` is the hammer, the bridge and the microphone and that the engine has models for all three. Half right: the envelope really does contain the part of the blow that changes with velocity, which a velocity-independent table must not carry, but it *also* contains the engine's own error in that envelope, and that does not change with velocity and nothing else in the schema can carry it. It has been unfitted since the felt fits landed in Phase E, and the roughness gains are innocent of it by construction — their geometric mean is 1 and their reference is a polynomial that has already absorbed the tilt. `estimate::shaping::envelope_tilt` fits the same polynomial **twice** — once to the recording and once to a render of the engine at the same key and the same velocity, measured by the same tracker — and writes the difference into the same table, offset so its geometric mean is 1 so that the level does not move. This is `estimate::directivity`'s pattern and `CombLine`'s and `strike_offset`'s (items 137, 199, 211): a quantity that is only meaningful as "how far is the engine from the recording" is inverted *on the engine*, so the tracker's own bias, the window and the microphone's comb divide out instead of being modelled. Measured at C4 against the shipped preset, with its own floors and roughness gains already in the probe: **−6.53 / −2.55 / −0.91 / −0.05 dB** on partials 1–4, a **7.5 dB span**. `presets/salamander-c5.toml` is **not** re-fitted here and is byte-identical: item 222 established that this tool is not re-entrant, and the only legitimate run is from the survey base, which is a full pipeline pass and a milestone of its own.

232. **Eight of the tuner's calibration round-trips now fail, they fail for one reason, and that reason is `FUNDAMENTALS.md` §7.7 arriving.** `cargo test --release --workspace` is **green everywhere except `tuner/tests/calibration.rs`**, where eight of nineteen gates are red. They are *estimator* gates: each writes a known parameter into a preset, renders the engine, and asks the estimator to recover it. Every one of them was calibrated against the free-running forward model, and the forward model has changed. Named with their measured numbers, because "re-derive the estimators" is a milestone and this is its worklist:
    - `a_known_per_string_decay_spread_comes_back_from_a_beating_unison` — **not a tolerance question at all**. It injects `voicing.unison_sigma_scale` and measures the pitch drift it causes; the field is inert (item 225), so nothing is injected. The property it was written for is now an *output* — the bridge splits the group's decay rates by a factor of 2.1 at C4 without being asked (item 224) — so the round-trip has no knob and has to become a statement about the construction instead.
    - `a_beating_unisons_decays_come_back_within_what_the_beating_allows` — worst per-partial T60 error **19.1 % at C2 (2 strings), 26.9 % at C4, 52.6 % at C6**, mean +3.1 / +18.6 / +33.6 %. `estimate::decay` fits **two** exponentials, which was the forward model exactly; the composite is now `2N` of them with a derived split, and the two-exponential fit reads the difference as a longer T60.
    - `a_known_fourth_order_inharmonicity_comes_back_within_a_tenth` — B4 comes back **0.0 against 3.0e-8** at C2. The partial tracker resolves a coupled group's split lines where it used to see one line per string, and the two-band `B` comparison the fourth-order term is inverted from moves with it.
    - `a_known_contact_width_comes_back_and_takes_the_felt_with_it` — the *control* returns **w = 0.018** where it must return nothing, and the fitted width **+14.9 %**.
    - `the_felt_and_the_velocity_map_come_back_from_a_velocity_ladder` — **p 2.09 against 2.61**, K −98.9 %.
    - `a_known_per_key_spread_comes_back_key_by_key` — **0.302 against 0.2** at C5. The polarization handover moved later for the reason item 229 gives, so the drift the spread is inverted from is measured at the wrong moment.
    - `the_halo_level_follows_the_coupling_the_fit_inverts_it_on` — doubling `resonance_coupling` moves the halo **+2.7 dB**.
    - `a_known_duplex_comes_back_from_the_engines_own_render_of_it` — nothing comes back.

    The common thread in six of the eight is one function: `estimate::decay` extrapolates each partial's amplitude back to `t = 0` through a two-exponential fit, and every estimator that reads a *spectrum* reads it through that extrapolation. The construction that replaced the two exponentials is the milestone; re-deriving what measures it is the next one, and §7.7 already lists which fields it touches. Nothing here says the engine is wrong — the engine's own gates, including the twenty-four acceptance tests and the per-partial forensics of `engine/tests/partials.rs`, are green — it says the tuner is still measuring the piano it used to render.

233. **The false beat is a term on the diagonal of `Omega_k`, and it is the only mechanism the recording's companions fit.** `notes.false_beat` is a ragged per-key table of `{ k, hz, db }` — at most eight per key, `hz` in 0.2..=3.0, `db` in -20..=0, one entry per partial, absent means none. Each entry splits the two transverse planes of **one wire** at **one partial**: the group's first string (`SPLIT_STRING`, the one the una-corda hammer always reaches) gets `+2 pi hz` added to its horizontal-block diagonal *before* `block_solve` runs, so the companion comes back as one of the group's own `2N` eigenvalues with the decay rate the coupled system gives it. The mode count does not move — the split takes the horizontal mode that was already there, 27.6 dB down and a hundredth of a hertz away, and puts it where the measurement says it is. `FUNDAMENTALS.md` §7.4 is why it is this and not one of the three mechanisms the model already had: the recording's C4 and A2 companions sit at **1.11 / 1.48 / 0.74 / 0.74 Hz on k = 1..4**, flat in `k` and 7–20x wider than the fitted unison, where a mistuning is a frequency *ratio* and must scale with `k`, the bridge's polarization split is a hundred times narrower and also scales with `k`, and `horizontal_offset_hz` was 22 dB out in level and the same number on every partial of every key. What is left is Capleton's own subject (JASA 115(2), 2004) — the wire's own geometry, which is why the offset is in **hertz** here where hertz was wrong there, and which is the falsifiable half: fitted per string and per partial, `delta` must come back *uncorrelated* across `k` or it is not a false beat. The statistic that proves the offset went in on the diagonal rather than onto the answer is the **trace**: eigenvalues sum to it and `-c J_N` contributes the same to both blocks, so `sum(horizontal f) - sum(vertical f)` moves by exactly the number in the table (measured 1.000 +- 0.05 Hz for a 1.0 Hz split, `string::tests::a_false_beat_splits_the_partial_it_names_and_nothing_beside_it`) however far the solve moves the individual modes — and it moves all of them, which is the second assertion. Rendered on a key whose unison has been narrowed so the two mechanisms can be told apart, the named partial goes from **0.10 dB of beat depth to 13.00** and its dominant modulation line from 0.580 Hz to **1.038** for a 1.0 Hz split (0.5 -> 0.641, 2.5 -> 2.533), while every other partial of the key holds its depth to 0.05 dB, its rate exactly and its centre to a millihertz.

234. **The false beat's *level* has to be solved for, because a force on one string reaches every mode of its block.** The obvious implementation — drive the split string's horizontal plane by `10^(db/20)` and call the result the companion — is wrong twice over: the eigenvectors `v_jm = 1/(d_j - lambda_m)` are only as localised as the detuning is wide against `|c|`, and in the horizontal plane it is not. Measured on the default preset at C4's second partial, a unit drive on one string's horizontal plane comes back on the three modes of that block in the ratio **0.45 : 0.27 : 0.29**, and a first attempt that picked "the companion" by eigenvector concentration (`|v_j| / ||v||` largest) targeted the mode with the *smallest* response and left a different one 6 dB above it. So what is normalised is the whole block's **coherent contribution at the strike**, `|sum_m G_m|` — the amplitude that beats against the mode carrying the note, which is what a measured beat depth inverts to and therefore the only thing a fit could ever write — against the loudest mode of the *vertical* block, which the split does not touch. Because `project` is linear in `u` that sum is affine in the extra drive, `S(a) = A + a B`, so the drive is one quadratic `|B|^2 a^2 + 2 Re(A conj B) a + (|A|^2 - target^2) = 0` and not a search; the bounds on `db` put `target` above `|A|`, so the constant term is negative and exactly one root is positive. Measured: the asked level lands within **0.1 dB** at every rate and level tested. The false beat's drive is added as a **second term beside the leak** rather than as a factor inside it — the whole point of the mechanism is that the split plane is driven 20 dB harder than the leak drives the other two — and that is also what keeps a preset with no splits arithmetically identical, term for term and rounding for rounding, to the construction before this one. Two consequences are recorded rather than hidden: the T60 normalisation is re-solved **with the split in it** (`decay_scale` takes it, and the grid stage stops being skipped for single strings that have one, because a false beat is a beat by construction and the beat-free envelope is not the signal), and the rendered beat depth over 0.3–3.0 s is *not* the asked level — the split plane decays more slowly than the mode it beats against, so the amplitude ratio sweeps through 1 somewhere inside any long window whatever it started at. That is §2.4's guaranteed null arriving as the mechanism working: it is what makes the recording's wobble ride the *loud* part of the partial (`wRMS/raw` 0.55) instead of spiking at a null.

235. **Velocity enters a linear string model in exactly one place, and it is the direction of the strike vector.** `FUNDAMENTALS.md` §7.3's second refutation is structural and not a tolerance: `u = s_j g_k` scales uniformly with velocity, so `c = V^-1 u` scales uniformly and every ratio in the mixture is a constant — the eigenmodes are velocity-invariant by eigenvalue and the mixture is velocity-invariant by linearity. Measured on the render, that is exact: over velocities 40 / 90 / 120 at C4 the default preset's per-partial beat depth moves by **0.002 to 0.016 dB**, all of it the hammer's nonlinear felt and none of it the mixture, against the recording's **1.90 dB**. `[voicing.strike_direction]` is the handle §7.5 step 3 asks for: `vh_db_at_pp` and `vh_db_at_ff` (offsets on `horizontal_gain_db` at MIDI 1 and 127, +-12 dB, linear in `t = (vel-1)/126`) and `share_tilt` (the full pp-to-ff swing of the group's share asymmetry, +-0.2, applied **about the mean and about mid-velocity**: `s_j(v) = 1 + (1 + share_tilt (2t - 1))(s_j - 1)`). Both are ratios *inside* `u`: the share row still averages exactly 1 at every velocity, so no loudness law is smuggled in, and `unison_layout.share` stays the shares of a mezzo blow so that a fit writing this field does not move the note it was fitted on. All three zero — and the section absent — is the fitted strike vector at every velocity, **bit for bit**. Measured with a moderate direction (-6 to +6 dB, 0.15 of tilt) at C4's fundamental: the horizontal/vertical amplitude ratio runs **0.105 / 0.160 / 0.209 / 0.277 / 0.416** at velocities 1 / 40 / 64 / 90 / 127, strictly monotone, with not one pole moved; the composite's beat depth moves 0.93 dB with it. On the render at -8/+8 dB and 0.2 of tilt the per-partial beat-depth spread over the forensics' own three velocity layers is **3.6 / 4.6 / 2.3 / 0.7 dB** on k = 1..4 against the recording's 1.90 and the velocity-independent construction's 0.000. Column `B2` is the first of the four `FUNDAMENTALS.md` §7.7 gates that anything in this repository can now reach.

236. **The velocity dependence costs one pass over the key's modes at note-on, and nothing at all to a preset that does not use it.** `project` is linear in `u` and the share tilt enters `u` linearly, so the mixture at any velocity is `gain + tau gain_tilt` **exactly** — the derivative is cached beside the value at preset load and a note-on is two fused multiply-adds per mode plus one multiply for the v/h scale, on the same path `set_una_corda` has always been on: bounded, allocation-free, no solve. `PianoString::set_strike(una_corda, vel)` replaces `set_una_corda` at the note-on site and does both halves of the direction in one pass; `set_una_corda` remains, as `set_strike` at the current velocity. The tilt tables are built **only** when the preset has a `[strike_direction]` — without one they are empty, the pass is the table swap it was, and the extra memory is zero. A string that nothing ever strikes (the sympathetic bus drives it, the hammer does not) holds the nominal velocity, which is the mixture the T60 anchors were normalised at.

237. **`notes.partial_gains` is the full measured ratio now, and the range is a factor of twenty.** The semantics change is item 231's, stated in the schema: the number is `a_k(0) measured / a_k(0) as the engine's own excitation model predicts it`, at one key and one velocity through one tracker — not the roughness residual left after a smooth polynomial in `ln k` has been divided out. The old reading threw away the engine's error in the spectral envelope along with the part of the blow that changes with velocity, and only the second of those belongs anywhere else; the first is worth **7.5 dB of tilt over C4's first four partials** and is why the engine leads with k = 1 where the recording at velocity 90 leads with k = 2. Engine-side that is documentation plus one widening: `MIN_PARTIAL_GAIN` 0.1 -> **0.05** and `MAX_PARTIAL_GAIN` 10 -> **20**, because the table now carries the envelope error *and* the roughness where +-20 dB covered only the roughness (`TUNING_REPORT.md` §3's 12–29 dB worst partials, `ANALYSIS.md` §4a's 9.3–17.7 dB deepest). A factor of twenty is the widest a table can be and still be one hammer striking one string: past it the fit has stopped correcting the excitation model and started replacing it. `tuner/src/preset.rs` moves with it, as it must to accept what the engine writes.

238. **Neutrality this time is bit-exactness, not a contract.** Both mechanisms are absent from both shipped presets and neutral when absent, so unlike item 229 there is nothing to negotiate: `presets/` is byte-identical, the sounding-path fingerprint of `engine/tests/preset.rs` stays **`61e29d4abaa316f9`**, and the demo, sweep and salamander md5s do not move. It is checked rather than asserted — `a_neutral_strike_direction_is_the_fitted_one_at_every_velocity` builds the same key with and without a zeroed `[strike_direction]` and compares every mode of every partial at four velocities under both pedals with `assert_eq!` on `f32`, and the false-beat drive is added beside the leak rather than folded into it precisely so that the no-split expression is unchanged rounding for rounding (folding it in cost the fingerprint on the first attempt, which is how the requirement was found). `cargo test --release --workspace` is green everywhere except the eight `tuner/tests/calibration.rs` gates item 232 names, which fail with the **same measured numbers** they did before this milestone (B4 0.0 against 3.0e-8, the contact-width control's w = 0.018, p 2.09 against 2.61, the per-key spread 0.302 against 0.2): this milestone added no red and moved none. Clippy: 0 diagnostics.

239. **The false beat is inverted from the recordings per key and per partial, and the falsification is a model comparison rather than a correlation threshold.** `estimate::motion::fit_false_beat` takes `FUNDAMENTALS.md` §7.4's own two statistics — the companion's **level** from the beat depth (`D = 20 log10((1+r)/(1-r))`, inverted for `r`) and its **rate** from the dominant line of the log envelope's modulation spectrum — and writes them as `{k, hz, db}`. On the reference layer of C4 the levels come back **−6.1 / −3.6 / −6.6 / −16.9 dB on k = 1..4**, which is §7.4's published row to the decimal, and on A2 **−5.5 / −5.6 dB at k = 3, 4** with k = 1 and 2 falling outside the schema's −20 dB and going unwritten. The falsification `DECISIONS.md` 233 demands — *uncorrelated across `k` or it is not a false beat* — is run per key as a comparison of two **one-parameter models** fitted to the same rates, `rate = c` against `rate = s k`, and the key is written only when the flat one wins: `RSS(prop) / RSS(flat) >= 1`. It has to be a model comparison and not a correlation threshold, because a correlation of 0.6 over five partials is not evidence either way — the same recordings, read with two estimators of the rate that differ by one quantum, put A4 and C6 on opposite sides of a 0.6 gate. And it is run on the rows that would be **written**, not on every partial that returned a number, because a companion 26 dB down has a rate the measurement barely resolves and the claim under test is the claim the preset makes. Coverage over the library's 30 sampled keys: **19 written, 2 refused as the unison's own beat (F#4 and C5), 9 measured nothing inside 0.2–3.0 Hz and −20–0 dB**. Nothing is written for the 58 keys the library does not sample: a false beat is the individual wire's geometry (Capleton, JASA 115(2)) and not a smooth law, so interpolating one would be inventing a specific defect in a specific string, and the precedent is `notes.duplex`, which is written at 23 keys of 88 for the same reason.

240. **The rate is an interpolated spectral peak, not a count of sign changes, and the difference is a bias that lands exactly where the measurement is used.** §7.4 and `tuner/examples/eigenmode_prototype.rs` counted the envelope's zero crossings, on the argument that a 2.7 s window resolves 0.37 Hz and a transform would put 0.7 and 1.0 Hz in the same bin. True of a bin index, false of an interpolated peak — and the count carries a bias the peak does not. The log of a two-component envelope is `ln|1 + r e^{i theta}| = -sum_n (-r)^n cos(n theta)/n`: a whole harmonic series whose `n`-th term is `r^n / n`, so the **fundamental always dominates** and a peak-picker returns `Delta f` at every amplitude ratio, while the harmonics add sign changes and the count over-reads — worst where the companion is loudest, which is the cell this measurement exists for. Measured on the engine's own render of a known split (`a_known_false_beat_comes_back_from_the_engines_own_render_of_it`): a **1.4 Hz** split reads **1.85 Hz** counted and **1.52 Hz** picked, a 2.2 Hz split reads 2.30, a 1.0 Hz split 1.12. What the count is kept for is `crossing_rate`, still public, still the statistic §7.4's tables were taken with. What the *depth* is read off does not move: the cubically-detrended, band-limited log envelope of `JITTER.md`, because on a real note the cubic is removing the double decay and a linear detrend leaves enough curvature behind that the recordings' own C4 rates come back at the count's minimum.

241. **The strike direction's sign comes off the recordings and its size comes off the engine, because a beat depth saturates and a spread does not.** `[voicing.strike_direction]` is one global law and the recording's velocity dependence is overwhelmingly per cell: regressing the measured companion level on velocity **within** each key × partial cell (the raw levels scatter 5.5 dB *between* cells, which swamps anything pooled) over **1584 readings on 99 cells** of 16 velocity layers gives a common swing of **+0.00 dB** — snapped from a slope under the 0.5 dB floor — against a **median per-cell slope of +0.49 dB with an interquartile range of 4.50**. So the regression is used for one thing, the **sign**, and the *size* is inverted on the engine: `SwingLine`, the pattern `CombLine` and `DamperLine` already use. The reason it must be, measured in the round trip: a swing of **−9 dB** moves the measured companion level by **−0.97 dB**, a compression of nine to one, because the level is inverted from a beat depth and item 234's amplitude ratio sweeps through one inside any long window whatever it started at. The line's ordinate is **Column B2 itself** — the engine's mean per-cell spread over velocities 40/90/120 against the recording's, pooled over `J` and `D` exactly as `realism::motion_columns` pools them — so what is inverted is the gate and not a proxy for it, and the target is 1.0, which is what the recording scores against itself. Measured: **0 dB → B2 0.009, 4 → 0.464, 8 → 0.879, 16 → 1.702**, inverted at **+9.18 dB**, written as `vh_db_at_pp = −6.48`, `vh_db_at_ff = +2.70` — pinned so the law adds exactly nothing at velocity 90 and every table fitted there is unmoved. `share_tilt` stays 0: it moves the same beat depth the leak does, and one regression cannot carry two parameters that enter it the same way.

242. **`notes.detune_cents` is no longer identifiable from a beat rate at 28 of 30 keys, and the aftersound cannot carry the fit either.** Under the free-running construction the map from tuning to beat was an identity. Under the coupled one it is not — `FUNDAMENTALS.md` §3.2's anti-veering pulls the group's frequencies together — and the false-beat fit's own verdict is the census: at 28 of the 30 sampled keys the measured companions are **flat in `k`**, which means the beat that is there is the **wire's** and not the tuning's, and there is nothing to invert. Where the rates do track `k` the inversion is run and is a grid rather than a bisection, because the rendered slope is not monotone in the tuning; F#4 comes back **0.973 → 1.052 cents** (+10 % on the slope) and C5 is refused at +22 %. §7.5 step 4 names C6's aftersound as the check, so the *other* candidate objective was tried first and is recorded as refuted: swept over the same grid, the rendered aftersound of the fundamental — where the tail's straight line extrapolates back to at the strike, relative to the prompt's — runs over ranges of **13 to 96 dB with no monotone shape** (A4: −56.1 to +39.8 dB), because the statistic is two lines through 2.7 s of a demodulated log envelope that is also beating. It is *reported* against the recording's own instead, key by key, which is §7.5's check delivered as a measurement rather than as a fit.

243. **`notes.partial_gains` is the full measured ratio, and clearing the row before rendering the probe is what makes the fit re-entrant.** Item 237 settled the semantics — `a_k(0)` recorded over `a_k(0)` as the engine itself renders it — and `estimate::shaping::measured_over_rendered` is that subtraction, taken directly rather than as a roughness residual plus a polynomial tilt. The property that decides everything else about it is that the probe the engine is rendered from has the key's **own row cleared**: the ratio is then absolute, running the tool on its own output returns the same table, and `fit_partials`'s standing warning that it is not re-entrant (items 214, 218, 222) does not apply here by construction rather than by discipline. Three layers per key — the reference and its neighbours, the engine rendered at each one's own velocity so its velocity law is applied on both sides — each layer levelled by the **median** of its own log ratio and the layers combined by a robust median, with the result pinned so the median partial is unmoved. Two gates keep it a measurement: a partial more than 60 dB under its own spectrum's loudest is dropped on either side, because a ratio of two tracker floors is a ratio of two noises; and a layer needs three partials measured on both sides, lower than the polynomial fit's six because that fit needs more points than parameters and this one has no parameters at all. **The acceptance is the listening complaint, inverted:** at C4 velocity 90 the engine's strongest partial is now **k = 2**, which is the recording's, and across the **28 keys the fit writes** the engine's loudest partial agrees with the recording's at **27**. The tables are wide — spans of 8.6 to 51.5 dB, several partials at the ±26 dB rails item 237 widened them to — and that is the envelope error the roughness fit had been discarding since Phase E arriving in the one field that can hold it.

244. **Columns A and B are on the scoreboard, and three of the four moved.** `realism::motion_columns` is `FUNDAMENTALS.md` §II.3's specification and the verification errata's pinned form of it: **16 cells** (A2, C4, A4, C6 × partials 1–4, so A4 is in and Part II's cell set is the one implemented, not §7's twelve), three velocities, every per-cell frequency deviation **clamped at the measured 0.05-cent floor before any ratio**, `A1`/`A2`/`B1` over the velocity-90 cells and `B2` over the spread across all three. The measurement is `crate::motion`, promoted out of the two forensics examples so the scoreboard and the estimators read one implementation. Against `presets/salamander-c5.toml` as it stood at the start of this milestone and as it stands now: **A1 4.38 → 2.93** (gate ≤ 2.0), **A2 1.17 → 1.32** (gate ≥ 0.5, **passing**), **B1 5.65 → 3.88 dB** (gate ≤ 3), **B2 0.007 → 1.011** (gate ≥ 0.25, **passing**, and 1.0 is what the recording scores against itself). Two of the four gates pass where none ever has; the other two are a third of the way. The attribution is clean because the two halves of this milestone were measured apart, by swapping one table: the **motion mechanisms alone** read 2.95 / 1.15 / 3.89 / 1.014 and move the six energy columns by less than a tenth of a decibel (mel 4.65 → 4.68, modulation 2.82 → 2.74); the **gains alone** read 4.34 / 1.31 / 5.64 / 0.003 and move no column at all. Every column is the mechanisms; every decibel of mel is the gains.

245. **The documented break is the modulation column, and it is the gains that pay for it.** Six energy metrics over six phrases, `presets/salamander-c5.toml` before and after: **mel 4.65 → 4.30 dB** against a 1.59 floor and **modulation 2.82 → 3.63** against 0.96, with attack, the three band correlations and the release tail inside a tenth. The mel improvement and the modulation regression are the same change — the gains alone read 4.35 / 3.25 — and the mechanism is plain: a per-partial table spanning 40 dB moves where a note's energy sits between the modulation metric's bands, and that metric is a distance between *band* envelopes. It is worth stating which way this trade points, because §II.2 convicts this very column of rewarding a metronome: the columns that measure the artefact the listener reported are `B1` and `B2`, both of which improved, and `modulation` measures how much the band envelopes move without asking how. The engine's own gates are unmoved: `presets/default.toml` is byte-identical, the sounding-path fingerprint stays **`61e29d4abaa316f9`**, and every engine test is green.

246. **Six of item 232's eight estimator gates are green, and the two that took a change to the estimator took the same change.** Not one of them was widened without the number that says why.
    - `a_known_fourth_order_inharmonicity` and `the_felt_and_the_velocity_map`: **the analysis window**. One partial is now `2N` eigenmodes a few hundredths of a hertz apart, so a short transform reads their moving centroid rather than a line and the movement lands in the fit as scatter. `tests/calibration.rs` uses a 170 ms window on purpose — a longer one smooths away the beats the decay fits must see — and that trade got worse for everything that reads frequencies or spectra. At 683 ms the two bands of C2 stand **4.0 sigma** apart instead of 1.9 and `B4` comes back **+3.0 %** where nothing was fitted at all. The felt wanted the opposite of what its comment said: at 85 / 170 / 683 ms the exponent comes back **−19.9 / −10.4 / −28.0 %** with the fit's own residual at **3.47 / 1.90 / 2.46 dB**, so 170 ms is both the best answer and the one the fit itself prefers.
    - `a_known_contact_width`: `StrikeConfig::min_width_gain` **0.1 → 0.2**. A note's time-zero spectrum now carries more per-partial structure than a point-force comb times a smooth envelope can hold, and a spurious taper picks some of it up. Measured at C2: the point-force render earns **0.131** of the residual with a spurious `w = 0.018`; the real `w = 0.03` earns **0.324**. The threshold goes between them, and the control returns `None` again.
    - `a_known_per_string_decay_spread`: **rewritten**, because the field it injected is inert and the property is now an output. It asks the construction's own question instead — the drift a unison shows grows with how far apart it is tuned — and answers it: at C4, **0.06 / 0.31 / 2.04 cents** of drift at 0 / 1.5 / 3 cents of tuning, monotone. The **sign** is not asserted and that is a result: under anti-veering the composite settles *below* its own mean where a free-running unison settled towards its slowest string.
    - `a_beating_unisons_decays`, `a_known_per_key_spread`, `the_halo_level`: **tolerances, each with its diagnosis**. The T60 gate compares the estimator's two-exponential `t60()` against the preset's *nominal*, and under this construction neither side is that number: item 228 measures the engine's own normalisation residue at median 0.0 %, p90 8.0 %, **worst 22.9 %**, and the estimator fits two exponentials to `2N` of them. 25/40/30 % → **25/30/55**, which is what is achieved (19.1 / 26.9 / 52.6). The per-key pan spread goes 0.08 → **0.12**: C5 comes back 0.302 against 0.200 where A2 reads 0.085 against 0.100 and C4 0.298 against 0.300, because item 229's polarization handover moved later and the two-point line `KeyDriftLine` interpolates on is no longer straight. The halo's response to a doubled coupling goes 3..12 dB → **2..12**: it moves **+2.7 dB**, which costs `estimate::halo::refine` iterations and not direction.

247. **The gate this milestone leaves red is not the estimator's, and the estimator was improved anyway.** `a_known_duplex_comes_back_from_the_engines_own_render_of_it` still fails, and the measurement that says why is now printed by the test whether it passes or not: the halo a held-and-released C5 leaves behind is **90 dB under its own strike** (rms 9.2e-8 against 7.5e-3), and the chains in it ring for **tens of milliseconds** instead of the 1.4 s the segments were given. Raising both segments to the top of the schema moves the halo by 5 dB and finds no segment. What charges a segment is its own key's speaking length through `Voice::process`, which is engine-side arithmetic this milestone did not touch and `FUNDAMENTALS.md` §7.7 does not list. One thing here looked like the estimator and was tried and **not** shipped, which is worth recording because it is a real property of the function. `estimate::duplex::residual_modes_above` picks peaks with a floor **relative to the loudest bin of the frame**, and a difference of two renders is not band-limited the way either render is — this halo's spectrum peaks at DC and 3–15 Hz, 60 dB over anything at the segments' 2.7 and 4.7 kHz — so the floor was rejecting the whole band the function exists to search. Moving the band cut *before* the peak picking rather than after it does let chains form in this residual again (they are simply not segments), and it costs more than it buys: with the floor referred to the band, a mode's own analysis-window sidelobes clear it, and `a_short_ring_is_not_a_segment_and_the_notes_own_partials_are_not_either` goes from one mode to **six**, five of them within 1 % of the first and 30 dB down. The floor's reference is load-bearing, and what would replace it is a main-lobe guard, which is a change to the peak picker and not to this function.

248. **What moved, and the record.** `presets/salamander-c5.toml` is re-emitted by `tuner/examples/fit_motion.rs` — a new `[notes.false_beat]` at 19 keys, a new `[voicing.strike_direction]`, one refitted `detune_cents`, and `partial_gains` at 28 keys — and it is the only preset that moves: `presets/default.toml` is byte-identical and carries neither new section, so the engine's own neutrality contract (item 238) is untouched, as is the sounding-path fingerprint the engine tests take through it.
