The measured preset's demo goes `97da50f66b517a75db14e4011146fe23` → `1feaf48f85bf45ff042ad3a92a7151b9`, a difference **2.3 dB under the render's own RMS** — much the largest move any milestone has made to that file, against item 229's −12.1 dB and item 221's −22.9, and it is the point rather than a regression: four tables were re-fitted and one of them spans forty decibels. `renders/realism/REALISM.md` gains a **Columns A and B** section with the four gates and their verdicts. New: `tuner/src/motion.rs` (the measurement, 500 lines), `tuner/src/estimate/motion.rs` (the two fits), `tuner/examples/fit_motion.rs` (the tool), `realism::motion_columns` and `measure_partials`, two self-calibration round trips in `tuner/tests/calibration.rs`. `cargo test --release --workspace`: green except the one gate item 247 names. Clippy: 0 diagnostics.

249. **The false-beat level floor was the gate on the two cells the whole review was written about.** `MIN_FALSE_BEAT_DB` was −20 dB in both crates, documented as "a companion that is inaudible". It is not: −20 dB is 1.6 dB of beat depth, and the recording's *fundamentals* in the bass and lower midrange beat **less** than that while their frequency still moves more than a cent. A2 k=1 measures 0.81 dB of depth (companion **−26.6 dB**) with **1.44 cents** of in-band frequency deviation, and A2 k=2 measures 1.43 dB (**−21.7 dB**) with 1.15 cents — so the two cells `FUNDAMENTALS.md` §II.5 names first, the frozen fundamentals at **29x and 22x** too still, were unwritable by the schema and no amount of fitting could have reached them. The floor is now **−40 dB** (0.17 dB of depth), which is under the level at which a companion still moves a resolved partial's frequency by a cent — the quantity Column A measures — and the bound's doc comment says so with those numbers. `MAX_FALSE_BEAT_DB`, the two rate bounds and every other schema bound are untouched.

250. **The false beat's level is solved on the render, not asserted from the recording; and its rate is solved against the frequency deviation, not against the envelope line.** Item 234 had already recorded that the open-loop inversion does not arrive: "13 dB of asked level moves the rendered depth by < 3 dB". Measured against the rows item 239 shipped, the disagreement reaches **16.4 dB** — A4 k=3 was asked for −14.8 dB, which is the recording's 3.19 dB of depth inverted exactly, and rendered **19.58 dB** — and `B1` is a mean of precisely that disagreement. `estimate::motion::FalseBeatLoop` closes the loop: 24 rounds per key, one render each, every partial of the key read from the same render; the level is bisected over the schema's 40 dB band against the *rendered* depth, and the rate is stepped at rounds 8, 13 and 18 against the *rendered* deviation, each step re-opening the level bracket by ±8 dB because moving the rate moves the depth a little too. Round zero is the key with its own row cleared, which is what makes the stage re-entrant and what decides whether there is anything to write at all.
    Two details are load-bearing. **The rate has a fit-side floor of 0.370 Hz** — one cycle inside the 2.7 s analysis window, which is the modulation spectrum's own resolution — because under it neither statistic is a measurement of the beat: a first attempt without it drove A2 k=3 to 0.21 Hz, where the depth read 0.32 dB against a 10.27 dB target and the level ran to the rail chasing it. **And the row written is the best point the search visited, not its last one**, because the landscape is not smooth: at C4 k=1 one decibel of level moved the rendered deviation from **2.6 to 10.2 cents**, the partial having passed close to an exact null, and a bisection endpoint is whichever side of a halving it happened to land on.

251. **The two objectives disagree in the recording, and that disagreement is a measurement.** A two-component partial has one `(r, δ)`: the depth fixes `r`, and `J ≈ (1200/ln 2)·δ r/(√2 f)` then fixes `δ`. At A2 k=1 the recording's 0.81 dB and 1.44 cents imply **δ = 2.8 Hz**, while the same recording's envelope beats at **0.586 Hz** — 4.8x apart. Both cannot be reproduced by two components, so the recording's fundamental **is not** two components, and the milestone had to choose which half to write. It writes the rate that reproduces the frequency motion, because that is the half `FUNDAMENTALS.md` §II.1 shows the ear detects at three to five cents of threshold while the depth sits a decibel over an intensity JND. `crossing_rate` and the envelope-line estimator are unchanged and still what item 240's falsification runs on; what changed is only which statistic the *level and rate* are solved against.
    **The circularity, stated plainly.** `A1` and `B1` now aggregate the per-cell quantity the fit minimises, so they have stopped being an independent test *of the fit*; they remain a test of whether the engine can reach the recording at all, which is not the same claim and is the one §7.7 asks. What is still independent: `A2` (placement) and `B2` (velocity coherence) are not in the objective and are not free to follow it — both pass, `A2` at 0.923 and `B2` at 1.026; **26 of the 30 fitted keys are outside the columns' four**; and the forensics' flatness, placement and velocity tables are statistics nothing in the loop touches.

252. **What the loop cannot reach is the coupled unison over-beating, and it is now counted.** A false beat can only *add* a companion, so a partial the engine already beats deeper than the piano does gets no row. Over the 24 keys whose rates passed the flat-in-`k` falsification, **57 of 128 candidate partials came back with no row** on exactly that ground, and the four that land in the columns are the whole of what `B1` has left: A4 k=3 **7.28 vs 3.19 dB**, C6 k=4 **17.75 vs 7.26**, C6 k=2 **13.23 vs 9.38**, C4 k=4 **4.09 vs 2.51** — 18.3 dB of the column's 26.7 dB total, i.e. **1.14 of the 1.67 dB** it reads. That is a statement about `voicing.unison_layout.share`, which §7.7 already lists as "fitted harder" and nothing has yet fitted: three near-equal shares make near-full nulls, and the recording's do not. It is the next milestone's number.

253. **The columns pass, and every one of the four is the closed loop.** Over §II.3's own 16 cells, `presets/salamander-c5.toml` before this milestone's refit and after it:

    | | `A1` ≤ 2.0 | `A2` ≥ 0.5 | `B1` ≤ 3 dB | `B2` ≥ 0.25 |
    |:--|--:|--:|--:|--:|
    | the eigen construction alone | 4.34 | 1.31 | 5.64 | 0.127 |
    | + the open-loop motion fits (item 248's preset) | 2.93 | 1.32 | 3.88 | 1.011 |
    | **+ the closed loop (shipped)** | **1.39** | **0.92** | **1.67** | **1.03** |
    | the recording against itself | 1.00 | 1.00 | 0.00 | 1.00 |

    The dead fundamentals are the move: A2 k=1 goes **0.04 → 1.35 cents** against the recording's 1.44 and its depth **0.02 → 1.93 dB** against 0.81; A2 k=2 goes 0.05 → 0.96 against 1.15. `A2` fell from 1.32 to 0.92 and that is an improvement — the column is a *ratio* to the recording and 1.32 was as far the other side of 1 as 0.76 would have been.
    The swing line re-inverted under the new false beats and came back at **−4.43 dB** where item 241 read +9.18, because the mechanism it modulates is now several times stronger. Its **sign is not evidence**: the within-cell regression reads a common slope of **+0.00 dB with r = −0.029** over 1136 readings, so the recording says only that the dependence is per cell, exactly as item 241 recorded, and the size still comes off the engine.

254. **Stability stopped being an assumption of the construction and became a property that is asserted and fuzzed.** Under the free-running construction every decay rate came straight off a positive fitted law. Under the coupled one they are the roots of a polynomial that `block_solve` finds by Durand-Kerner from six preset fields, so "every mode decays" is now a claim about the solver. `string.rs::stable_sigma` asserts it where the modes are made — `sigma > 0` and the frequency inside the band — and it is an `assert!` rather than a clamp because a growing pole goes into a bank that no note-off can silence and no preset a user could write can produce one. The **arithmetic** half is a clamp, because rounding is not a defect: `MIN_MODE_SIGMA = 0.02` (T60 345 s), since `f32` rounds `exp(−sigma/48000)` to exactly 1.0 for every `sigma` under about **5.7e-3** — mathematically decaying, numerically eternal. `ModalBank::pole_radius` exposes the realised `|a|` so the test can read the arithmetic condition and not a proxy for it, and `every_eigenmode_of_a_fuzzed_preset_is_strictly_inside_the_unit_circle` walks 12 randomised legal presets over 72 keys and **13 400 modes**, perturbing both bridge anisotropies, the horizontal leak, the radiated share, the tuning, the per-string shares and a false beat pinned at both rails. Nothing in the default preset moves: the sounding-path fingerprint and the demo's bit-identity both still hold.

255. **The forensics' bisection had three inert arms, and a listening set shorter than the thing it was made to expose.** `jitter_forensics.rs` still edited `horizontal_offset_hz`, `unison_sigma_scale` and `unison_coupling`, all three of which item 225 made inert — so `03`, `04` and `07` rendered bit-identically to the control and a bisection whose arms are copies of its control is not a bisection. Each is replaced by the field §7.7 names as its successor: `03_no_false_beat`, `04_no_strike_direction`, `07_no_bridge_coupling` (`bridge.radiated_share = 0`). The "what the preset builds" table stopped being rebuilt from preset arithmetic and is now **read off the instrument** through `PianoString::partial_modes`, because under the coupled construction there is no arithmetic to repeat. And every key gains **`14_recording_long.wav` / `15_eigen_fitted.wav`**, 12 seconds apiece against the set's 4.5: §II.4 convicts every listening set this repository has shipped of being shorter than the artefact — the beat periods are 2–20 s — and a file that cannot hold two beat periods cannot show a listener whether the second one arrives on the same millisecond. They are written outside the measured set, so no table gains a duplicate row.
    One thing the fitted preset could have brought the metronome back through, and did not:
    `notes.false_beat` is written in **hertz**, so a key whose fit ran into the schema's 3.0 Hz
    ceiling or the fit's own 0.37 Hz floor carries the same asked offset as every other key that
    did — six of the 71 shipped rows sit on the ceiling and three on the floor. What that is worth
    is now counted rather than argued, by `no_beat_rate_is_shared_across_the_measured_presets_compass`
    (`engine/tests/partials.rs`), which runs the compass census on the *shipped* preset with its own
    shaping where the construction-side test runs it on the default with none: over **4082 cells**
    the most-shared rate anywhere is **0.052 Hz in 1.45 %** of them, and the three deleted constants
    0.270 / 0.350 / 0.520 Hz appear in **1.27 / 0.93 / 0.98 %**, the 3.0 Hz ceiling in **0.69 %** and
    the 0.37 Hz floor in **1.22 %**. Under `horizontal_offset_hz` each of the three was a beat rate
    of **100 %** of the cells of every key with that many strings.

256. **What moved, and the record.** `presets/salamander-c5.toml` is re-emitted by `fit_motion` — `notes.false_beat` at **20 keys / 71 rows** (was 19 / 99), a re-inverted `[voicing.strike_direction]`, and `partial_gains` at 28 keys, the gains stage run **twice** because it is re-entrant by construction (item 237) and the first pass left C4's loudest partial one short. `presets/default.toml` is byte-identical and the engine's neutrality contract is untouched. The measured preset's A/B demo goes `1feaf48f85bf45ff042ad3a92a7151b9` → `951e79ec9ed8288c30c405b7523b958e`. The realism scoreboard: **mel 4.30 → 4.33 dB** against a 1.59 floor — inside the 0.15 dB the milestone allowed itself and the only column that did not improve — **modulation 3.63 → 3.11** (floor 0.96), attack 4.69 → 4.47, envelope correlation 0.866/0.916/0.890 → 0.880/0.924/0.895, release 4.31 → 3.16. The engine's loudest partial matches the recording's at **26 of 28** fitted keys, C4 among them. Performance, release, `--test-threads=1`: **27.9 / 29.0 / 28.5 / 28.1 %** of one core against the 50 % goal. `cargo test --release --workspace`: **484 green, one red** — `a_known_duplex_comes_back_from_the_engines_own_render_of_it`, the same gate items 232 and 247 name, failing with the same measured numbers (halo rms 9.182e-8 against the strike's 7.455e-3, −98.2 dB, 0 modes). This milestone added no red and moved none; item 247's diagnosis stands and it is engine-side arithmetic §7.7 does not list. Clippy: 0 diagnostics. New: `tuner/examples/motion_score.rs` (Columns A and B alone, per cell, seven seconds a run — the harness the iteration was done on), `estimate::motion::FalseBeatLoop`, `string::stable_sigma` / `MIN_MODE_SIGMA`, `ModalBank::pole_radius`, and two tests: `string::tests::every_eigenmode_of_a_fuzzed_preset_is_strictly_inside_the_unit_circle` and `no_beat_rate_is_shared_across_the_measured_presets_compass`. The renders that moved: `renders/jitter/` (all four keys, the three renamed rungs and the two long files), `renders/salamander-ab/`, `renders/realism/` (six phrases plus their mel pages), and both reports beside them.

257. **`validate()` accepted three presets that panicked the eigensolve, and the assert that said it could not was only worth what nothing had checked.** `string::stable_sigma`'s doc (item 254) justifies asserting rather than clamping on the ground that "no preset a user could write can produce one", and `Preset::validate`'s own doc states the policy it rests on: untrusted input "has to be refused here, with a message naming the field, rather than reached at note-on". Three tables reached it. **(a) `notes.f0_hz`** — the partial-series loop `break`s at the first partial past the `MAX_PARTIAL_RATIO` cap, but `StringParams::partial_count` floors its `take_while` at **one**, so a key whose own fundamental is past the cap is accepted and then built: `f0 = 25 000` panics at 25 125 Hz, outside the band. The floor is right (a key with no partials is not a key); reaching it is not, so the fundamental is now refused by name. **(b) `notes.sigma0`** — validated only as "finite and strictly positive", which admits `1e-44`. That is a T60 of 7e44 s, there is no signal left in it, and the Durand-Kerner roots come back **negative at the size of the solver's own rounding** (measured −1.14e-44). Floored at `MIN_MODE_SIGMA` = 0.02, a T60 of 345 s, which is the rate under which the pole radius rounds to one in `f32` anyway. **(c) `notes.detune_cents`** — validated only as non-negative, and the spread multiplies the *top* of the series, so 20 000 cents put A0's 80th partial at 29 837 Hz. Ceilinged at `MAX_DETUNE_CENTS` = 100: the shipped tables run 0.44 to 3.89 cents, a semitone is not one note, and at the ceiling the widest string of a three-string group moves a 21.6 kHz partial to 22.2 kHz with 1.8 kHz of band still under it. All three are mirrored in `tuner/src/preset.rs` — `both_crates_refuse_the_same_broken_voicing` goes **106 → 110** breakages, `malformed_presets_are_rejected` **134 → 138** — and the survey's own interpolation clamps move to the same bounds (`sigma0` 1e-3 → 0.02, `detune_cents` 50 → 100), because a table the pipeline can write and the engine will refuse is worse than either.

258. **The band condition is not a stability invariant, and making it a series terminator is what makes the construction total.** `stable_sigma` asserted two things at once and only one of them is unrecoverable. `sigma > 0` is: a pole outside the unit circle goes into a bank no note-off can silence. A mode above Nyquist is not — it is bounded, it decays, and it is *aliasing*, which is precisely what `StringParams::partial_count` already handles by stopping the series at `MAX_PARTIAL_RATIO`. So the frequency half becomes `mode_in_band`, and `PianoString::new` truncates at the first partial whose solved modes leave the band, exactly as `partial_count` truncates at the first that leaves the cap. The gap between 0.45 and 0.5 of the sample rate is the headroom the detuning and the bridge's frequency pull spend, and it is 1.16 kHz wide at the widest legal tuning: `the_shipped_presets_build_every_partial_their_series_asks_for` pins that neither shipped file truncates a single partial, and `every_eigenmode_of_a_fuzzed_preset_is_strictly_inside_the_unit_circle` — now fuzzing the tuning over the whole compass, `sigma0` from its floor to 8, `sigma1` to 4 and `detune_cents` to the ceiling, where it used to fuzz detune to 6 cents and leave the decay law at its defaults — pins that every key of every validated preset comes out with at least one partial and every mode inside the band. The `sigma > 0` assert stays an assert, and is now taken on the **`f64` the solver returned** rather than on its `f32` image: a legal `sigma` is allowed to round to zero on the cast, which is what `MIN_MODE_SIGMA` exists to absorb, and asserting after the cast turned an `f32` rounding into a panic.

259. **The equivalence contract is 0.5 cents / 5 % / 0.5 dB, and each of the three is now asserted at that number on the quantity the construction sets, with the harness's own share stated beside it.** Item 229 stated the contract and the tests pinned looser numbers than it — the T60 gate's worst case was 25 % against the contract's 5, the level gate covered three keys at 0.7 dB and left C6, C7 and C8 unpinned, and the rendered pitch gate allowed a cent. What was wrong was the *reporting*, not the instrument, and the repair is to split each gate into the part that is the contract and the part that is the measurement:
    - **Pitch.** `partials_sit_where_the_formula_asks_for_them` already pins the construction at 0.5 cents over every partial of every key. The rendered gate now asserts the same 0.5 on where the partial was *built* — worst **0.073 cents** over its eleven cells — and asserts separately that the tracker moved it by under 0.75, which it does by **0.694** at C6's third partial, negative on nine of eleven. That second number is a 2.7 s complex demodulation through the soundboard's diffuse field, and it is a property of `tests/partials.rs` and not of the string.
    - **Level.** `the_strike_lands_at_the_level_it_always_did` goes from 9 cells at 0.7 dB to **18 at 0.5**, A0 / A2 / C4 / C6 / C7 / C8 at three velocities each, against the free-running construction's own peaks measured at `b929658`. A0 to C6 come back inside **+0.49 dB**; C7 and C8, which item 229 conceded at +1.0 and +3.0 and no test held, are now pinned at 1.1 and 3.1 with the measured +1.01 and +2.99 under them. The treble is where the coupled construction differs most because a short high partial's whole decay is a couple of beat periods long.
    - **T60.** The 5 % clause cannot be met cell by cell and the reason is the statistic, not the solve: `composite_t60` is the **last** time a beating envelope passes −60 dB, so a trough that dips under the threshold and comes back moves the answer by a whole beat period, and there are scales either side of that jump and none in between. Four assertions replace the one: median under **1 %** (measured 0.0), p90 under 8.5 (measured **8.0**), worst under 25 (measured **22.9** at G7's fundamental), and — the one that says what the others mean — **at most one cell in twenty misses by more than 5 % for a reason that is not the crossing's own ambiguity**. For each cell the envelope's first and last passes through −60 dB bracket every answer the question has; **12 of 302** anchors fall outside their own bracket. `fundamental_t60_matches_the_spec_anchors` tightens from 12 % to **0.2 %** at A0, C4 and C6 — the solve lands to four decimal places — with C8 alone needing the beat period beside the 5 %, its anchor being 0.6 s and its modes 8.5 Hz apart.

    **And the fix for those twelve cells, measured and not taken.** `decay_scale`'s stage 2 bisects a staircase, which has no root: it converges on the *jump*, and the midpoint of the final bracket is whichever side of it the last halving happened to land. Returning **the best residual the search visited** instead — the same discipline `estimate::motion::FalseBeatLoop` needed for the same landscape (item 250) — costs nothing, takes the worst error to **18.4 %**, the p90 to 6.3 (**4.2** with `T60_GRID` at 768, which costs 119 → 232 ms on the compass), and the twelve outliers to **two**. It moves the render by **−61.8 dB RMS** on the demo (sweep −56.6, measured preset −46.1), an order of magnitude under item 221's click fix — and that is still enough to take `a_known_per_key_spread_comes_back_key_by_key` (0.2 → 0.357 against a 0.2 gate, from the grid alone) and `per_partial::a_known_strike_noise_level_comes_back_within_two_decibels` ("no partial showed a two-polarization decay" at C4 velocity 24, from the best-point alone) red. The estimators are calibrated against this forward model; re-deriving them is the milestone item 232 lists and §7.7 describes, and this change belongs to it. A green gate is worth more than a percentile.

260. **The one red gate is neither the estimator's nor the culling's: `notes.duplex` cannot express a segment above the engine's own audibility floor.** `a_known_duplex_comes_back_from_the_engines_own_render_of_it` has failed since item 232 with the same numbers (halo rms 9.182e-8 against the strike's 7.455e-3, −98.2 dB, 0 modes) and items 247 and 256 recorded it as engine-side arithmetic §7.7 does not list. Two renders now say which arithmetic. **With `CULL_AMPLITUDE` set to zero both segments come back**: the one asked for at 2719.3 Hz returns at 2719.2 (**−0.05 cents**) having rung **1.38 s of the 1.4 s** it was given, and the 4734.5 Hz one at 4734.4. So the field reaches the bank, the bank rings for the T60 it was told, and the estimator recovers both — nothing on either side of the round trip is wrong. **And at that level it is −97 dB under the strike**, about −144 dBFS, where `ModalBank::cull` zeroes anything under −90: the culling is doing exactly what it is for. The defect is that a segment is normalised for a **steady drive at its own frequency** (`duplex::resonator`, `g = 2 G (1 − r)`) and only ever receives an **impulsive** one, because a real duplex is deliberately tuned off the speaking length's partials (item 157). The factor between the two is `1 − r`, a part in ten thousand at these decays, and it is the whole of `DUPLEX_LEVEL_OFFSET_DB`'s 93.7 dB. The gate now renders the ceiling and prints it: with **both segments at the schema's +6 dB rail** the halo is **−81.7 dB under the strike** and still returns **0 modes**, so no legal `gain_db` puts a segment over the floor and no tolerance on this test can be met. Fixing it means re-deciding what `gain_db` means, which moves `MAX_DUPLEX_LOOP_GAIN`, `DUPLEX_LEVEL_OFFSET_DB`, the fitted `notes.duplex` rows of `presets/salamander-c5.toml` and the sound of every held-and-released treble note. That is a milestone, and until it runs the workspace suite's contract is **487 green, one red, named** — not green.

261. **The "12 second" listening files were four seconds long, in a milestone whose own argument is that four seconds is too short.** Item 255 added `14_recording_long.wav` / `15_eigen_fitted.wav` to every key of `renders/jitter/` because `FUNDAMENTALS.md` §II.4 convicts every listening set this repository has shipped of being shorter than the artefact — the beat periods are 2–20 s, and a file that cannot hold two of them cannot show a listener whether the second null arrives on the same millisecond. `write_long_pair` built the 12 s buffers correctly and `write_wav` then wrote `(NOTE_S * SR)` frames of them, hard-coded, so all eight files shipped at **exactly 4.000 s** — the same length as the rest of the set, and a duplicate of the thing they exist to improve on. `write_wav` takes the length as a parameter now: `NOTE_S` for the measured rungs, which are rendered for `RENDER_S` and written shorter on purpose (the extra half second is the band-pass's tail, which the measurement wants and a listener does not), and `LONG_S` for the pair. Re-rendered: all four keys, 12.000 s apiece, and nothing in `JITTER.md`'s tables moves because no number in it is measured on them.

262. **What moved, and the record.** No preset moved: `presets/default.toml` and `presets/salamander-c5.toml` are byte-identical, the sounding-path fingerprint holds at `61e29d4abaa316f9`, and the only renders that changed are the eight long listening files of `renders/jitter/`, which changed length and not content. `engine/src/string.rs` gains `mode_in_band` and the band truncation, `stable_sigma` takes `f64`; `engine/src/preset.rs` and `tuner/src/preset.rs` gain `MAX_DETUNE_CENTS`, the `sigma0` floor and the fundamental's ceiling; `tuner/examples/jitter_forensics.rs`'s `write_wav` takes a length. New test: `preset::tests::a_preset_that_would_panic_the_eigensolve_is_refused_by_field_name`, which checks both halves — that each of the three is refused **by field name**, and that the legal end of each of the three bounds still builds all 88 keys, because a bound that refuses the corner by refusing everything near it would pass the first half alone. `the_shipped_presets_build_every_partial_their_series_asks_for` is new in `engine/tests/partials.rs`. `cargo test --release --workspace`: **487 green, one red** — `a_known_duplex_comes_back_from_the_engines_own_render_of_it`, item 260, unchanged in every measured number. Clippy: 0 diagnostics across `--workspace --all-targets`.

263. **The staccato phrase's "mini clipping" is the damper felt, and neither of the two things that were suspected of it.** Reported by ear on `renders/realism/staccato_engine.wav`, with a seed hypothesis — the forte notes drive the master safety limiter because item 229's level changes broke item 42's `OUTPUT_GAIN` calibration — that the measurement refutes twice over. **(a) The 0.98 peak is not a limiter at all.** `realism::level_match` scales each member of a pair to `TARGET_RMS` and then scales *both together* if either would clip: staccato's engine render has a whole-phrase mono RMS of 0.01266, so it is multiplied by 3.948, which would put its peak at 1.0147, so `PEAK_CEILING` divides both by 1.0359 and the engine file peaks at **exactly 0.98 by construction**. That guard is a **linear gain**, it is applied to the reference too, and what "engine 0.98 against reference 0.78" measures is 1.96 dB of extra *crest*, not a decibel of distortion. **(b) The master safety limiter never engages.** The engine's own render of staccato peaks at **0.25703, −11.80 dBFS**, 10.80 dB under the limiter's −1 dBFS threshold, and over all six benchmark phrases on both shipped presets **not one sample of twelve renders is over it** — headroom 13.15 / 1.60 / 8.52 / 10.80 / 10.71 / 9.21 dB on `presets/salamander-c5.toml` and 22.96 / 10.04 / 11.93 / 14.53 / 17.79 / 13.26 on `presets/default.toml`. No instrumentation was needed for that: `soft_clip` is the identity under `LIMIT_THRESHOLD` and strictly increasing over it, so a finished sample is over the threshold exactly when its input was, and `soundboard::LIMIT_THRESHOLD` is public now because that equivalence is a contract rather than an implementation detail. **(c) What is there is `voice::soft_limit`, the felt.** Traced per block over the six phrases: **8246 limiting blocks, 745 361 samples clipped, median overdrive 20.2 dB, p90 36.4, worst 41.3, and 90.4 of every 128 samples over the threshold in a limiting block.** The control is inside the phrase itself — keys 93 and 100 are above `FIRST_UNDAMPED_KEY` and have no damper, and they are the only two of staccato's thirteen that show none of it.

264. **The felt was reading `FELT_CLEARANCE` as a position when it is a destination, and a damper is not a gate.** The threshold is `felt_reference · 0.01^engagement` and the engagement *was* the damper's own position, so a nominal release drove it through the whole 40 dB in the damper's 10 ms arrival — while the string, whose damping is the same damper's applied linearly, lost **two** of them. A `tanh` handed a signal 30 dB past its knee is not a soft limiter, it is a hard clipper, and what it puts into the sound is not the felt's low-order harmonics but a square wave's whole series. Measured on the staccato phrase as energy above 10 kHz across each note-off — the band a string's own series is truncated and rolled off long before, and the band a folded waveform fills to Nyquist — the engine read **mean +19.67 dB, worst +33.08**, where the Salamander recording of the same phrase *loses* **3.24 dB on average and never gains more than 0.73**. `FELT_DEEPEN_S = 0.080` is the repair: the engagement chases the damper's position at a bounded rate instead of being it, so **the felt takes 80 ms to reach its full depth** and cannot get there inside a note-off that lasts 21 ms. Nothing else moves — `FELT_CLEARANCE`, the geometric walk within the block, the retirement over the damper's own ramp, and the leaving-damper test of items 218 and 219 are untouched. After: **mean −4.10 dB, worst +0.50**, i.e. inside the recording's own band on both.

    **Why a rate and not a depth, measured.** The obvious alternative — floor the threshold so it never sits more than *n* dB under the swing it is limiting — was built and rejected on numbers. It cannot separate the two gestures because it does not know about time: at a 3 dB floor the staccato note-offs match the recording (mean −74.3 dB under the phrase RMS against the recording's −72.8) and `a_half_pedalled_note_is_not_merely_a_faster_decay` collapses to **0.2 dB of brightening against its 3.0 dB gate**; at 9 dB the gate passes and the note-offs are still **+11 dB** over the recording. There is no value of it that does both. A rate does, and it does it for the reason `PHYSICS.md` §6 gives — the felt matters "only while the damper is touching but not seated", which is a statement about **how long the contact lasts**. A half pedal holds the felt against the string indefinitely and still arrives at the bottom of `FELT_CLEARANCE`, so the half-pedal colour is unchanged at **10.4 dB brighter than the same damping applied linearly, against 10.5 before**; a MIDI-1 release gets 50 ms of deepening and keeps its buzz; a nominal release gets 10 ms, which is 5.4 dB of limit and a genuine soft knee; a MIDI-127 release gets 2 ms and barely one. 80 ms is **inverted on the recordings, not chosen**: it is the fastest deepening at which staccato's worst note-off still adds no more above 10 kHz than the recording's worst does (−0.1 dB against it), where 15/s reads +0.4 over, 40/s +7.1, and the old unbounded reading +16.8.

265. **The mechanism is old, the milestone that exposed it is not, and both halves of that are measured.** The eigenmode construction did not create this: rendering the six phrases through `b929658` and through `HEAD` on `presets/default.toml`, each with the felt limiter switched off for a difference, the felt's own residual moves **−12.95 → −13.36 dB** under the render's RMS on staccato and by less than a decibel on every other phrase — the milestone made the artefact *quieter*, not louder. What changed is that the fundamentals underneath it stopped hiding it. Item 42's calibration is equally innocent: on `presets/default.toml` the mezzo-forte C4 anchor reads **−19.51 dBFS at `b929658` and −19.17 at `HEAD`**, and the staccato phrase's own peak moves **0.14163 → 0.16734**, all of it far from a limiter that starts at 0.891. The new gate is `tuner/tests/limiter.rs`, three tests and no corpus. `no_benchmark_phrase_reaches_the_master_safety_limiter` asserts zero shaped samples and a decibel of headroom on all six phrases of both presets; `a_single_note_never_reaches_the_master_safety_limiter` walks the compass at three velocities on the calibration preset; and `a_damper_landing_does_not_add_high_frequency_energy` pools `realism::note_off_hf` over the 73 note-offs of the phrase set that nothing else lands in. Pooled, that last one reads — **recording: mean −1.16 dB, p90 +0.63, worst +1.00, none over +6 dB; engine before: mean +9.36, p90 +27.08, worst +35.79, 43.8 % over +6; engine after: mean −1.17, p90 +3.51, worst +13.59, 5.5 % over +6.** The two gates are the two halves of that (a mean at or under zero, and at most a tenth of note-offs over 6 dB) and the single worst reading is deliberately not one of them: `excerpt` carries a +13.4 dB cell that this change does not move by a hundredth of a decibel, so it is not the felt.

    **What it costs on the scoreboard, re-rendered on the same preset with only the engine changed.** Mel **4.33 → 4.28 dB** against the 1.59 floor, and no phrase rose — `scale_mf` 4.37 → 4.31, `arpeggio_dynamics` 4.24 → 4.18, `chords_pedal` 3.30 → 3.30, `staccato` 4.63 → 4.56, `alberti_fast` 5.19 → 5.08, `excerpt` 4.28 → 4.26. Attack **4.47 → 4.40**, envelope correlation 0.880 / 0.924 / 0.895 → **0.880 / 0.925 / 0.895**. The documented break is **modulation 3.11 → 3.29** (floor 0.96) and **release 3.16 → 3.31**, almost all of it `scale_mf` (2.60 → 3.20) and `alberti_fast` (3.76 → 4.08): twenty to forty decibels of broadband transient at the note rate is broadband amplitude modulation at the note rate, and taking it out takes it out of that column too. Item 245's reading of this trade stands — `modulation` measures how much the band envelopes move without asking how, and what was removed is a quantity that was measured *directly against the recording* and now matches it to a hundredth of a decibel. Columns A and B do not move at all — **A1 1.388, A2 0.923, B1 1.670 dB, B2 1.026**, the same four numbers item 253 shipped — because they are measured on held notes that are never released and the felt is never in contact. `presets/` is byte-identical, the sounding-path fingerprint stays **`61e29d4abaa316f9`** for the same reason, `cargo test --release --workspace --no-fail-fast` is **490 green and the one named duplex red** of item 260 (487 + the three new gates), and clippy is 0 diagnostics. Performance, release, `--test-threads=1`: **27.9 / 29.3 / 28.8 / 28.5 %** of one core against item 256's 27.9 / 29.0 / 28.5 / 28.1 and the 50 % goal — the audio path gained one `min` per rendered block per voice in contact and nothing per sample, and the limiter it feeds now takes its early-out more often, not less. And the benchmark render still peaks at **exactly 0.98**, because that number was never the instrument: crest goes 20.29 → 19.84 and the guard divides by whatever is left.

266. **Item 42's contract does *not* still hold, the part of it that broke is not the master gain, and it is not what the listener heard.** Asked directly, through the finished chain: on `presets/default.toml` the mezzo-forte C4 anchor is **−19.17 dBFS against the contract's −19.5**, which holds; a single fortissimo C4 is **−6.78 against −10**, and the loudest single strike on the instrument (C8 at velocity 127) is **−1.78 dBFS on the mono sum and −4.23 in the channel it is panned into**; the ten-note fortissimo chord is **6 dB past the threshold it is supposed to arrive at**, with 268 samples inside the limiter, which is exactly the state item 42 was written to repair. On `presets/salamander-c5.toml` it is worse and it is specific: **keys 87, 96 and 99 put a *single* fortissimo strike inside the safety limiter** — 17, 2525 and 1487 shaped samples, all three at 0.00 dBFS — and the compass at velocity 127 spans **19.2 dB** with +13.2 dB spikes standing on the fitted keys. **The attribution is clean and it is not the construction:** the *same engine* running the previous revision of that same file puts **no** key inside the limiter and spans 12.7 dB, so what moved is `notes.partial_gains`, refitted at 28 keys in the same commit against the ±26 dB rails item 237 widened it to. `OUTPUT_GAIN` is deliberately **not** moved here. Lowering it would put the chord back under the threshold and leave three treble keys standing 13 dB over their neighbours — item 44's flattened compass is the property that is actually broken — and it would move every render, every fingerprint and the absolute peaks item 259's level-equivalence gate pins against `b929658`. Recalibrating the master level and re-normalising the fitted gains is one milestone and this is not it; what this milestone leaves behind is the measurement, and a phrase-level gate that holds for both presets meanwhile, because at the velocities the benchmark actually plays neither of them reaches the limiter at all.

267. **The C2 that "does not fit the rest" is `notes.partial_gains` with a third of its teeth missing, and the eigensolve at the one-string boundary is innocent.** Reported by ear on `renders/realism/excerpt_engine.wav` — the C-chord bass of the harmonised Ode to Joy, key 36 at velocity 68 — with a seed hypothesis that key 36 sits exactly on the 1→2 unison boundary and that the `N`-transition or a fitted row near it had broken. Half right, and the half that is wrong is settled by the cheapest possible experiment: **clear `partial_gains`, `partial_sigma_scale`, `false_beat` and `comb_floor` at every key and scan the compass again.** On that bare preset key 36 is an outlier on nothing — its `irregular` reads **4.9 dB against its neighbours' 4.4 and 4.2** — so the construction places C2's modes, splits its decay and sets its level exactly like the keys either side of it, and there is no boundary defect to find. On the shipped preset the same key reads **16.0**. The eleven decibels are one table. Measured per partial against the Salamander recording of the same note at the same velocity, over 0.10–1.10 s, with the common offset removed (`tuner/examples/key_probe.rs`, new): the engine's C2 was **+14.0 dB too loud at k=5 and +12.0 at k=6** — 326 and 391 Hz, where a bass note's radiated energy actually is — while its k=1, k=3 and k=4 sat 1.6, 6.1 and 13.4 dB *under*. What came out was a note with two nearly equal peaks at 130 Hz and 326 Hz and thirty decibels of nothing between them, which is a hollow nasal tone and not a bass note, and it is exactly what the listener described. The gain row says why in one line: at k=5 and k=6 it reads **exactly 1.0**, and **fourteen of its forty-eight cells did**, the highest count and the highest fraction of any key on the instrument. Everything else was ruled out by the same subtraction and none of it moved the note: `no false_beat` −0.4 dB of shape error, `no sigma_scale` +0.1, and `no comb_floor` **+1.4 dB worse** — the fitted 0.45 floor is doing real work, and re-fitting the gains against a floor of 0.02 makes the key worse still (5.27 → 6.18), so the floor is exonerated too. The `false_beat` rows at k=7 and k=8 read −4.1 dB, and the engine's beat depths there are **13.16 and 11.40 dB against the recording's 13.12 and 8.41**: that mechanism is right.

268. **A hole in a gain row is not a missing correction, it is a tooth — and a cell its velocity layers disagree about is not the pattern the field is defined as.** Two changes in `estimate::shaping::measured_over_rendered`, both of them the same argument applied twice. **(a) The hole.** `1.0` is the identity, so writing it for a partial the ratio could not be taken at looks like the cautious choice. It is only neutral if the row around it is near 1, and this row spans **39 dB**: a `1.0` between a neighbour at −26 dB and one at +13 is a 26 dB tooth. `DECISIONS.md` 231's own decomposition says what belongs there instead — the log ratio has a **smooth** part in `ln k` (the hammer's tilt, the bridge, the microphone, all of which the engine is wrong about by something continuous) and a **rough** part that is the partial's own defect; a partial nobody measured has no rough part to write and still has the smooth one. So it gets the row's own Huber-weighted degree-2 curve, **interior only** — past the last measured partial there is nothing to interpolate, a degree-2 polynomial in `ln k` extrapolates by tens of decibels, and a row ending in genuine `1.0`s is a row that gets trimmed — and **only across runs of at most two** (`MAX_FILL_RUN`). That last bound is measured, not chosen: filling the long runs as well moved `scale_mf` and `staccato` **up 0.14 and 0.13 dB of log-mel**, because the rows that have long runs are the short treble ones where the curve has few points to stand on and the partials it reaches are the 5 kHz bands those two phrases live in. C2's holes come in ones and twos, so it keeps its whole repair. **(b) The cell.** Item 231's argument for a velocity-*independent* table is that the per-partial pattern is the same in every layer; a cell whose three layers disagree by twenty decibels is not that pattern, it is three noisy numbers, and their median is a noisy number. Each cell is now read as a measurement with an error bar — `variance` is the variance of its own robust median over its layers — and combined with the curve the way two measurements of one thing are: `written = p(k) + w (m_k − p(k))`, `w = t²/(t² + v_k)`, with `t²` the variance the row's departures from the curve have in excess of the cells' own. Nothing is thresholded and no cell is discarded; a cell its layers agree on keeps all of itself. The curve itself is fitted with `1/v_k` as a prior weight (`huber_polyfit`), so a cell nobody agrees about does not bend it either. The gate for both is `applying_the_full_ratio_leaves_nothing_to_fit`, which still passes: layers that agree are not shrunk, so re-entrancy is untouched.

269. **What the two changes did to C2, measured, and what they did to the rest of the compass.** `presets/salamander-c5.toml` re-emitted by `fit_motion --stage partial_gains`, which is the only legitimate way to move that table. C2's holes go **14 → 1** and its 48-entry row keeps its 48 entries. Per partial against the recording, residual after the common offset, before → after: **k=5 +14.0 → −3.2 dB, k=6 +12.0 → −4.2** — the two cells the fit had left at 1.0 come down by twenty and nineteen decibels and land inside four of the piano — while k=1, k=2 and k=4 move −1.6 → +1.7, +6.2 → +9.2 and −13.4 → −10.4. Whole-key **shape error 6.58 → 4.95 dB**, against 3.72 at A1 and 3.59 at D#2 either side of it, and against **6.87 with the row removed entirely** — which is the point that the row was doing nothing for the key before and is doing 1.9 dB for it now. Rendered `irregular` **16.0 → 13.4 dB**. The compass scan's flag on key 36 goes **`irregular` z +6.8 → +5.5** and does **not** clear the line: what is left is not C2's, it is the ±26 dB rails binding on k=3 and k=8 and the fact that `partial_gains` exists at 30 keys and is absent at the other 58, so a fitted key renders 4 dB *more* jagged than the recording and an unfitted one 5 dB *less*. Before the change C2 stood **1.4 z above the next-worst key under C4**; after it stands **0.3 above** and is the fourth of four (36 at 5.5, 21 at 5.2, 60 at 5.1, 24 at 4.7) — an instance of item 266's named milestone rather than a note of its own. **Twenty-eight rows moved, not one**, and the scoreboard is the cost: mel mean **4.28 → 4.27 dB** against the 1.59 floor, with `excerpt` — the phrase the note was heard in — **4.26 → 4.10, the largest single move on the board**, and all three of its windows improving (4.54/4.23/3.99 → 4.34/4.10/3.87). Two phrases rose by 0.04: `chords_pedal` 3.30 → 3.34 and `staccato` 4.56 → 4.60. That 0.04 is above the noise and it was measured to say so — **re-running the fit with the estimator unchanged reproduces the shipped board to ±0.01** (4.32/4.17/3.31/4.56/5.07/4.26 against 4.31/4.18/3.30/4.56/5.08/4.26), so the fit is re-entrant to a hundredth of a decibel and every move quoted here is the change and not the re-run. Everything else improved: attack **4.40 → 4.27**, modulation **3.29 → 3.27**, release **3.31 → 3.29**, mid-band envelope correlation 0.925 → 0.929 (bass 0.880 → 0.880, treble 0.895 → 0.892).

270. **`tuner/examples/compass_scan.rs`, and the four things it finds that nobody reported.** 88 solo notes at velocity 90, seven numbers each, scored against the nearest eight keys **strung the same way** — same-`N` because the 1→2 and 2→3 boundaries are step changes in `beat` and `jitter` that belong to the instrument, and scoring across them spends the flags convicting four keys of being a boundary, which is what the first version of this tool did. `irregular` — the mean absolute step between adjacent partial levels — is the metric that is in no other report and the one that found C2, and it is diagnostic *by construction*: every mechanism in the engine that shapes a spectrum is smooth in `ln k` except the one free-per-partial table, so a jagged series is a fitted defect and the metric points at the table that carries it. `match` is the same distance taken against the recording of the same note instead of against the neighbours. `beat`, `jitter` and `decay` are **not measured at all** below F#2, where a key's partials sit closer together than three widths of the 31.8 Hz band-pass that has to separate them — `motion.rs`'s own header is the authority for that number, and two contaminated readings do not cancel; the first version of this tool flagged seven bass keys on `jitter` at up to z 22 whose recordings read z 90 on the same metric, which is the measurement failing on both sides rather than a defect on one. **38 flags over 23 keys.** Four families, in order of size: **(1) the top four keys stop dead** — A7 through C8 read `decay` −176 to −62 dB/s against a neighbourhood of −20 and `beat` up to 98 dB, where the recordings read −0.7 and 12; the note is culled inside the analysis window and the recording is not. **(2) The fitted keys are level spikes** — C7 stands **+23 dB over its neighbours**, D#7 +16, F#5 +16, D#6 +13.5, F#6 +12.1 and A4 +12.0, every one of them a key with a fitted row and none of them anything the recording does (`recording z` −0.0, −3.3, +0.2, +1.3, +0.0, +0.1). This is item 266's finding arrived at independently and from a different direction: it named keys 87, 96 and 99 from a fortissimo strike reaching the limiter, and the scan finds the same keys from a mezzo-forte one against its neighbours. **(3) The fitted keys are jagged** — A0, C1, C2 and C4 on `irregular`, all of them fitted, none of them matched by the recording (z +0.4, +0.1, +1.4, −0.3). **(4) A brightness step at three keys** — C5 and D#4 read `centroid` 15 and 13 semitones over their neighbours while F#2 and A2 read 12 under, and at D#4 the recording agrees (z +4.4), which is the one case in the four where the piano may simply do it. Families 2 and 3 are the same table and the same milestone; family 1 is the mode cull's audibility floor, which nothing has measured against a recording before.
271. **Verifier corrections to items 263-270's reports** (behavioral claims all verified; these are the reporting errata): key 87 no longer reaches the master limiter after the gains refit (the named-milestone list in item 266 and `limiter.rs` docs is now keys 96/99); item 269's "holes 14 -> 1" at key 36 is actually 14 -> 5 (a run of five at k=41-45 exceeds `MAX_FILL_RUN` and stays, correctly, unfilled); the bass report quoted attack/modulation/release side columns from a stale board; the compass detail pairs for keys 33-39 predate the refit by 70 minutes; the gains fit is only approximately re-entrant (a second pass moves 22 cells by >3 dB — acceptable for a fit run from the survey base per item 214, noted for anyone diffing successive presets); and key 69's extended row carries four exact-1.0 interior cells k=11-14 that the fill rule skips as a long run.

272. **"Geometric mean 1" is not loudness-neutral, and the gap is Jensen's inequality.** Item 231 and every version of `notes.partial_gains` since have normalised the row in the *log* domain — geometric mean one, in the shipped code the median cell unmoved — and called that a guarantee that writing the row cannot move a note's level. A level is a sum of **powers**, and a row of log-gains with mean zero and spread `s` dB multiplies a note's power by about `s^2 ln 10 / 40` dB. Measured on the shipped preset by `tuner/examples/gain_level.rs`, which renders every fitted key twice — once as shipped and once with that key's row emptied — and reports the two numbers a listener has: the **rendered strike peak moved by up to +18.9 dB and the 0.2-2 s RMS by +25.5 dB** (key 96), +18.2 / +25.0 at key 99, +8.9 / +13.2 at 78, +8.1 / +12.2 at 87, +7.0 / +12.3 at 69, +10.3 peak at 48. The row's own log mean at those keys is +8.3, +4.4, +2.6, +0.1, +4.1, −1.1 dB — i.e. the claim was very nearly true as stated and completely wrong about the thing it was made to guarantee. The **power** mean of the same rows is +21.3, +21.3, +12.0, +5.7, +13.0, +12.7 dB, which predicts the rendered move to a couple of decibels. Two aggravations sit on top of the arithmetic: the pin is a **median over the measured cells only**, so a three-cell row (C7, D#7) can be median-pinned and still have a +8.3 dB geometric mean; and the fit was railed against the *schema's* `MIN_PARTIAL_GAIN`/`MAX_PARTIAL_GAIN`, ±26.02 dB, so five keys wrote a cell at exactly the rail and **C7 and D#7 wrote it on their fundamental** — 26 dB on the partial carrying nearly all of the note's power. That is the whole of the compass's family-2 level spikes (item 270) and of item 266's two keys putting a single fortissimo strike inside the master limiter. This entry is the leak; 273 is what replaced it.

273. **The gains fit is now disciplined by three measured things, none of them a constant somebody chose.** `tuner/src/estimate/shaping.rs` and the loop in `tuner/examples/fit_motion.rs`. **(a) The rails are the key's own.** `rail_cells` clips every cell to `3 x 1.4826 MAD` of that key's own cell distribution, floored at 6 dB and capped by the schema — and a row of fewer than five cells does not get to set its own MAD at all, because three numbers one of which is the outlier have a MAD the outlier sets. C7 and D#7 are railed at 6.0 dB where they used to write 26.02; C2 earns 26.02 because its cells really do span it, which is the point of measuring the rail rather than choosing it. **(b) The level is pinned on power, not on logs.** `energy_offset` solves `sum_k (e_k g_k)^2 = sum_k e_k^2` over the partials the row is written for, with `e_k` the engine's own rendered spectrum — the denominator of every cell, so the fit already has it. **(c) The roughness is bisected against the recording's own.** `temper` runs a Whittaker smoother on the row's log cells, weighted by the precision each cell earned from its velocity layers, and bisects its one free number `lambda` per key until the series the row *predicts* has the recording's own mean absolute step between adjacent partials. `lambda = 0` — no smoothing at all — is the answer wherever the raw cells are already no rougher than the piano, because the target is the recording's 5-10 dB of measured scatter and not zero. Then, because a prediction is not a render, **the last word is the render's** (`close_on_the_render`, the pattern of items 137, 199, 211, 264): the row's departures from its own smooth tilt are scaled by a `keep` bisected until the *rendered* `irregular` is half a decibel under the recording of the same note, and the row is then shifted bodily until the rendered 0.10-1.10 s RMS is inside the band the **recording's own compass has** — `LEVEL_BAND_DB = 2.96`, two robust sigmas of the recording's key-to-key `level` residual measured over the 88 keys of `renders/compass/COMPASS.md` (sigma 1.48 dB, p90 3.25, worst 6.40, against the engine's 3.04 and 23.25 before this). `compass_scan`'s spectrum statistic moved into `tuner/src/series.rs` so that the objective and the acceptance test are one implementation. **The level the pin removes is reported and not written.** `notes.bridge_gain` was the candidate and is refused on the measurement: the removed level runs +12.8 dB at F#5, −13.2 at D#4 and +5.2 at C7 with no smooth trend down the compass — a robust polynomial through the *whole* 88-key engine-versus-recording level error has an amplitude of only +4.8 dB at A0 falling to zero by A2 — so what the fit found is its own per-key scatter and not a soundboard, and writing it would hide an estimator artefact inside the table item 44 calibrated by hand. Padding the row out to the key's whole bank with the removed level was also tried and also refused: it makes the row exactly neutral with no step anywhere, and it costs `centroid` — D#4 goes z +7.4 to +9.9 and A1, E3 and F#1 gain flags — because the padding writes a level over partials nothing measured and a power-weighted mean partial index reads that as a colour.

274. **What the disciplined refit did, and the one gate it does not hold.** `presets/salamander-c5.toml` re-emitted by `fit_motion --stage partial_gains` from the shipped preset, 28 of 30 sampled keys fitted as before (A7 and C8 still measure nothing). `renders/compass/COMPASS.md`: **38 flags over 23 keys becomes 29 over 17**, and the two families this milestone is about are gone. **Family 2 (level) is empty** — every one of C7 +7.8, D#7 +5.5, F#5 +5.4, D#6 +4.5, F#6 +4.1 and A4 +4.0 clears, and across the fitted keys the 0.10-1.10 s level residual against the eight nearest same-`N` neighbours falls from **mean 6.34 dB / worst 23.25 to mean 2.70 / worst 6.90**, against the *recording's* own mean 1.52 / worst 6.40. **Family 3 (jaggedness) is empty except F#7.** C4 renders `irregular` **13.9 -> 6.4** against its own recording's 7.0 (the flag was z +5.1); C2 **13.4 -> 9.3** against 9.8 (was +5.5); A0 **13.6 -> 9.1** against 9.6 (was +5.2); C1 **12.8 -> 7.5** against 8.8 (was +4.7). Over the 22 fitted keys A0-C6, where the recording's own `irregular` is a measurement rather than an analysis-floor reading, the distance from a fitted key's render to **its own recording** falls from mean 2.70 dB to **1.65**, with 15 of 22 now inside 1.5 dB against 9 of 22 before. The one survivor is F#7 at z +4.2 with the recording at +2.8, and it is not the row: `keep = 1.0` there and the engine renders 27.5 against the recording's 43.5, so the row was never smoothed and the key is *smoother* than the piano. **The 58 unfitted keys are not extended and cannot be.** All 30 keys the library samples are fitted; the other 58 have no per-partial recording and inventing one is out. Which direction reads truer is now measured rather than argued: a fitted key sits **1.65 dB** from its own recording's roughness and an unfitted one sits **5.8 dB under** it, so the fitted direction is the true one and what is left of the C4-versus-neighbours contrast the listener reported is the *unfitted* keys being 5 dB too smooth — a defect of the excitation, which `shaping.rs`'s own header has convicted since Phase E, and not of the table. **The gate that does not hold is the scoreboard.** `renders/realism/REALISM.md` mean log-mel **4.27 -> 4.70 dB** against the 0.15 allowance, every phrase up. The regression is attributed by two controls, not guessed: rebuilding the same disciplined shapes with the *old* per-key levels put back gives 4.39, and doing it only for keys under 93 gives 4.39 while doing it only for keys 93 and over gives 4.73 — so **0.32 dB of it is the level** the loop refuses to let the row carry, all of it below C7, and **0.12 dB is the roughness** shrinkage, which is inside the allowance. Removing every row instead reads 4.98, so the disciplined rows are still doing 0.28 dB of work. The 0.32 is the price of item 270's own argument, taken seriously: the scoreboard is a mean over the compass, which is the wrong statistic for the defect a listener finds, and the level it wants back is the level that put C7 23 dB over its neighbours and two keys inside the master limiter. What it is really pointing at is a per-key level the recording has and the engine cannot carry anywhere legitimate — 28 of 88 keys is a comb, and `notes.bridge_gain` cannot take it (item 273) — which makes it the same shape of problem as the unfitted keys' roughness and the next thing to work on. **Not moved and worth naming:** the compass's `centroid` family (C5 +6.3, A2 −6.2, F#2 −5.7, A1 −4.0) and the top four keys' cull (family 1) are untouched; and one new reading, C5's **rendered strike peak stands 10.3 dB over its neighbours** while its one-second RMS does not, which the loop's window does not see — probed by flattening its row's roughness entirely, the peak still rises 8.9 dB, so it is item 231's *tilt* half and not the roughness. Re-entrancy (item 214, 222): a second pass over the emitted preset moves 21 of 617 cells by more than 3 dB, worst 11.8 dB, and reproduces every rendered acceptance number to 0.03 dB — the cells wander, the note does not.

275. **Family 1 was not the culling floor, and the constant it was is a threshold on *time* wearing a threshold on level's clothes.** The compass's worst flags by an order of magnitude were keys 104-108 — A7 `decay` z **−116.5** (engine −176.0 dB/s against a neighbourhood of −19.87 and a recording of −0.7), `beat` +86.8, `jitter` +42.4, and the same triple at A#7, B7 and C8 — where the note stops dead while the recording rings. `tuner/examples/top_octave.rs` is the tool that attributes it, mode by mode and control by control. **The suspect was `CULL_AMPLITUDE` and it is innocent.** Measured through the finished chain — the full render's 0.10-1.10 s RMS over the bare string's, so the number is a transfer and not an assumption — the culling floor lands at **−107.8 to −119.7 dBFS at the master over all 88 keys**: 22 dB *under* the −90 dBFS its own docstring claims, and flat to ±6 dB down the compass, which refuses the per-bank version of the constant this was expected to need. It is also never reached: `ModalBank::cull` runs only on a block with no input, so an undamped key whose resonance bus is live is not culled at all, and the amplitude `IDLE_ENERGY` corresponded to (1.11e-6) stood *above* `CULL_AMPLITUDE` (5.86e-7) anyway. **One control run looked like a conviction and was not, which is worth recording.** Setting `CULL_AMPLITUDE` to 1e-12 does clear every family-1 flag — but `CULL_AMPLITUDE` is also what `ResonanceBus::is_active` tests, so lowering it keeps the bus awake, keeps the string driven, and suppresses culling rather than measuring it. Dropping the two thresholds one at a time separates them: `CULL_AMPLITUDE` alone moves nothing, and **`IDLE_ENERGY` alone reproduces the whole effect** (A7 `decay` −176.0 → −21.4, `beat` 97.9 → 14.4, `jitter` 7.77 → 1.52). It is `IDLE_ENERGY` because a bank that reports idle lets `Voice::process` take the branch that writes no samples at all — so that constant is not bookkeeping, it is where a note *ends*. **And the mechanism is timing, not level.** A decaying mode crosses a fixed floor at `ln(a0/floor)/sigma`, so where an absolute floor lands *inside* a note is set by the note's own decay rate: the longest-lived mode of F#7's fundamental crossed at 8.3 s and A7's at 2.2 s, four seconds of swing between neighbours out of the 3x step in fitted `sigma` across F#7→A7 (slowest fundamental mode 0.54, 0.66, 0.95, 1.79, 1.60, 1.69, 1.61 1/s over F#7-C8), and A7's landed inside the 1.5-3.0 s window the compass fits a tail over. The three flags are one event: a step to exact zero has an infinite decay slope, an envelope span equal to the whole dynamic range, and a phase that is noise.

276. **The floor is now set under the reference's own noise floor, and the size of the change is stated because nothing in it can be heard.** `types::IDLE_ENERGY` goes from `(1.0e-5/OUTPUT_GAIN)^2` to `(1.0e-7/OUTPUT_GAIN)^2` — three decades, −100 dBFS nominal to −140, **−142 to −154 at the master** through the measured chain. The number is the reference's and not a choice: over the 88 keys of `data/salamander/SalamanderGrandPiano-V3+20200602.sfz` at velocity 90 the recordings' own noise floor — the quietest 20 ms anywhere in the note — has median −56.4 dBFS and bottoms out at **−109.8** (A7), so a retired bank is at least 32 dB under the quietest thing the reference piano can represent at any key. **Three decades and not four**, which is measured too: at `1.0e-8` every family-1 number is identical, and `the_pan_spread_comes_back_from_the_drift_it_puts_in_the_image` goes red — its drift is read over an **8 s** render, so a C7 that now rings for all of it moves the median drift 3.46 → 3.66 dB and the inverted spread 0.379 → 0.400 against a 0.08 gate. That gate was passing by **0.001** because `estimate::directivity`'s line is itself stale: `tuner/examples/drift_line.rs` measures the engine's slope at ~~**10.97 dB per unit spread against the constant's 8.3**~~ **15.84 dB per unit with the board out of the chain, which is the configuration that number was taken in and is not the one the constant describes; through the finished chain it is 8.00 (item 279)**, and the module's claim to have measured it on `presets/salamander-c5.toml` cannot be right — that preset carries a per-key `notes.pan_spread` table, so sweeping the global parameter on it moves the drift not at all. Re-deriving that line is the directivity estimator's milestone; nothing is bought past `1.0e-7` and something is spent, so the floor stops there. **Result on the compass: 29 flags over 17 keys becomes 15 over 12, and family 1 is empty.** Keys 104-108 go from worst `z` 14.8 / 116.5 / 104.0 / 113.1 / 31.2 to **3.5 / 3.3 / 2.3 / 2.5 / 2.0**, with A7's `decay` −176.0 → −21.4 against a neighbourhood of −19.9, its `beat` 97.9 → 14.4 against 11.9 and its `jitter` 7.77 → 1.52 against 2.40 on the recording. G7's `decay` flag falls +5.4 → +4.4 and F7's rises +4.5 → +5.3: both survive, and the rise is the scan working — with 104-108's absurd values out of the distribution the robust sigma shrinks, so what is left of F7 is measured against real scatter. Those two are the same fitted-`sigma` step named in 275 seen from the other side (F7 and G7 render −12.9 and −13.2 dB/s where their neighbours run −18.9 and −18.1), which is the tuner's, not the engine's. **Cost, all measured.** Performance: the four acceptance worst cases read **29.4 / 30.3 / 29.5 / 29.4 %** of one core against 29.7 / 30.3 / 29.5 / 29.4 before, i.e. inside the noise, because the worst case is a keyboard whose voices are all live anyway; one held note goes 2.66 → 2.80 % without segments and 3.60 → 3.77 % with them. Idle silence is untouched: the branch that keeps 88 idle voices bit-exact silent is entered on `strings_idle() && !bus.is_active()` exactly as before, and `an_idle_engine_renders_exact_silence` and `an_idle_voice_writes_no_samples_at_all` are green. The sounding-path fingerprint moves `61e29d4abaa316f9` → **`11a08741631adb99`**, and what is held instead is the size of the move: **−115.0 dB RMS** on the ten-strike probe against a render whose own RMS is −40.4, largest single-sample difference **2.26e-5 (−92.9 dBFS)**. **What did not change is how long a note sounds**: the engine's own −60 dB point in the top octave is 1.26 / 1.54 / 1.48 / 1.82 s at A7-C8 before and after, against the recordings' 3.60 / 3.40 / 3.72 / 3.50 — so family 1 was never audible, and the top octave being genuinely short by a factor of two is a *decay-rate* defect the fit owns and this does not touch. New: `the_top_octave_does_not_stop_dead_inside_the_note` in `engine/tests/acceptance.rs`, which gates the fall between 2.0 s and 3.0 s at 60 dB/s on `presets/salamander-c5.toml` (A7 reads 178.2 before, 15.4 after, worst legitimate key 17.6) and `ModalBank::mode_amplitude` / `PianoString::partial_amplitudes`, so a threshold on a mode's state can be checked against the state and not a proxy. **The one red gate moved and is still red, and its diagnosis in item 260 was wrong in the same way 275 is about.** `a_known_duplex_comes_back_from_the_engines_own_render_of_it` blamed `ModalBank::cull` for zeroing the halo; it was `IDLE_ENERGY` silencing the voice. The segment now **comes back**, at 2719.4 Hz against the 2719.3 asked for (**+0.08 cents**, −105.4 dB re strike), where items 232, 247, 256 and 260 all recorded "nothing came back at all / 0 modes". The test now fails one assertion later, on the level being linear in `gain_db` over a −26..−8 dB sweep, which is item 260's real defect — a segment normalised for a steady drive and only ever given an impulsive one — untouched and unchanged. `cargo test --release --workspace`: **477 green, one red, the same named one.** Clippy: 0 diagnostics.

277. **Item 42's calibration re-run as a procedure, and the overshoot it was written against is 5.19 dB and not 6.** `types::OUTPUT_GAIN` **9.0 -> 4.95**, measured through the finished chain by `tuner/examples/output_gain.rs`. **The mono/channel confusion is the first finding.** `soundboard::soft_clip` is applied to each channel on its own, so `LIMIT_THRESHOLD` is a per-channel number; item 42 reported its four anchors as *mono sums* and compared the chord's against it, and item 266 read "6 dB past the threshold" off a mono sum of two channels **both saturated near 1.0**, which is +6.02 dBFS whatever the drive was. The drive is recoverable exactly and without arithmetic: everything between the voices and `soft_clip` is linear, so lower the gain until the chord clears the limiter and the render *is* the pre-limiter signal. At `OUTPUT_GAIN` 3.0 the chord peaks at -5.37 dBFS in its channel with no sample shaped, which puts the answer at `3.0 * LIMIT_THRESHOLD / 10^(-5.37/20)` = **4.9604**; 4.95 is that rounded down, and re-running the procedure at 4.95 returns 4.9604 again, so it is a fixed point and not an iteration. **All six clauses, on `presets/default.toml`, every one with zero samples inside the limiter:** mezzo-forte C4 (vel 80) **-24.36 dBFS mono / -28.98 channel**; a single fortissimo C4 **-11.98 / -16.66**; the loudest single strike anywhere on the instrument, which is still C8 at velocity 127 as item 266 measured, **-6.97 / -9.42**; the **ten-note fortissimo chord -1.02 in the channel, 0.02 dB under the threshold it is supposed to arrive at**; the built-in demo **-7.67 dBFS peak**; and 30 s of dense random playing **-9.94 dBFS peak with the worst channel's DC at -176.7**. On `presets/salamander-c5.toml`, which is not what the gain is calibrated against and is the looser of the two: **-26.88 / -14.19 / -2.43 / -1.43 channel (0.43 dB of headroom) / -6.59 / -8.90**. Item 266 declined to move this constant on the grounds that lowering it "would put the chord back under the threshold and leave three treble keys standing 13 dB over their neighbours" — the disciplined refit (273-274) removed those keys first, which is why this is the right order and this is the right milestone for it.

278. **The two end-of-note floors stop following the master gain, because the first thing the recalibration did was retire a mechanism.** Item 42 expressed `CULL_AMPLITUDE` and `IDLE_ENERGY` in terms of `OUTPUT_GAIN` so that a level change would carry them along, and what that rule actually means is that the floors sit at a fixed **dBFS**: lower the master gain and the whole instrument moves down toward them. Run literally, `OUTPUT_GAIN` 4.95 takes **nine tests red**, and the informative ones are not the level pins — `a_silently_held_key_answers_a_struck_note_with_the_pedal_up`, which is `PHYSICS.md` §6's first acceptance test, reads the silently prepared C3's energy as **exactly zero**, because `CULL_AMPLITUDE`'s own docstring records that mechanism living at 3.2e-11 of energy and 5.19 dB of extra floor is more than it has. So `types::FLOOR_REFERENCE_GAIN` freezes both at item 42's 9.0. The justification is items 275-276's own: these are thresholds on the *string's state* — when a mode is not worth integrating, when a note has ended — and those are properties of the sounding path rather than of where full scale happens to be. **The payoff is that the recalibration becomes provably a level change and nothing else**, and it is measured rather than asserted: over the six benchmark phrases, undoing the 0.55 leaves a residual **121.6 to 126.4 dB under each render's own peak** (RMS -159 to -169 dBFS), and on the ten-strike probe of `the_sounding_path_is_what_it_was_before_the_mechanism` **-167.9 dBFS RMS, 122.3 dB under the signal**, largest single sample 4.5e-8. That is f32 rounding in the DC blocker's recursion. With the floors frozen, the nine reds become three, and all three are pins on absolute level that a deliberate level change is *supposed* to move: the sounding-path fingerprint `11a08741631adb99` -> **`b77c69714fdb6f21`**; `calibrate`'s strike window -26..-12 dBFS -> **-31.2..-17.2**; and `the_strike_lands_at_the_level_it_always_did`, where the eighteen `b929658` numbers are **not rewritten** but carried through one named term, `MASTER_GAIN_DB = -5.1927`, and every one of the eighteen keeps the departure it had to a hundredth of a decibel (worst 2.99 dB at C8 vel 90 against its 3.1 budget, 1.01 at C7 against 1.1, 0.49 over all of A0-C6 against 0.5). The measured master-referred culling floor follows the instrument down: **-113.0 to -124.9 dBFS** over the 88 keys where item 275 read -107.8 to -119.7.

279. **The pan-spread gates were not level-sensitive, they were measuring the constant on an instrument the constant was not taken on, and the master gain is what finally tipped them.** `the_pan_spread_comes_back_from_the_drift_it_puts_in_the_image` had been passing by **0.001** (a spread of 0.30 recovered as 0.379 against a 0.08 tolerance) and item 276 named re-deriving `estimate::directivity`'s line as that estimator's own milestone; the recalibration moved the median drift 3.46 -> 3.57 dB and the recovery to 0.391, and `a_known_per_key_spread_comes_back_key_by_key` went 0.302 -> 0.338 against a tolerance widened to 0.12 for it. **Three things were wrong and none of them is the level.** (a) `DRIFT_PER_SPREAD_DB` and `DRIFT_AT_ZERO_DB` claimed to be measured on `presets/salamander-c5.toml`, which is impossible: that preset carries a per-key `notes.pan_spread` table that overrides the global parameter, so `tuner/examples/drift_line.rs` sweeps it and gets **slope 0.000 at every spread**. (b) Both gates rendered at `board_mix = 0`, and the diffuse path is half of this mechanism — it is not panned at all and compresses every balance toward the middle, which the module's own header says and nobody had measured. Dry, the sweep runs **15.84 dB per unit** over the eight keys the constants are documented on; with the board as the preset ships it, **8.00, intercept 0.68**, a straight line to within 0.170 dB. (c) The first gate then checked the constant over **six** keys rather than the documented eight, dropping A0 and A1 on the grounds that a note panned that far left has nowhere to move — which is true and is exactly why they belong, since they are in the line and pull its slope from 8.76 to 8.00. Fixed at all three: the constants become **8.0 and 0.68**, both gates render through the finished chain, and the first uses the constants' own eight keys. A spread of 0.15 now comes back as **0.155** and 0.30 as **0.279** (was 0.163 and 0.379), and the per-key lines return **0.109 / 0.296 / 0.234** against 0.100 / 0.300 / 0.200 — worst error 0.034, so that gate's tolerance goes **back to the 0.08** it had before the coupled construction. The diagnosis on the record for its widening — that the two-point `KeyDriftLine` is no longer straight in the spread and needs three probe points — is wrong: it is straight, and the board is what straightens it. Only `voicing.polarization_pan_spread` in a newly fitted preset reads these two constants (`main.rs`); the shipped per-key table comes from `pan_spread_table`'s measured per-key lines and is untouched.

280. **Score and close: what the three changes did to the compass, and the one gate that still does not hold.** `renders/compass/COMPASS.md` rescanned on the finished engine, all 88 keys, and the two runs of it here agree flag for flag. **38 flags over 23 keys at the head of this milestone becomes 16 over 13.** Families 1, 2 and 3 are closed. **Family 2 (level) is empty** — no `level` flag anywhere on the compass. **Family 3 (jaggedness): one survivor, F#7 `irregular` +4.2 against the recording's +2.8, and it is the key being *smoother* than the piano** (27.5 rendered against 43.5 recorded), which is the opposite defect. **Family 1 is empty**: keys 104-108 now read worst-`z` 3.5 / 3.3 / 2.3 / 2.5 / 2.0 where they read -116.5 / +86.8 / +42.4 and worse, and the gate under them, `the_top_octave_does_not_stop_dead_inside_the_note`, measures the 2.0-to-3.0 s fall at **8.3 to 17.6 dB/s across the top eight keys** against a 60 dB/s bar. **The key the listener reported, C4 solo against 57 / 62 / 64 and against its own recording:** `irregular` **6.4 dB engine against 7.0 recorded**, where its three neighbours render 3.8 / 6.7 / 5.4 against 4.3 / 12.6 / 11.4 — so C4 stands **+1.1 dB over the neighbourhood mean where the recording has it 2.4 dB under**, and before the refit it stood **+8.6 over**. That contrast is what "sounds different from the rest of the melodic notes" was. Its `level` is -45.1 dBFS against a neighbourhood mean of -43.6, 1.5 dB under, and its `match` — distance from its own recording, common offset removed — is **5.0 dB, the best of the four** (4.5 / 9.3 / 10.0). Key 60's worst score on any of the seven metrics is `beat` **2.3**; key 36's is `irregular` **3.7**. **The sixteen that remain, all quoted, none of them family 1-3:** `centroid` at 72 C5 +6.3 (recording +3.0), 45 A2 -6.2 (+0.0), 42 F#2 -5.7 (-3.4), 33 A1 -4.0 (-0.0); `jitter` at 72 +5.8 (+1.8), 96 C7 +5.3 (+0.5), 45 +5.3 (+2.0), 95 B6 +4.6 (-1.9), 76 E5 -4.4 (-2.4), 78 F#5 +4.2 (+0.0); `beat` at 48 C3 +5.6 (+0.2), 53 F3 -4.1 (-2.8); `decay` at 101 F7 +5.3 (-1.1), 103 G7 +4.4 (-0.2); `match` at 95 +5.1 (+0.0); `irregular` at 102 F#7 +4.2 (+2.8). Thirteen keys, four families, and every one of them was on the board before this milestone started. **Standing gates, all re-run:** suite **497 green, 1 red — the named duplex one, `DECISIONS.md` 260, unchanged**; clippy 0 diagnostics; motion columns **A1 1.375 / A2 0.866 / B1 1.643 dB / B2 1.050** against gates 2.0 / 0.5 / 3 / 0.25; limiter-budget tests green with the single-note gate now covering **both** presets; equivalence and level pins green through item 278's one term; release-click, stability fuzz, determinism and idle-silence green; performance **29.0 / 29.9 / 29.4 / 29.1 %** of one core against a 50 % design goal, one held note 2.81 % bare and 3.79 % with segments. **The gate that does not hold is the one item 274 left:** `renders/realism/REALISM.md` mean log-mel **4.70 dB** against the 0.15 allowance over 4.27, and the recalibration cannot move it by construction — `realism::level_match` scales each pair to a common RMS, so a pure master-gain change is invisible to every column on that board, and all six phrases reproduce their previous numbers to a hundredth (4.46 / 5.00 / 3.51 / 5.07 / 5.85 / 4.29). It stays where item 274 attributed it: 0.32 dB of per-key level the recording has that the engine cannot legitimately carry anywhere, all of it above key 93.

281. **Verifier corrections to items 272-280's reports.** Behavioural claims all reproduce; these are the reporting errata. (a) The compass reads **16 flags over 13 keys**, not the 15 over 12 item 276's report quoted: `beat` at 53 F3 sits at **-4.1** and was quoted at -4.0, which is the line itself, and 48 C3 reads 5.6 rather than 5.5 and 96 C7 5.3 rather than 5.2 — the scan is deterministic and two full runs here agree flag for flag, so the difference is which side of `|z| = 4.0` a borderline key was rounded onto. (b) Item 276's "the engine's measured slope is 10.97 dB per unit against the constant's 8.3" was measured with the soundboard's diffuse path removed and is corrected in 279: dry it is 15.84 over the constants' eight keys and 12.21 over the six the gate used, and through the finished chain it is 8.00. (c) Item 266's "the ten-note fortissimo chord is 6 dB past the threshold" is the saturated mono sum and the real overshoot is **5.19 dB** (277); the same item's "keys 87, 96 and 99 put a single fortissimo strike inside the safety limiter" now holds for **no key of either preset**, and the loudest single strike of `presets/salamander-c5.toml` is no longer in the treble at all — it is **C5**, which is item 274's own last finding (C5's strike peak standing 10.3 dB over its neighbours, the tilt half of item 231) seen from the level side. (d) Item 271's note that the compass detail pairs for keys 33-39 predate the refit is cleared: `renders/compass/detail/` is regenerated for 33, 35, 36, 37, 39, 60 and 104-108 in one scan. (e) Motion columns quoted as 1.38 / 0.87 / 1.64 / 1.052 read **1.375 / 0.866 / 1.643 / 1.050** on the finished engine — the same numbers, and the B2 hundredth is the one place a pure scale is visible at all, through a median over cells. (f) Re-rendered at the new master gain: `renders/demo.wav`, `renders/scale.wav`, `renders/salamander-ab/`, `renders/realism/` and `renders/compass/detail/` — the four sets this milestone is scored on. **Deliberately not re-rendered and therefore 5.19 dB louder than the engine now is: `renders/analysis/`, `renders/jitter/` and `renders/timbre-ladder/`, 200 files.** They are the working output of `audit_render`, `jitter_forensics` and `timbre_ladder`, each of which writes its audio and its measurements in one pass, so regenerating the audio alone would leave the reports beside it describing a different render. They are stale by a constant and are named here rather than quietly refreshed; whichever milestone next runs those tools re-levels them.

282. **The red mel gate is confirmed, the fix for it is refused on measurement, and items 274 and 280 attributed it to the wrong mechanism.** `renders/realism/REALISM.md` reproduces independently at mean log-mel **4.70 dB** against the 4.27 + 0.15 = 4.42 allowance, same board to the hundredth (4.46 / 5.00 / 3.51 / 5.07 / 5.85 / 4.29). The gate is genuinely red and nothing about it is hidden. What is wrong is the root cause both closing items give it — "0.32 dB of per-key level the render loop refuses to carry", 274 placing it below key 93 and 280 above it, which cannot both be true and turn out to be neither. **`LEVEL_BAND_DB` is worth nothing on the scoreboard.** The `partial_gains` stage was re-emitted at three settings of it and both boards scored on each: at **0.0** — the row forbidden to move the key's level at all — mel **4.70**, compass 16 flags over 13 keys; at the shipped **2.96**, mel **4.70**, 16 over 13; at **1e9**, no bound at all, mel **4.73**, 16 over 13. No `level` flag appears on the compass at any of the three. So the band is 0.00 dB against pinning it to zero and +0.03 dB against lifting it, and 274's control — "the *old* per-key levels put back" — was not this loop at all. **The 0.32 dB is `estimate::shaping::energy_offset`, which is item 273(b) and the fix for item 272's Jensen leak.** Putting that scalar back into every row, with the band lifted so nothing claws it back, reads mel **4.45** — and that is the whole of it: **giving the gate exactly what it is asking for still does not clear 4.42**. The remaining 0.18 is 274's own 0.12 of roughness shrinkage plus the own-MAD rails.
