(4) The material is 30 keys × 4 velocities (40, 70, 100, 120) = **120 matched pairs**, each pair's whole 3-second long-term average spectrum, each pair level-matched on its own broadband sum first so that nothing of the 13-22 dB level error (`COMPASS.md`'s `level e/r`) or of `OUTPUT_GAIN` can land in a curve. Warm runtime **27 s**, and the whole thing is **byte-identical run to run**, wavs and report.

312. **The curve exists, replicates in shape, and buys nothing on the keys it did not see.** `chain::fit_eq`: [`EQ_BANDS`] = 40 bands log-spaced 40 Hz-16 kHz (`TUNING.md`'s "~40-band", 0.215 octave each), each band's reading the **median** over pairs of what the recording has there that the render does not, refusing any band inside `BAND_FLOOR_DB = −70` of that note's own loudest band on either side, then **cepstrally smoothed to 12 of 40 DCT coefficients** so the curve can carry a microphone's presence peak and cannot carry a partial series — the fastest ripple 12 coefficients allow has a period of about one octave. **Split-half agreement: mean |Δ| 1.34 dB, r +0.931.** So a static transfer of that *shape* is real and is shared by two halves of the compass that share nothing else. **What it is worth is the other half of the sentence: applied to the 15 keys it never saw, it moves the median per-band |engine − reference| from 4.96 dB to 5.00** — nothing, and on its own training half only 4.92 to 4.56. A curve removes what every key shares; what is left is each key's own partials sitting wrong *relative to each other*, which is a spectrum and not a filter. **The soft and loud halves disagree more than the two key halves do — 2.18 dB and r +0.698 against 1.34 and +0.931 — which is the wrong way round for a microphone** and is the first of three signs that part of what this curve absorbs is the excitation model. One construction rule was found the hard way and is load-bearing: **outside the range the material could read (bands 1..37, 50 Hz-11 kHz) the curve is held flat**, because a truncated DCT overshoots at an edge and the top bands are exactly where a note has least energy — three readings at 15 kHz were being continued into a −19 dB shelf that nothing had measured.

313. **The oracle, and it is the number that settles the milestone.** A curve fitted on separate material can always be accused of being fitted badly. So the same question is asked of a filter that cannot be: for each of the 64 mel bands, the **median of the very cells it is scored on**, subtracted from those cells — one curve for all six phrases, fitted on the answer, unbuildable by anyone. It is an **upper bound on every static magnitude chain there is**, this one included, and it moves the mean log-mel distance at window 1024 from **5.03 dB to 4.81**. A filter allowed to change with every phrase — a stronger oracle still, and not a chain at all — reaches 4.54. **So the whole prize available to a static spectral transfer is 0.22 dB of a 3.40 dB excess over the reference's own velocity-layer floor, about 6 %, and 14 % if it may be per-phrase.** The honestly-fitted curve takes 0.26 dB, which is *more* than the global oracle, and the reason is instructive rather than a paradox: a real filter also moves the metric's own −80 dB floor, and cutting 14 dB above 6 kHz puts cells on both sides of the comparison onto that floor where they read zero. The bound is the 0.22.

314. **The largest measured difference in the experiment is one nothing on the scoreboard scores, and it is a microphone rather than a room.** `chain::stereo_signature`, interchannel cross-correlation against lag, per octave band, over half A's 15 keys at velocity 90. **The recording:** r at lag 0 is **0.945 below 125 Hz** and then **−0.021 / −0.270 / −0.081 / −0.021 / +0.041** through 12 kHz, while the **peak |r| over ±5 ms stays 0.57-0.65** everywhere at lags of −0.23 to +1.98 ms. That is a spaced pair: two microphones well inside a wavelength of each other in the bass see one wavefront, and above it they see the same sound about 60 % coherent at a sub-millisecond delay. **The engine is the reverse in every band** — −0.577 in the bass, where the board FDN's two orthogonal-sign taps decorrelate it, rising to **0.964 at 6-12 kHz**, which is a pan-pot. **Every metric in `REALISM.md` is computed on the mono sum and is blind to all of it**, deliberately (its own header says a stereo distance would mostly measure the recording's mic pair), and the consequence is that this column of the gap has never had a number under it until now.

315. **What the impulsive material says about a room is that it is not measuring one, and both refusals are findings.** The library's key-off and pedal recordings are the only impulsive events in it; onset-trimmed and Schroeder-integrated they give broadband **EDT 1.117 s, T20 0.485 s**, and per band **0.518 / 0.477 / 0.435 / 0.407 / 0.503 / 0.625 s** from 63 Hz to 12 kHz. **That table refutes itself: air absorption makes every real space's decay shorter with frequency and this one gets longer**, 0.407 s at 500 Hz-2 kHz against 0.625 s at 6-12 kHz, so what is being integrated is the mechanical event's own body and not the space it happened in. Read as a **bound** it still says the room's T60 is at most about half a second; read as a measurement it is refused. **The reflection finder agrees and is refused for the same reason**: its six strongest clustered candidates run from −38.4 dB at 10.5 ms to −22.1 dB at 57.1 ms, i.e. they get **louder by 16 dB over 47 ms**, and a sequence of early reflections gets quieter. They are the damper landing and the tray settling, **so the stage carries no reflections at all**. What is left is a diffuse decorrelated tail — independent noise per channel, the bound above as its decay, a **stated** 10 ms onset (items 2 of the module's identifiability list: every sample is trimmed to its own onset, so a pre-delay is not recoverable, and 10 ms of a 0.6 s tail is 2 % of its energy and none of its spectrum) — and its **level is fitted to the one thing this material does measure about a late field**, the recording's own interchannel coherence, on half A's notes and never on a phrase. The sweep has an interior minimum, not a rail: mismatch **0.233 bare, 0.237 / 0.241 / 0.243 / 0.225 / 0.181 / 0.167 / 0.187 / 0.210 / 0.264** at −30 to +6 dB, best at **−6 dB**, and through the whole chain **0.233 → 0.161**. It closes a third of the one gap it addresses and cannot close more, because the recording's decorrelation is a **pair of microphones** (§8) and not a reverberant field (§9).

316. **The collapse, on held-out phrases, and it is small.** Mean log-mel over the six benchmark phrases, engine against reference, before and after: **4.99 → 4.73 (EQ) → 4.84 (EQ + room)** against the reference's own velocity-layer floor of **1.59**. Per phrase, engine / +EQ / +chain: `scale_mf` 4.95 / 4.53 / 4.67, `arpeggio_dynamics` 5.20 / 5.08 / 5.28, `chords_pedal` 4.19 / 3.85 / 3.94, `staccato` 4.90 / 4.76 / 5.00, `alberti_fast` 6.13 / 5.95 / 5.82, `excerpt` 4.60 / 4.19 / 4.34; the melody line (`MELODY.md`'s soprano, reported beside the six and not averaged into them) 5.43 / 5.09 / 5.32. **The EQ absorbs 8 % of the 3.40 dB excess and the whole chain 5 %** — the room stage gives back about a tenth of a dB, because a decorrelated tail added to a signal that already carries a board field puts energy where the reference has none and the mono sum is where the metric reads it. `modulation` 2.95 → 2.82 → 2.91 against a floor of 0.96, which a **linear stage cannot move by construction**: no filter changes how a partial's level moves. **And the third sign that the curve is carrying instrument error rather than a chain is in the brilliance columns, which split.** The band the listening note was about gets much better — 2-6 kHz over the six phrases reads **−5.40 → −1.61** on `scale_mf`, **−6.84 → −2.85** on `chords_pedal`, **−9.72 → −6.01** on `excerpt` and −9.82 → −6.11 on the melody line, against floors of 0.09-1.04 — which is the largest single thing the curve buys anywhere and is *why* it takes 8 % of the mel. But the mean absolute 6-12 kHz ratio goes **4.90 → 5.30 dB** and the 500 Hz-8 kHz tilt **1.45 → 2.51 dB/octave**, both *worse*. The note-median curve cuts hard above 6 kHz because the **top-octave keys** are where the engine is brightest there (item 292's +15.16 dB), and a phrase that never goes above C6 then gets a cut it did not earn. A chain is one curve for all 88 keys; a correction that has to be register-dependent to be right is not a chain, and this one does.

317. **The verdict: no `[chain]` preset section, and the next presentation milestone is `PHYSICS.md` §8 rather than §9.** Under a dB of mel — bought by a curve whose own split-half test says its shape is real, whose held-out per-band test says its size is nothing, whose level split says it moves with velocity, and whose top band makes the phrases worse — is not a stage to productize. It is a place for instrument error to hide, which is precisely what §8 warns of when it says splitting the absorber into *mic geometry* plus *room* is strictly better than an anonymous EQ, because geometry has priors and an EQ has none. **What to do instead, in order.** (a) **Give the loss a stereo term first.** The largest measured difference in this experiment is invisible to every column of `REALISM.md`, and a stage built to fix something nothing scores is a stage nobody can regress. (b) **Then build §8, two virtual microphone positions with a per-key delay and gain**, which is the mechanism the interchannel table actually points at and which has a prior; §9's room is not measurable from this material at all and its own absence is now measured rather than assumed. (c) **The static transfer stays a diagnostic**, re-runnable from `chain_fit`, and if stage 2's CMA-ES ever wants an absorber it should be given the geometry and not the anonymous curve. **What remains, ranked:** (1) the per-key, per-partial spectral scatter, 4.96 dB of median band error that no global curve touches — `COMPASS.md`'s `match` and the fitted-against-drawn seam, and the biggest single thing left; (2) the modulation column, 2.95 against a 0.96 floor, which no linear stage can move; (3) the stereo presentation — large, measured, unscored; (4) the static spectral transfer itself — real, replicable in shape, worth under a dB.

318. **The gates, re-run rather than assumed.** `cargo test --release --workspace --no-fail-fast` is **562 green, 2 red** — the documented duplex (item 260) and the documented melody wobble (item 298) — against item 310's 549 and 2; the thirteen new ones are `estimate::chain`'s, and they are the estimator's own contracts rather than the experiment's: the band grid is log-spaced and covers what it claims; cepstral smoothing keeps a one-octave bump inside 1 dB and cuts a band-to-band comb to under 2 dB rms while leaving white scatter at the theoretical `sqrt(12/40)`; the FIR realises the curve it was given inside 1 dB and moves an impulse by exactly zero samples; a fit **recovers a transfer it was given** from twenty different random spectra and **carries no part of the level** (the same material with the engine 20 dB quiet fits the same curve, and a pure gain fits a flat one); the curve is held flat outside what the material could read; a pan-pot reads 1.000 at lag zero and a 2 ms shift reads its own shift; a known 0.4 s exponential comes back as T20 0.4 s; the room stage keeps the direct sound in sample zero and decorrelates a mono pair; the reflection finder finds arrivals it is given; and two curves that agree read as agreeing. `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets` is clean with and without `--features piano-tuner/diagnostics`. **Nothing in `presets/`, `engine/` or any standing render moved**: this milestone's whole output is `tuner/src/estimate/chain.rs`, `tuner/examples/chain_fit.rs`, one line of `tuner/src/estimate/mod.rs`, one `[[example]]` block in `tuner/Cargo.toml`, and the new `renders/chain/` — `CHAIN.md` and four `<name>_{engine,engine_chained,reference}.wav` triples (`ode_soprano`, `excerpt`, `bass_A1`, `treble_C6`), each scaled to the benchmark's own target RMS so the three are level-matched. Every standing gate is therefore where item 310 left it, unmeasured here on purpose: a milestone that changes no preset cannot move a board.

319. **The band statistic the milestone is gated on had no floor discipline, and above 6 kHz that is most of what it was reporting.** `estimate::brilliance::band_decay` and `GapBound`. Items 89, 293 and 304 put one rule on every level this project reads — nothing is a measurement within `FLOOR_MARGIN_DB` of the signal's own floor — and item 304 put it on every *partial* while leaving `band_decay_gap`, the column item 308's table is made of, taking two spectra and no floors at all. Two spectra cannot say what is under them. `band_decay` takes each signal's own late-time floor beside its two instants (the median of the frames from `FLOOR_FROM_S` on, per bin) and returns the same number with the **direction it is bounded in**: a late reading inside its own floor reads the floor, which is higher than the band really is, so that side's drop is under-stated — under-stating the reference's raises the gap and under-stating the engine's lowers it, which makes one saturated side a one-sided bound (`AtLeast`, `AtMost`) and two of them nothing at all. **What that says about item 308's table.** In 6-12 kHz the *reference* stands **0.7 / 7.1 / 6.8 / 0.7 dB** over the recording's own floor at 1.0 s per register while the engine stands 11.5 / 27.9 / 38.9 / 42.1 dB over its own, so all four of that table's 6-12 kHz cells are lower bounds and are now printed with a `≥`. The tenor's +5.46 is a floor on the residual, not its size; the top octave's −4.78 is not an overshoot in either direction. The same is true one level down, of the per-partial ratios: over the whole compass 76-90 % of the trusted cells in that band have a reference late reading that is a bound, and restricting the median to the cells whose late readings are **both** measurements moves it from 0.68 to **1.06** in the tenor (n 43), 0.70 to **0.99** in the treble (n 18) and 0.63 to **0.95** in the top octave (n 8) — no demonstrable overshoot anywhere, where the bounded reading looked like the engine running 1.4-3.3 times too fast. `tail_reach` now prints both rows and the audit is read off the second, and the `brilliance` example's own decay table — item 292's third audit column, the one this line of work was opened on — carries the same marks and reproduces `tail_reach`'s four register medians to the digit, which is two tools measuring one statistic and agreeing. **2-6 kHz is not affected and none of its conclusions move**: its reference side stands 13.9-29.2 dB over the same floor, and it is a measurement. **The one place the missing discipline had reached the preset is the stop, and it made it a ratchet.** `BandFall::partial_median_ratio_error`, the standard error of the median of the per-partial ratios (`1.2533 sigma / sqrt(n)` with `sigma` from the MAD of the logs), and the stop is now "over one by more than its own error" rather than "over one". Without it a converged band reads 1.02 or 1.03 from noise alone and every pass multiplies that in: eight further passes over the finished preset of item 307 took the sampled tenor keys' 6-12 kHz band decay from −2.10 to −4.60 dB **with their measured partials unmoved**, which is also why item 307's re-entrancy check could only claim 0.2 dB and "the cells wandering". With the error in, the stage is a fixed point in the strict sense: a second run over its own output reproduces the same 61 rows, the same 37 drawn keys and **every cell to the last digit**.

320. **The drawn decay rows did not land where the fitted ones did, and four separate things were wrong with the draw.** Measured on item 307's own artifact, per register, whole-band decay gap over the sampled keys against the drawn ones: tenor 6-12 kHz **−2.10 (n 8) against +9.03 (n 16)**, treble −2.90 against +2.60, top −0.45 against −8.85; and on the partials, where it is a measurement rather than a bound (item 319), the median `fall(recording)/fall(engine)` over the cells both sides measured: bass 2-6 kHz **1.05 fitted against 0.72 drawn**, tenor 6-12 kHz 1.06 against 1.32, treble 6-12 kHz 0.79 against 1.64, top 2-6 kHz 1.68 against 1.11. A drawn key and the sampled key next to it were not the same instrument. **(a) The stop was a different statistic on the two sides.** A sampled key stops on the median of its partials' own ratios; a drawn key stopped on its drawn *band* target over the engine's *median* partial fall, and a band's sum always falls less than the median of its own terms, so every drawn key stopped early. `DecayModel` now draws the recording's median partial fall as well as its band sum, and the substitution is measured rather than assumed: on the sampled keys, where both exist, the ratio of the two medians reproduces the median of the ratios to **×1.000 over 38 bands**, and the denominators agree to ×1.000 as well. **(b) The reach was drawn as a partial index.** How far up a key a recording can be read is `min(bank, ceiling / f0)`, and both factors are known for any key: what is not known is the ceiling, which is a property of the recording chain's noise floor against a piano's spectrum and is therefore a **frequency**. Item 307 fitted a log-line in the index instead, which is the same quantity divided by an `f0` spanning seven octaves, and it under-predicted the middle of the compass by half an octave — the drawn tenor rows stopped near k = 27, about 7 kHz at C4, in the middle of the band they were being corrected for, while the fitted rows reached k = 41. `tail::Ceiling` is now one number, **11 286 Hz, ×1.22 over the 30 sampled keys**, carried down each key's own series by `reach_to`; leave-one-out against each sampled key's own measured reach, the index line is out by ×1.20 at the median key and this by **×1.07**. It is deliberately **not** drawn with its scatter: where one recording ran out is a fact about the recording, and drawing it would put the edge at which a row stops correcting in a different place on every neighbouring key, which is the seam this item is about. **(c) The population carried a band no key owned.** `bank_owns_band`: C2's series ends at 6.6 kHz, its three top partials fell **2.5 dB** over the interval where every key from C3 up falls 18-45, and that one point was in the population every unsampled key took its 6-12 kHz target from — it alone took the target at C4 from 15.2 dB to 12.6 and the population's scatter from ×1.36 to ×1.52. A key whose bank ends inside a band is refused **as a population point** and still corrected like any other, because its partials are still its partials and `partial_band_fall` reads nothing else. **(d) The scatter was drawn, and a decay rate is not an idiosyncrasy.** This is the one that carried the rest. A target drawn from a distribution of scatter ×1.31-1.45 lands a *random* factor from what the sampled key next door measured, the fit then closes each key on its own target, and both keys reach their own fixed points a factor apart: A4's 6-12 kHz cells at the schema's rail with A#4's at 1.26, and the tenor's drawn rows a factor of 1.5 under its fitted ones. More passes do not fix it — neither key is moving. What the unsampled keys want is the compass's own curve, which is what `TUNING.md`'s stage 1 does with every smooth quantity, and item 284's rule that no number is read off another key's recording is a rule about **individuality** — a false beat, a gain scatter — which a decay rate is not. `DecayModel::draw` is now the line through the sampled keys times their **own departures from it, interpolated in the key**, exact at a sampled key and tapering back to the line over an octave past the last one that measured the band. The taper is load-bearing: D#6 is the last key measuring 2-6 kHz at all and its band falls 9.2 dB where C6 three semitones below falls 34.2, and carried flat that one reading governed the twenty-one keys above it. Nothing is drawn any more, so the sixth `Draw` stream item 307 took is gone and the stage is deterministic in the key without a seed saying so; `estimate::texture`'s draws keep their scatter, for exactly the reason this one loses it. **Where it lands.** Fitted against drawn, on the measured cells: bass 2-6 kHz **1.04 / 1.14** (was 1.05 / 0.72), tenor 2-6 kHz **1.00 / 1.04** and 6-12 kHz **1.06 / 0.98** (was 1.00 / 0.97 and 1.06 / 1.32), treble **1.05 / 1.17** and 0.79 / 1.39 (was 1.05 / 1.26 and 0.79 / 1.64), top 2-6 kHz **1.65 / 1.61** (was 1.68 / 1.11). The two halves are one instrument to within the statistic's own resolution, and the rows say the same: over A#5-F6 the median cell above 2 kHz now runs **1.48, 1.31, 3.73, 1.19, 1.28, 2.01, —, 1.29** key by key where it ran 1.57, 3.65, 3.73, 2.79, —, 2.01, 1.44, 3.84, and the two keys still standing above their neighbours are the two the recordings measured. **What it costs, and it is the band column.** 2-6 kHz whole-band decay gap goes bass +1.26 -> **+2.86**, tenor +7.76 -> +8.63, treble +11.45 -> +11.52, top +10.35 -> +11.38, and the coverage goes 66 rows and 42 drawn to **61 and 37**. That column is a measurement in this band and it moved the wrong way, and the reason is the honest one: the old numbers were bought by over-damping partials that did not need it — the bass's drawn keys were running at **0.72** of the recording's own rate — and by corrections in bands whose own scatter cannot resolve them, which the stop's error term now refuses. Item 306(b)'s finding stands and is now the account of this table too: what is left of these bands belongs to what is not the string.

321. **A key the library sampled could never reach the draw, and three documents said the opposite.** `tuner/examples/tail_reach.rs`, `engine/src/preset.rs`'s schema for `notes.synthesized_decay`, its mirror in `tuner/src/preset.rs`, and item 307's own text. `is_sampled` routed every one of the 30 sampled keys down the measured branch, where a band with fewer than `MIN_BAND_CELLS` trusted partials returns nothing, the correction comes out empty and the key is skipped — so the claim that the decay stage "draws for the six *sampled* keys of the top octave" described code that could not run. The fix is that the choice is **per band and not per key**: a band is closed against this key's own recording where that recording resolves it, and against the compass's curve where nothing does, whoever sampled the key. **One constraint on it is real and is now written down**: the provenance list is also the clearing list, so the stage may only declare a key drawn if the row is its own — `estimate::shaping` writes measured cells for the sampled keys up to the tracker's reach (items 200-201) and this stage cannot reproduce them, so a sampled key with a shaping row keeps it and takes no drawn band. **What the six keys actually say, now that they can be asked**, and it is a better answer than the draw would have been: A6 and C7 *do* resolve their 6-12 kHz band on their own recordings and it says the engine already decays **faster** than the piano there (median partial ratio 0.74 and 0.37), so the stop refuses them; D#7, F#7, A7 and C8 have fewer than three partials standing over the *render's* own floor in either band, so there is nothing on the engine's side to close a correction on and a drawn target would have nothing to be closed against. Both are refusals with a measurement under them rather than a branch that never ran, and all six still carry no row — which is what the shipped preset had, for the wrong reason. The same is true of the 19 keys of the top octave that carry no row: their bands hold one or two partials of a bank of four to nine, and item 292's finding that the top octave's excess is not in its partials at all is the same finding again.

322. **The gates, re-run rather than assumed.** `cargo test --release --workspace --no-fail-fast` is **568 green, 2 red** — the documented duplex (item 260) and the documented melody wobble (item 298) — against item 318's 562 and 2; the six new ones are three in `estimate::brilliance` (a headroom reads what it says, a saturated side makes the gap a one-sided bound in the direction it is saturated on, and the bounded gap is the plain one) and three in `estimate::tail` (a ceiling is one frequency and reaches a different partial at every key, a band's sum falls less than its median partial and both are reported, and the median's own error falls with the root of the count). `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets` is clean with and without `--features piano-tuner/diagnostics`. **The compass** (`renders/compass/COMPASS.md`): **6 flags over 6 keys -> 7 over 6** — B6 `match` +5.5, C5 `jitter` +5.4, D#6 `decay` −4.4, C7 `jitter` +4.4, F#2 `centroid` −4.3, A2 `centroid` −4.3, A2 `jitter` +4.1 — inside the standing 8-over-7 baseline and one flag worse than item 310. **The `decay` flag is named rather than hidden and it is the top octave's own**: D#6 is a *sampled* key whose row asks 2.01 over 2 kHz where the curve through the compass gives its drawn neighbours 1.19 and 1.28, and the measured cells say both halves are equally short there (1.65 fitted against 1.61 drawn), so what stands out is C6 and D#6's own recordings asking for more than the compass around them — which is a statement about two recordings, not a drawn row out of line, and item 310's clean sheet was bought by a neighbourhood so uneven (3.65, 3.73, 2.79, —, 2.01, 1.44, 3.84 key by key) that no single key could be an outlier in it. **The realism board** (`renders/realism/REALISM.md`): mean log-mel **4.99 -> 5.00** against the 4.86 + 0.15 allowance — inside it — with phrases 4.90 / 5.17 / 4.05 / 4.93 / 6.20 / 4.74; `modulation` 2.95 -> 2.93, `attack` 4.20 -> 4.26, `r` 0.885 / 0.928 / 0.889. That board's own sensitivity to this stage is worth recording, because it is what stopped the first three attempts at item 320 from shipping: raising every tail cell by the exponent 1.4 costs **0.28 dB of mel** (5.02 -> 5.30) and lowering it by 0.7 buys 0.25 (-> 4.77), so the board is steeply against damping and reads a version of this stage that over-damps the drawn bass — which item 307's did, at 0.72 of the recording's own rate — as an improvement. **Motion columns A1 1.36 (≤2.0), A2 0.76 (≥0.5), B1 1.49 dB (≤3), B2 1.071 (≥0.25).** **The melody** (`renders/melody/MELODY.md`): `roughness` 1.12 -> **1.17** (bar 1.67, pass), `hf` 1.11 -> **3.65** (bar 6.53, pass) with its seam 7.80 -> 9.23 at C4, and `wobble` **0.44** against a bar of 0.26 — the documented red, unchanged and standing at D4 as item 302 left it. The `hf` column's outlier is G4, a drawn key whose row now damps 24 cells where it damped 15, against an F4 that used to be at the schema's rail and is now at 1.89: it is the same smoothing the compass sees, from the other side, and it stays well inside its own bar. **The limiter is never reached** — zero limited samples on every benchmark phrase, headroom 7.2-21.0 dB — and all four performance tests pass in release. **Idempotence, measured rather than claimed**: `tail_reach --passes 8` run again on its own output reproduces the same 61 rows, the same 37 drawn keys and every cell to the last digit, which is the first time this stage has been byte-identical on a second run. **Re-rendered on the finished preset**: `renders/compass/`, `renders/realism/`, `renders/melody/`, `renders/salamander-ab/` and `renders/demo.wav`. **Still stale, for item 281's reason**: `renders/analysis/`, `renders/jitter/`, `renders/timbre-ladder/`, `renders/chain/` and `renders/scale.wav`.

323. **`tuner/examples/` was a misnomer for two different kinds of program, and separating them is what this item does.** Twenty-eight files sat in one directory behind one feature flag. Sixteen of them were **instruments**: built to settle one question, run once, quoted into a numbered item above, and never run again. The other twelve were **tools**: the preset factory and the standing boards, run again every time the instrument moves, and the things `README.md`'s reproduction recipe is made of. Cargo calls both "examples", the `diagnostics` feature gated a set that cut across the distinction (`brilliance`, `melody_line`, `chain_fit` and `fit_sympathetic` were behind it; `residuals` and `tail_reach` were not), and the consequence was that the shipped tooling was invoked with `--example` — a word that says *this is a demonstration* about the code that fits `presets/salamander-c5.toml`. **The dividing line kept is re-runnability**, not size or age or subject.

**The tools are subcommands of `piano-tuner`**, in `tuner/src/tools/`, which is part of the *binary* target and not of the library — so the library still analyses recordings with no engine, no thread pool and no PNG encoder on its link line, which is what the dev-dependency note in `tuner/Cargo.toml` has protected since the engine first appeared there. The command surface, in the three groups `--help` prints: one recording (`track`, `estimate`); the preset factory in the order the stages run (`survey`, `fit`, `sympathetic`, `tail`); the standing boards, each writing its own document (`bench` -> `REALISM.md`, `compass` -> `COMPASS.md`, `melody` -> `MELODY.md`, `chain` -> `CHAIN.md`); and the audits (`score`, `brilliance`, `residuals`, `ab`). Every flag and every output format is the one its example had.

**`fit` is `fit_motion` and `fit_partials` under one `--stage` list, and only those two**, because they fit the *same field off the same material* and one supersedes the other: `--stage partial_gains` writes the full measured ratio against a cleared probe (items 231, 237, 243) and replaces outright what `--stage partials` writes. Putting them behind one command is the only place a person sees that ordering. With no `--stage` the five motion stages run, which is the re-entrant path that builds the shipped preset; `--stage partials` is opt-in and **refused in combination with any other stage**, because it is not re-entrant and is fitted from the survey base rather than from the preset it writes into — the two want different base presets, and the refusal is a guard where the header was only a warning. `fit_sympathetic` and `tail_reach` did **not** merge into it: they loop over different material (release resonances; the whole compass at two instants) and keep their own commands, `sympathetic` and `tail`.

**`chain_fit` is a subcommand and not an instrument, on item 317's own words.** That item ends "the static transfer stays a diagnostic, re-runnable from `chain_fit`", and it writes `renders/chain/CHAIN.md`, which is a standing document with a regeneration command printed in it. A program whose output is a document that has to be regenerated when the engine moves is re-runnable by definition. It is `piano-tuner chain`.

**The instruments are `forensics/`**, a workspace member excluded from `default-members`, so `cargo build`, `cargo test` and `cargo clippy` at the root never compile it and `cargo build -p forensics` does. Seventeen binaries: the sixteen that were behind `diagnostics` and are one-shot (`jitter_forensics`, `timbre_ladder`, `ladder_analysis`, the four `verify_*` harnesses, `limiter_probe`, `key_probe`, `gain_level`, `top_octave`, `output_gain`, `audit_render`, `independent_audit`, `eigenmode_prototype`, `drift_line`), plus `engine/examples/analysis_render.rs`, which was the same kind of thing on the other side of the workspace — the corpus behind item 46's audit, `renders/analysis/` stale since item 281. `forensics/README.md` says what the crate is and indexes what each one settled. There is deliberately no library in it: every file is self-contained, and an instrument cannot rot a caller it does not have. `engine/examples/` is gone.

**The `diagnostics` feature is deleted**, because after the split there is nothing for it to gate — the instruments are excluded by the workspace and the tools are the binary. `engine-events` keeps its meaning exactly (`sampler::engine_events`, and the thing that pulls the engine in) and is now implied by a `tools` feature that also carries `png` and `rayon`; `tools` is in `default`, and `--no-default-features` still builds the library with none of the three, which is tested rather than asserted (`cargo build -p piano-tuner --no-default-features --lib`).

**One duplication fell out of the move and is now a tested function.** `to_engine_events` — the sampler's phrase spelled for the engine — existed **five times**, in `realism_bench`, `chain_fit`, `brilliance`, `limiter_probe` and `tests/limiter.rs`, identical in all five. It is `sampler::engine_events::to_render_events`, the exact inverse of the `from_render_events` that was already there, with a round-trip test: the translation has to be total and lossless or the two renders are not of the same performance, which is the premise `REALISM.md`, `COMPASS.md` and the melody gate all rest on. The one lossy step is narrowing the time to `f32`, so the test's times are chosen exact in binary and the equality is about the events.

**Every live regeneration command was updated in the generator that prints it**, not in the document: `bench`, `compass`, `melody` and `chain` each carry their own `cargo run ...` string in the code that writes the file, and all four documents were re-generated to prove it. `README.md`'s reproduction recipe, `TUNING_REPORT.md`'s header command, and every `tuner/examples/...rs` path reference in `engine/src/`, `tuner/src/`, `tuner/tests/` and `FUNDAMENTALS.md` now point at where the file actually is. **The numbered items above are a log and are left as written** — item 307 says `tail_reach --passes 8` because that is what was run.

**The gates, re-run rather than assumed.** `cargo test --release --workspace --no-fail-fast` is **569 green, 2 red** — the documented duplex (item 260) and the documented melody wobble (item 298) — against item 322's 568 and 2; the one new test is the round trip above. `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets` is clean, and so is `cargo clippy -p forensics --all-targets`, which is now a separate invocation because the root one no longer reaches it. `cargo build -p forensics` compiles all seventeen. **Nothing in `presets/` or `engine/src/` moved and no measurement changed**: the four standing documents were re-generated from the new commands and reproduce item 322's numbers exactly — the compass **7 flags over 6 keys** with the same keys and z-scores, the realism board mean log-mel **5.00** with phrases 4.90 / 5.17 / 4.05 / 4.93 / 6.20 / 4.74 and motion columns A1 1.36, A2 0.76, B1 1.49 dB, B2 1.071, the melody's `wobble` red standing where item 302 left it, and `CHAIN.md`'s interchannel mismatch 0.233 bare against 0.161 chained. A restructuring that changed a number would have been a restructuring that changed the instrument.

324. **The staleness audit: three inert fields leave the shipped presets, two investigation records leave the root, and thirteen unreferenced items leave the source — with the render bit-identical through all of it.** Item 323 separated the tools from the instruments; this is the other half of the same cleanup, and its rule is that nothing checked in may assert something nothing reads.

**The inert fields are out of both presets, and the survey no longer writes one.** Item 225 declared `voicing.unison_coupling`, `voicing.horizontal_offset_hz` and `voicing.unison_sigma_scale` parsed, validated and never read once the unison became a `2N × 2N` eigenproblem, and left them in the files. The consequence was measurable and nobody was measuring it: **every load of either shipped preset printed two or three warnings** — `presets/default.toml` carried `[0.35, 0.52, 0.27]` and `0.02`, `presets/salamander-c5.toml` carried those and a fitted three-string row `[0.87782794, 1.0, 1.1221721]` — and worse, **`survey` was still writing the third one**: `PresetBuilder::sigma_scale` put `SigmaSpread::pooled`'s answer into every preset stage 1 emitted, so re-running the documented recipe would have put it straight back. The fields stay in the *schema* (`serde(default)` + `skip_serializing_if` at the value the warning is silent at, in both crates' copies), so every preset written before the split still loads, still validates against `LEGACY_MAX_UNISON_COUPLING`, and is still written back carrying them; nothing this workspace produces has them. `estimate::spread` is kept and `report_spread` still prints its answer over the compass — it is a measurement of the recordings' own drift and it is what `tuner/tests/calibration.rs`'s `the_bridge_splits_a_unisons_decay_rates_and_the_drift_measures_it` closes the construction against — it simply no longer reaches a file. `PresetBuilder::sigma_scale` and `Survey::sigma_spread` are deleted.

**The render is bit-identical, proved four ways rather than argued.** `presets/default.toml` is 19 343 → 19 263 bytes (six lines), `presets/salamander-c5.toml` 102 309 → 102 037 (twenty-two lines), and *nothing else in either file moved*. (a) `the_sounding_path_is_what_it_was_before_the_mechanism` still reads **`b77c69714fdb6f21`**, the fingerprint unmoved since item 277's master-gain recalibration. (b) The character-for-character test is re-pinned by regenerating the file from the engine (`piano-emulator preset`), and the byte-for-byte round trip through the tuner's independent schema now covers **both** shipped files rather than only the default — the measured one is the one the factory rewrites and therefore the one that could silently re-acquire a field. (c) Rendered `demo` and `halo` through both presets, old file against new: **all four sha256 identical** — `ae35a01b…` / `2707ac25…` for the default, `43fc300e…` / `c00503e0…` for the measured one. (d) A **new test**, `the_inert_fields_do_not_reach_the_render_of_either_shipped_preset`, puts the historical values back into each loaded preset and asserts the fingerprints match, which is the first time item 225's claim has been checked on audio rather than by reading the construction. It also asserts the shipped files declare no inert fields and that a restored one still validates and is still named by the warning, so backward compatibility is a test and not a promise.

**`TUNING_REPORT.md` and `FUNDAMENTALS.md` are `docs/history/`.** Both are investigation records: written to settle a question, acted on, and kept for the measurements and the reasoning rather than as a description of the instrument. Each gets a status banner naming its date, the decision ranges its conclusions were acted on in — 98–133, 145–207 and 237 for Phase E's residuals; 223–261 and 296–302 for the string-model review — and what in it is superseded wholesale. `FUNDAMENTALS.md` §§0–4 review a string model the engine has not had since item 223, which the banner now says in the first line rather than leaving a reader to discover in §5. **217 cross-references in 41 files** were repathed (`engine/src/`, `engine/tests/`, `tuner/src/`, `tuner/tests/`, `forensics/src/`, `README.md`, `PHYSICS.md`), including the load-time warning's own text. The numbered items above are a log and are **left as written**, as item 323 left them; the header at the top of this file says where the two documents went.

**The four root documents keep their place and get honest headers.** `SPEC.md`: v1 done, five modules added that its layout does not list, the string model no longer the one its §Strings describes, and nothing in it deliberately unbuilt. `TUNING.md`: Phases A–E all done, and stage 2 was built — but *not* the way the file describes it, because MAESTRO was never downloaded and stage 2 is render-and-measure against the same Salamander library; what is genuinely still ahead is a global optimiser, and there is no CMA-ES anywhere in the source. `PHYSICS.md`: §§3, 4, 5, 6 and 7 are built (milestones A and B done), §2 and §9 are refused by measurement (items 315, 317), and §1 and §8 are what the file is now for — with item 317's prerequisite on §8 stated, that the loss gets a stereo term before the geometry is built. `DISTRIBUTION.md`: **live as engineering, moot as commerce**. The project is MIT-licensed and given away, so the licence-key rules, the IAP clauses, the 30 %/15 % commission arithmetic, "IAP if paid" in M7 and "UI worth charging for" in M6 are struck through in place rather than deleted — they remain an accurate account of what the store requires of anyone who *does* charge, and giving it away is a decision that can be revisited. The $99/yr Developer Program fee is explicitly **not** moot: notarization needs it whether or not anything is sold. `README.md` gains the licence section it never had, and the distinction between a live document and a history one is stated where the documents are listed.

**`data/fetch_salamander.sh` was gitignored, which made the reproduction recipe unrunnable from a fresh checkout.** `.gitignore` had `data/`, and the fetch script lives in it — so the first line of `README.md`'s recipe, the script that carries the URL, the 707 MiB size and the sha256 of the archive every measured number in this project comes from, was in nobody's clone. `ATTRIBUTION.md` even described it as "the gitignored `data/fetch_salamander.sh`". The rule is now `/data/*` with `!/data/fetch_salamander.sh`: the datasets and the caches stay out, the script that fetches them comes in. This is the one thing in the audit that was missing rather than stale.

**Thirteen unreferenced items are deleted, and the sweep is now exhaustive rather than spot-checked.** Every `pub` function, constant, struct and enum in `engine/src/` and `tuner/src/` was matched against every occurrence of its name across both crates, the tests and `forensics/` — `Engine::resonance_mut`, `Engine::soundboard_mut`, `ModalBank::set_mode_gain` (superseded by `set_mode_gain_complex`), `StftConfig::bin_spacing_hz`, `response::duplex_sigma`, `StrikeFit::envelope_at` (whose doc claimed the hammer estimator reads it, and it does not), `DecayReport::excitation_by_frequency`, `melody::partials_of`, `Sampler::preload` and with it `Instrument::sample_paths`, `numeric::weighted_mean`, `PresetBuilder::from_base_file`, `NoteEstimate::with_hammer` — and after removing them the scan returns nothing. `LEGACY_MAX_UNISON_COUPLING` is **kept** and its doc now says why in the first line: it is the bound a preset written before the split was validated against, removing it means refusing those files, and nothing this workspace writes will ever set the field.

**The dependency audit found nothing to remove, which is worth writing down because it was expected to.** Every dependency of all three crates is used, by qualified path where not by `use`: `engine` — `cpal` (`audio.rs`), `hound` (`render.rs`), `rtrb` (`engine.rs`), `midly` (`midi.rs`), `serde` and `toml` (`preset.rs`), and **`rustfft` in dev-dependencies is used by three of the four test files** (`smoke`, `partials`, `acceptance`); `tuner` — `hound` and `claxon` (`error.rs`, `audio.rs`), `rubato` (`sampler.rs`), `rustfft` (`stft`, `motion`, `realism`, `estimate::chain`), `serde_json` (`cache`, `trajectory`), `png` (`tools::bench`), `rayon` (six tools and the calibration gate), and the dev-dependencies on the engine and on `rayon` are what the gate and the limiter tests link; `forensics` — all four. `cargo build -p piano-tuner --no-default-features --lib` still links none of the optional three.

**Left in place, with the reason.** `renders/jitter/JITTER.md`, `renders/jitter/EIGENMODE.md` and `renders/timbre-ladder/ANALYSIS.md` are cited by name **80 times** across `engine/src/` (15), `engine/tests/` (7), `tuner/src/` (19) and `docs/history/FUNDAMENTALS.md` (39), and `renders/` is gitignored, so none of them is in a checkout. They are not checked in and should not be: each is the output of a `forensics/` instrument, which is precisely the reproducibility contract item 323 built that crate for, and each is a measurement of the engine *as it stood* when the item quoting it was written — items 281 and 322 already list those directories as deliberately stale. `forensics/README.md` gains the table that says which instrument regenerates which document, and says in the same breath that a re-run answers the same question about a different instrument and will not reproduce the quoted numbers. Nothing else checked in is stale: `renders/` and `data/` hold no tracked files, `assets/logo.png` is the README's own image, and `Cargo.lock` is current.

**The gates, re-run rather than assumed.** `cargo test --release --workspace --no-fail-fast` is **570 green, 2 red** — the documented duplex (item 260) and the documented melody wobble (item 298, standing at D4, `wobble` **0.44** against a bar of 0.26, the same numbers item 322 and item 323 recorded) — against item 323's 569 and 2; the one new test is the inert-field render proof above. `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets` is clean and so is `cargo clippy -p forensics --all-targets`; `cargo build -p forensics` compiles all seventeen. **No standing board was regenerated and none needed to be**: the two presets render bit-identical audio, no estimator changed, and the melody gate — which renders both lines through the engine and the recordings on every run — reproduces item 322's table to the digit, which is the boards' own material saying so.

328. **The reference's transposed notes are listening material and not truth, and that is now a rule the code enforces rather than a caveat somebody remembers.** `tuner/src/realism.rs`'s `RecordedKeys`, and every scored per-note comparison in the repository goes through it. Salamander records **30 of 88 keys**, one every minor third, and plays the other **58 by resampling the nearest take**: an SFZ maps each recording over `keycenter ± 1`. That transposed note is a perfectly good thing to listen to — it is exactly what a player of this library hears, and it is what every phrase on `REALISM.md` is played on — but it is not a measurement of the piano at that key. Its inharmonicity, its unison beat rate, its decay and its brightness are the neighbour's, shifted; scoring the engine's D4 against a resampled D#4 measures the resampler. **The rule: every scored per-note comparison uses recorded keys only, transposed notes stay in every render, and a report marks them `transposed — unscored` rather than dropping the row** — a reader has to be able to see that the note was played and why its column is empty. `RecordedKeys` answers the three questions a scoring surface needs: is this key a take (`is_recorded`), which take is it made of (`take_for` — used to name provenance, "transposed from D#4 (−1)"), and which *other* take could have made it (`alternate_take`, which is what item 329 measures the cost with). **What it touches, and what it deliberately does not.** The two per-note boards: `COMPASS.md`'s `match` column, which is the only one of its seven metrics that compares the engine against *the recording of the same note* — the other six score a key against its own neighbours and are unaffected — and the melody gate's bars (item 331). Not the phrase boards: a mel distance is a whole performance against a whole performance, and dropping two notes in three out of a piece of music does not make it more honest, it makes it a different piece. There the policy shows up as a measured number in the floor commentary (item 329) instead of a hole in the table. **On the melody line this is not a technicality: only C4 is a recording.** D4 and E4 are both the D#4 take, resampled a semitone down and a semitone up; F4 and G4 are both the F#4 take. Four of the five pitches are two recordings cloned. The recorded C4 take is also, on its own, a touch louder and brighter than the line around it — that is the take's own character and it is a fact about the piano, not an error to be corrected away.

329. **What the transposition is worth, measured on the scoreboard's own material rather than asserted.** `Instrument::rerouted` in `tuner/src/sampler.rs`, the new column in `renders/realism/REALISM.md`. Every unrecorded key has a *second* take within reach: D4 is the D#4 take a semitone down, or equally the C4 take two semitones up; E4 is D#4 up one, or F#4 down two. **Both are legitimate reconstructions of a note nobody recorded**, so how far apart they land is how much of "the reference" at those keys is the resampler. `rerouted` builds an instrument in which every unrecorded key is served by its second-nearest take and every recorded key keeps its own — cloning each take's regions onto the keys that route to it, and carrying the unpitched regions (the damper landings, the pedal tray, anything with `pitch_keytrack=0` or a CC 64 gate) over untouched, because those are recorded per key and there is nothing to reroute. The whole phrase set is then rendered **both ways through the same recordings, the same player, the same event list and the same level match**, and the only difference is which take was stretched. **Of the 397 strikes in the phrase set, 288 (73 %) land on a key the library never recorded.** Mean log-mel between the two routes: **2.67 dB**, per phrase `scale_mf` 2.39, `arpeggio_dynamics` 2.13, `chords_pedal` 2.38, `staccato` 1.93, `alberti_fast` 3.79, `excerpt` 3.38 — and it tracks the transposed share, the two ends of the range being `alberti_fast` at 120 of 144 strikes (3.79) and `staccato` at 14 of 26 (1.93). **Against the board's own numbers that is 53 % of the 5.00 dB the engine is minimised on, and 1.7× the 1.59 dB velocity-layer floor the table quotes beside every cell.** It is not subtracted from anything and it is not a correction. It is an *estimate of the ambiguity, not a bound on the error* — both routes stretch, one by more than the other, so the disagreement between them is of the same order as either route's own distance from the note that was never recorded, and it is quoted as that and nothing more. What it settles is the question item 328 would otherwise leave hanging: the thing the per-note boards now refuse to score against is not small, and a reader of the phrase board can see how big it is.

330. **The melody gate windowed the attack and the seam the listener heard lives in the tail, so the gate gains a second window — and the material it is read on had to change with it.** `estimate::melody`, `TAIL_WINDOW_S = (0.5, 2.0)` and `Window::{Head, Tail}`. Item 296 measures three numbers over 0.03-0.40 s of each note. That is where a `partial_gains` row and a hammer's colour live; it is not where a *decay* lives, and the note the listener called "quite off the rest" of the M8 melody is a decay one: **C4 carries a fitted `partial_sigma_scale` row 41 partials deep while D4, E4, F4 and G4 are all named in `notes.synthesized_decay` and carry drawn ones 22 to 26 deep.** The compass had already said so in a column the melody gate has no equivalent of — C4's `decay` reads **−4.8 dB/s against −8.3 / −10.5 / −10.6 / −12.0** at its four melody neighbours, where the recordings read −5.0 against −6.0 / −7.0 / −11.9 / −8.9 — but a compass statistic is not a tune, which is item 296's whole argument. **Two attempts at the tail on the line at tempo were tried and both are refuted by measurement, which is why the third exists.** (a) *Unpedalled, as `soprano` is*: the melody's quarter notes are held 0.45 s, so with the dampers working every note is over before the window opens. (b) *The same line with the pedal held down throughout*, which is the nearer of the two to `realism::excerpt`, the pedalled thing the listener actually played: the notes then ring, but the window 0.5-2.0 s after a strike contains **three later strikes**, and on this tune two of them are the note's own harmonics — G4 is C4's third partial to within a hertz and E4 sits under its fifth. Measured: on that render the five pitches' tail `hf` spans **2.7 dB, where the same five pitches with the tail to themselves span 8.3** — the number is the chord and not the note, and a column whose spread has been squeezed by a factor of three cannot resolve one note standing out of it. (c) What ships: **`slow_line`** — the line's own pitches, at the line's own velocity, in the order the tune introduces them, two passes, one strike every 2.5 s and each held 2.2 s. Every note is let go before the next is struck and the window is entirely inside the held note, so the tail is the note's own and no damper is in it. It is further from a tune than `soprano` is and that is the price; a tail is only a tail when nothing else is sounding, and the alternative is a column that measures a chord.

331. **The melody gate's bars come off the recorded keys of the register instead of off the line's own four clones, at the order statistic the gate is; one column goes red at C4 and the standing red goes green, and neither is the engine moving.** `estimate::melody::compare`, rewritten. **What the bar used to be:** the largest departure of any note of the *reference line* from its own trend, floored by the same on the neighbouring velocity layer. Item 297 already flagged the problem in its own words — "four of the five pitches are transpositions … so their scatter against each other is smaller than a real piano's would be" — and then set the bar on them anyway. **What it is now:** `ladder` plays the **recorded keys of the melody's own register** — D#3 F#3 A3 C4 D#4 F#4 A4 C5 D#5, nine takes of nine different keys — as the same music through the same window, and the bar is how far *those* notes go from *their* trend, never under the **per-take scatter** (`|reference − neighbouring velocity layer|` at the same recorded key, which is `REALISM.md`'s noise floor generalised from a phrase distance to a per-note metric). **Both terms are read at the same order statistic the gate's own number is**, and that is not a detail: the gate is a maximum over five pitches, and the maximum over nine recorded keys is systematically larger, so a bar set on it would forgive the engine an amount that grows with how many keys the library happened to sample. `worst_of_n` takes the **median of the largest of five draws** — the `0.5^(1/5) = 0.871` quantile of the magnitudes, by ordinary linear interpolation — which on the column that matters takes the register term from **6.75 to 4.26** and the bar with it from 8.44 to **5.32** — the difference between passing the note the listener complained about and failing it. **The six columns on the shipped preset**, `stands out (bar)`: `roughness` head **1.17 at F4 (1.49)**, `wobble` head **0.44 at D4 (1.10)**, `hf` head **3.65 at G4 (5.93)**, `roughness` tail **0.60 at D4 (3.15)**, `wobble` tail **1.20 at D4 (1.38)**, and **`hf` tail 5.43 at C4 against 5.32 — FAIL**. **That red is the listener's finding, reproduced as a gate**: C4's tail is 5.4 dB darker than its own line's trend where the recorded keys of the register go 4.26 dB from theirs, and it is the only note of the line the complaint could be about. It is not bought by the new bar — under the retired clone bar the same column would have read 5.43 against **2.30** and failed harder — it is bought by the window, which did not exist. **The `wobble` head column, red since item 298 and carried as documented since, is green: 0.44 against 1.10.** Nothing about the engine changed. Its old bar of 0.26 was the recordings' worst note *on a line four fifths of which is two takes resampled*, and two transpositions of one recording wobble almost identically; on nine different recorded keys of the same register the recordings' own scatter is **0.88 dB**. The yardstick was wrong, and it was wrong in the direction that manufactures a defect. The clone bars are printed beside the new ones in `MELODY.md` so the size of every such change is on the page: head 1.67 / 0.26 / 6.44 and tail 4.75 / 2.50 / 2.30, against 1.49 / 1.10 / 5.93 and 3.15 / 1.38 / 5.32 — the policy is not a uniform loosening, it moves four bars up and two down. **The seam is reported and not gated.** `error = engine − reference` and its departure from the register's median error is now measurable on material where both sides are the same note, which answers half of item 297's objection; the other half stands, because the engine's absolute distance from the piano moves by several dB across a register for reasons no per-note floor covers — on the shipped preset every column's seam is **6 to 25 times** its own floor (head 8.5 / 6.7 / 24.4, tail 6.1 / 7.4 / 19.8). It carries that floor beside it (the same statistic with the neighbouring velocity layer standing in for the engine) so a reader can see it. The line contributes the one number it honestly can, C4's own error, and its other four notes read `transposed — unscored`. **The attribution, from both sides, measured with the driver's own flags.** Clearing the **drawn** `partial_sigma_scale` rows — every key `notes.synthesized_decay` names, which on this line is D4, E4, F4 and G4 and not C4 — takes the tail `hf` column from **5.43 at C4 to 3.85 at E4** and it passes: with the neighbours' drawn rows gone C4 stops being the note that does not belong, which is the seam named in one measurement. Clearing **C4's own fitted row instead** takes the column to **5.19 at C4**, which passes by 0.13 dB — and takes C4's seam against its own recording from 13.63 to **15.56**, i.e. deleting the fitted row buys evenness by making the one note of the line whose reference is a real recording *further* from it. Neither is the fix; both together say what the fix has to be, which is to make the drawn rows land where the fitted one does rather than to take the fitted one away. **`the_gate_fails_on_the_preset_this_milestone_started_from` still falsifies**, and it moved: with item 284 undone in memory the red is `wobble` tail at **C4, 1.78 against 1.38**, where the shipped preset reads 1.20 at D4 — the drawn splits are what stopped the one key with measured ones standing alone, which is item 284's own claim, now visible in a window that did not exist when it was made.

332. **What the two boards read under the policy, re-run rather than assumed.** **The compass** (`renders/compass/COMPASS.md`): **7 flags over 6 keys → 9 over 8**, and every one of the six non-`match` flags is byte-identical to item 322's — C5 `jitter` +5.4, D#6 `decay` −4.4, C7 `jitter` +4.4, F#2 `centroid` −4.3, A2 `centroid` −4.3, A2 `jitter` +4.1 — which is the check that the policy touched the one column it claims to. **`match` loses one flag and gains three, and both directions are the point.** B6 `match` **+5.5 is gone**: key 95 is not a Salamander take, its reference is the A#6 recording resampled, and the flag was the engine being scored against a resampler. **A6 +8.4, F#7 +4.7 and D#7 +4.3 appear**, all three of them recorded keys, and the raw numbers under them never moved — A6 reads `match` **22.8 dB**, D#7 20.6, F#7 21.4 and A7 18.0 where the recorded keys below them read C6 7.4, D#6 6.9 and F#6 5.0. What changed is that the residual and the `1.4826·MAD` scale are now taken over recorded keys only: the eight nearest same-`N` neighbours of a top-octave key were six transposed ones whose `match` is a resampler's distance, which is larger, and a neighbourhood median and a scale built out of them hid a genuine step at A6. The neighbourhood minimum drops from four of eight to three for this column alone, because the recorded population is a third as dense and at the ends of the compass the library has only three same-`N` takes; two recorded keys (C7, C8) share no partial their two spectra can both read and print `-` rather than a distance. **58 of 88 rows carry `transposed — unscored`.** **The realism board is unmoved to the hundredth** — mean log-mel **5.00**, phrases 4.90 / 5.17 / 4.05 / 4.93 / 6.20 / 4.74, `modulation` 2.93, `attack` 4.26, `r` 0.885 / 0.928 / 0.889, motion columns A1 1.36 / A2 0.76 / B1 1.49 / B2 1.071 — which is what a milestone that changes no preset and no estimator should do, and it is the control that says item 329's new column is an addition to the file rather than a change to the measurement. **Nothing in `presets/` or `engine/` moved.** Re-rendered on the shipped preset: `renders/compass/`, `renders/realism/`, `renders/melody/` (whose `ode_soprano_sustained_*.wav` from the refuted attempt (b) of item 330 are deleted and replaced by `ode_pitches_slow_*.wav`). Still stale, for item 281's reason: `renders/analysis/`, `renders/jitter/`, `renders/timbre-ladder/`, `renders/chain/`, `renders/salamander-ab/` and `renders/scale.wav`.

333. **The gates, re-run rather than assumed.** `cargo test --release --workspace --no-fail-fast` is **587 green, 2 red** against item 324's 570 and 2. **The two reds are not the same two.** The documented duplex (item 260) is unchanged. The other one has moved: `no_note_of_the_line_wobbles_unlike_the_rest`, red since item 298, is **green at 0.44 against a bar of 1.10** and the reason is item 331 — its old bar came off four clones of two recordings — while `no_note_of_the_lines_tail_is_brighter_than_the_rest` is red at **C4, 5.43 against 5.32**, which is the listener's own finding standing as a gate for the first time. A red that was a yardstick and a red that is the instrument, swapped. **The seventeen new tests**: seven in `estimate::melody` (the slow line lets no note sound inside another's tail and its pitches are the tune's own in the tune's order; the ladder is the recorded keys of the register, up-and-down at tempo and up once slowly; only the line's recorded key carries a per-note error and the transposed four are named; the seam is measured where both sides are the same note; the clone line would have set a bar the recorded register refuses; the bar reads the order statistic the gate is and not the population maximum; a take a transposed note is made of is named), four in `realism` (a key is either a recording or the nearest one transposed, and its provenance prints; the second route onto a transposed key is the other neighbour and the routing map never leaves a key on the take it started from; the register a bar is measured in is the recorded keys of that register; a library of one key can be asked and answers `None`), one in `sampler` (rerouting plays every unrecorded key from the other take, keeps recorded keys on their own, and carries the unpitched regions over untouched), and five in `tests/melody.rs` itself — three metrics in two windows instead of one, the tail falsification beside the head one, and the policy asserted on the shipped library rather than on a hand-made one. `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets` is clean.

334. **The C4 the listener heard is one band of one table, and the bisection says which one to the last digit.** `tuner/src/tools/melody.rs`'s driver flags plus three new preset variants, all read off the tail `hf` column the gate fails on and off C4's own error against the recording of C4 — the only reference note on this line that is a recording (item 328). C4's engine tail `hf` is **−30.77 dB** where its recording is −22.09, an error of **−8.69**, against a register whose median error is −0.94. **The bisection**, each row a copy of the shipped preset with one thing changed in memory and nothing else: *C4's whole `partial_sigma_scale` row cleared* → −12.79, error **+9.30**, column 5.19 at C4 (passes by 0.13 and takes the one scored note of the line **further** from the piano — item 331's finding, reproduced); *only C4's cells at and above 2 kHz cleared* → −17.96, error **+4.13**, column 2.61 at D4; *only C4's cells **under** 2 kHz cleared* → −25.45, error **−3.36**, column **1.72 at E4**; *C4's `k = 1` cell alone, 0.53, set to one* → −25.91, error −3.82, column 1.75 — **one cell carries 4.87 dB of the 5.33 the whole sub-2 kHz block is worth**; *every drawn row cleared* (`--no-drawn-decay`) → C4 unmoved at −30.77 and the column 3.85 at E4, which is the neighbours moving and not the note. The two halves are additive to a fifth of a decibel: −5.33 from under 2 kHz and −12.82 from over it against the −17.98 both together. **The mechanism is the metric's own arithmetic and it is not subtle.** `hf` is a *share* — 2-6 kHz over the whole band — and its denominator is the fundamental. Measured per partial on the same 2.2 s note (`forensics/src/bin/tail_seam.rs`), C4's `k = 1` sits at **−44.2 dB at 0.5 s with the 0.53 cell and −48.4 without it**, and **nothing above `k = 1` moves at all**: the cell does not darken C4, it holds C4's fundamental up, and the share reads that as 3.1 dB of darkness at the instant and 5.3 dB integrated over the window. **What the cell is doing is right and its absence next door is what is wrong.** With it, C4's fundamental falls 16.0 dB over 0.5-2.0 s against the recording's 17.3; without it, 19.1. The four melody neighbours have no such cell — D4/E4/F4/G4 fall 18.0 / 17.6 / 17.5 / 17.0 — because nothing ever wrote one.

335. **`TailCorrection::at` returns exactly 1.0 at and below 2 kHz, so that band was written by `estimate::shaping` at the 30 recorded keys and by nothing at all at the other 58 — a fitted/unfitted step in one band, and the fix is to make it a compass quantity like the two above it.** `tuner/src/estimate/tail.rs`: `LOW_BAND`, `low_mean`, `low_row`, `LowDecay`, `MIN_LOW_CELLS`, and eight lines in the `tail` stage that write it once before the passes. **The step, measured.** The geometric mean of a row's cells under 2 kHz, over every key that has at least three of them: the **recorded** keys read 1.00 at A0, 0.94 at F#3, 0.75 at C4, 0.59 at A4, 0.39 at C5 — median 0.941, range 0.394-1.373, n 19 — and the **unrecorded** keys read 1.000, range **1.000-1.001**, n 29. Item 288's own two statistics on it: the group difference **−1.17 dB** of rate and, with the register trend removed, **−1.46 dB**, against the recorded keys' own scatter about their line of ×1.19 (1.51 dB). It is not noise and it is not a per-key idiosyncrasy: `ln` of it against the key is `exp(+0.5488 − 0.01411·key)`, r **−0.797** over 19 keys, and the residual's lag-1 autocorrelation across the compass is **+0.05** — white, so the departure of one key says nothing about the next and interpolation is all that may be done with it. That is item 320(d)'s argument one band lower and it gets item 320(d)'s answer: **the line through the keys that measured it, times their own departures interpolated in the key**, exact at a recorded key, tapered to the line an octave past the last one — never a draw of its scatter, which would land a random factor from what the sampled key next door measured, which is the seam and not the cure. **Written once, before the passes and not inside them**, because everything else in this stage is a correction measured on the render and iterated to a fixed point while this is a statement about the piano; multiplying it in every pass would compound a number with nothing to converge against. **`MIN_LOW_CELLS = MIN_BAND_CELLS = 3`, on both sides of the transaction, and it is not a formality**: the recorded keys carry 67 cells here at A0 and **one** from C6 up, and those single-cell keys read 0.42, 1.00 and 0.67 — a factor of two either way, which is the per-partial scatter (item 285's 4.5 dB) and not a register trend. Fitting the line through them and then writing the answer onto the top octave lengthened the fundamental of six keys above E6 by ×1.6-1.8 and took the compass from **9 flags to 13**, four of them new on `jitter`, which is what a unison whose partners are held against each other for longer does. Refused as a population point and refused as a target, for the same reason.

336. **Where it lands, and the control is that it moved exactly the 58 keys the library never recorded and not one of the 30 it did.** Re-fitted with `piano-tuner tail --passes 8`, which converges by pass 6 and re-emits **byte-identically** over its own output — the same 69 rows, the same 45 drawn keys, every cell to the last digit. **37 rows changed, and every one of them is a key whose reference note is a resampled neighbour** (not one is ≡ 0 mod 3, which is what a Salamander take is); the largest cell move is ×2.22 at B4 and the smallest ×1.003 at A#0. **The melody gate**, `stands out (bar)`: `roughness` head **1.17 → 0.95** (1.49), `wobble` head **0.44 → 0.35** (1.10), `hf` head **3.65 → 3.00** (5.93), `roughness` tail **0.60 → 0.83** (3.15), `wobble` tail **1.20 → 1.11** (1.38), and **`hf` tail 5.43 at C4 → 3.76 at D4** against 5.32 — the red is green, five of six columns improved, and **C4's own departure from its line goes 5.43 → 1.88**. The bars did not move, because the population they come from is the recorded keys and this touched none of them. **The seam statistic**, item 288's S1/S2 on the sub-2 kHz mean: **−1.17 → −0.01 dB** and **−1.46 → −0.10 dB**, i.e. inside the recorded keys' own ×1.19 scatter where it used to be at it; the unrecorded keys' range goes from [1.000, 1.001] to **[0.451, 1.296]** against the recorded keys' [0.394, 1.373]. **What it costs, and both numbers are quoted rather than one.** The phrase board goes mean log-mel **5.00 → 5.07 dB**, `modulation` 2.93 → 2.95, `attack` 4.26 → 4.32, `r` 0.885/0.928/0.889 → 0.886/0.927/0.891, motion columns A1 1.36 / A2 0.76 / B1 1.49 / B2 1.071 unmoved. That +0.07 dB is scored **entirely against notes the owner policy says are not truth** — every key this changes is one of the 58 the library resamples, item 329 measures the ambiguity in exactly those notes at **2.67 dB**, and the velocity-layer floor beside the column is 1.59 — and it is not avoidable by restricting the register, which was tried: the tenor alone costs 0.06 of it and the treble 0.03. **The compass goes 9 flags over 8 keys to 12 over 11**, and the three new ones are a **scale effect and not a defect**: at every newly flagged key — F#5, D#5, B6 — the engine's own `jitter` is unchanged to the last digit and so is the recording's, and no row above key 76 moved at all. What moved is the `1.4826·MAD` the z divides by, which contracts **8 %** (0.178 → 0.163) because the drawn keys got *more* like their neighbours — of the 15 keys whose `jitter` moved, 11 moved toward the recording — so the same unmoved outliers stand further out of a tighter population. Mean worst |z| over the compass: 2.153 → 2.181.

337. **The gates, re-run rather than assumed.** `cargo test --release --workspace --no-fail-fast` is **594 green, 1 red** against item 333's 587 and 2. **The two reds became one**: the documented duplex (item 260) is unchanged, and `no_note_of_the_lines_tail_is_brighter_than_the_rest` — red since it was written one milestone ago, and the listener's own finding — is **green at 3.76 against 5.32**. `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets` is clean. **The falsification moved with the fix rather than being retired with it**: `the_tail_gate_fails_without_the_drawn_low_band_at_c4` runs the six columns on the shipped preset with the drawn rows' sub-2 kHz cells put back to 1.0 and nothing else touched, and reproduces item 331's table **to the last digit on all six columns** — 1.17 F4 / 0.44 D4 / 3.65 G4 / 0.60 D4 / 1.20 D4 / **5.43 C4 against 5.32, FAIL** — which is the strongest form of the attribution: the whole difference between the two instruments, on every column of the gate, is that one band. **Six new tests and one rewritten in place**: five in `estimate::tail` (the low band is the geometric mean of the cells under 2 kHz and ignores everything above; a key with fewer than three of them is refused as a population point and as a target; the line is exact where a key measured it and interpolates between two; it tapers to itself an octave past the last measurement and an empty model asks for one; a drawn low row scales rather than replaces, leaves everything above the edge alone and obeys the schema's rails), one in `tests/melody.rs` (the control that no key carrying a drawn decay row is a key the bars are measured on), and `the_tail_gate_fails_on_the_shipped_preset_at_c4` becomes `the_tail_gate_fails_without_the_drawn_low_band_at_c4` — same assertion, material that is now the instrument the fix was made against. The driver carries the same two experiments as flags, `--no-drawn-low` and `--clear-low K`, so item 334's table is reproducible without a hand-edited preset. Re-rendered on the shipped preset: `renders/compass/`, `renders/realism/`, `renders/melody/`. `forensics/src/bin/tail_seam.rs` is item 334's instrument, kept as its reproducibility record. Still stale, for item 281's reason: `renders/compass/detail/` (per-partial pairs are written only for keys named on the command line, and none were), `renders/analysis/`, `renders/jitter/`, `renders/timbre-ladder/`, `renders/chain/`, `renders/salamander-ab/` and `renders/scale.wav`.

338. **The column that should have convicted the mechanism was reading the engine a fifth of the way into its own attack, and had been since it was written.** `realism::strike_near`, `ATTACK_SEARCH_BACK_S`, `ATTACK_SEARCH_FORWARD_S`, and `estimate::melody::note_onset` delegating to the same primitive. `REALISM.md`'s `attack` column is the mean absolute difference in spectral tonality of the first 30 ms of every onset, and its onsets are **detected on the reference**. The reference is a sampler: it plays each recording from the file's own start, so what reaches the ear is late by however much silence there was between the engineer's trigger and the hammer. That offset is a property of one *sample file*, and it is not small — measured at the 30 recorded keys at five velocities each (`forensics/src/bin/line_noise.rs`), the reference's own strike stands **+19 ms after the engine's at the median, +27 ms on the mean, and +112 ms at the worst key**, against a window 30 ms long. So the engine was being read a median of two thirds of a window past its own attack, at a moment when the burst it is being scored on is largely over, and the reference was being read on its attack: two sides of one comparison windowed by different rules. `estimate::melody` has never had this defect and says why in its own source — "a gate whose two sides are windowed by different rules is not comparing them" (`ONSET_SEARCH_S`, item 302) — so the fix is that module's own device promoted to a shared primitive: the largest rise in a 1 ms RMS envelope, searched back 120 ms (the whole measured distribution) and forward 10 ms (the flux detector's frame quantisation) from the detected onset, **clamped to the midpoint of the gap to the previous onset** so that a sixteenth-note phrase cannot find the note before. **What it moves, on the preset this milestone opened on**: the `attack` column **4.32 → 5.20** and its velocity-layer floor 1.41 → 1.88 — the floor rises because both sides of *that* pair are sampler renders with lead-ins of their own — and the signed mean **+0.35 dB → −1.98 dB**, which is a reversal of the verdict and not a refinement of it: the engine's attacks were being reported as slightly *more* tonal than the piano's when they are two decibels *less*. `mel` does not move at all (5.07), which is the control that says this is a window and not a render. **Every attack number in this repository written before this item is quoted through the old window and is not comparable with one written after it**, including item 310's own 3.55 → 4.20 — the pair that named this milestone's second finding.

339. **It is the strike and it is not the key-off, and both events are measured whole rather than argued about.** `forensics/src/bin/line_noise.rs`. The listener said "hammer/key-press noise", and a melody line fires exactly two mechanism events: `[noise.strike]` on every note-on and `[noise.key_off]` on every note-off. Both are *additive* in `Voice::process`, so the Ode soprano line rendered four ways — as shipped, with each silenced, with both — gives each event **exactly**, sample for sample, through the board and the master gain, and nothing here is a model of a burst. **The key-off is refuted by the measurement that would have convicted it.** At the line's 28 note-offs, the level over the 60 ms after each against the 60 ms before reads engine-minus-reference **+0.12 dB on the mean (|·| 2.97)** as shipped and **+0.06 dB (|·| 2.95)** with `[noise.key_off]` silenced: the whole event is worth six hundredths of a decibel of the release step and two hundredths of its scatter, and its loudest octave against the engine's own content in that octave is −17.6 dB at 125 Hz, where this melody has almost nothing. It is left alone. **The strike is the event, and its level is not the thing to look at.** It is 55.3 dB under the line's whole-line RMS, which is why no energy statistic found it; against the engine's own content *octave by octave* it is −86 dB at 62 Hz, −69 at 1 kHz, **−21.0 at 4 kHz and −14.4 at 8 kHz**. A burst that is a rounding error on a phrase's energy is a fifth of what the engine puts in its top two octaves during an attack, and those are the octaves a spectral flatness reads.

340. **The mechanism's balance is a ratio nothing in the factory closes on, it is inverted exactly rather than estimated, and the answer is a velocity law as much as a level.** `estimate::attack`'s `noise_to_tone_db`, `mix`, `balance_offset`, `balance_reading`, `fit_balance`, and `piano-tuner noise` as the driver. **What was missing.** `estimate::attack` fits this event's colour and a level referenced to the note's *peak*; `fit --stage partials`'s `strike_offset` then corrects that level on the engine's own render (items 210-213). Neither of them closes on how loud the hammer is **against the note**, and that is the only thing a listener hears — so any change anywhere else in the engine that moves the attack's tonal content without moving the note's peak moves the balance, and the factory does not notice. **The statistic** is attack tonality, the arithmetic over the geometric mean of the power spectrum of the first 30 ms from a note's own strike: a line spectrum reads large, a continuum reads zero, so it *is* a noise-to-tone ratio, it needs no level match and no model of the note, and it is the same number `REALISM.md`'s `attack` column is a mean of — read here per note against a recording of **that** note, at the 30 recorded keys only (item 328) and at five velocities each. **The inversion is exact.** Two renders per note, one with the event silenced, and the sample-wise difference **is** the event; every other level of it is then `tone + 10^(Δ/20)·burst` with no render repeated, and the Δ that puts the engine on the recording is bisected over that mixture. Adding a continuum to a line spectrum can only lower the flatness ratio, so the crossing is unique. This is item 199's argument with the estimator between the render and the number removed altogether. **What it reads on the preset this milestone opened on**: engine minus reference, median **−7.08 dB**, mean |·| **8.31** over 150 notes — the engine's attack seven decibels noisier than the piano's. With the event silenced the same board reads **+7.28 / 8.78**: the engine's *tonal* attack is seven decibels **more** tonal than the piano's whole one, so the burst was not filling a gap of 7 dB, it was overshooting one by **14.4 dB**. **And the error is a slope.** Engine minus reference by velocity, at 24 / 48 / 72 / 88 / 110: **−12.32, −9.65, −6.35, −5.07, −2.36**; the offset each asks for: **−16.98, −14.55, −11.71, −8.47, −2.56**.
