A hammer whose noise is right at forte and 17 dB too loud at pianissimo is a velocity law, and `[noise.strike]` has exactly one field for it. **The fit is Theil-Sen and that is a property of the material, not a preference**: the readings are censored (24 of 150 do not invert, all of them because the engine is already noisier than the piano with the event silenced — a finding, printed as `floor`, and never the other way round), and the per-key scatter of this quantity is **10.53 dB** about the fitted line, because how much hammer noise one *recording* has is a property of that recording. Through the 126 that invert: level at the nominal drive **−7.29 dB**, `velocity_db` **24.40 → 43.90**. **Re-entrancy is the point of inverting on the engine and it is measured, not claimed**: a second pass over the tool's own output asks for **−0.00 dB and −0.00 dB**. **After**: median **−0.77**, mean |·| **4.46**, and by velocity **+0.31, −0.55, −1.12, −2.28, −1.37** — centred *and* flat, which the level alone does not do (level only: −3.36 median, still sloped −6.86 to −0.67) and the law alone does not do either (velocity only: −4.84 median, flat at −4.84). **Two hypotheses are refuted by measurement rather than by argument.** The first is that M8's decay correction reaching inside the attack window is what unbalanced this: with **every** `notes.partial_sigma_scale` row cleared the same board reads −6.97 / +7.09 against the shipped −7.08 / +7.28, which is a tenth of a decibel. On the *phrase* board through item 338's corrected window the same experiment is worth 0.38 dB of the `attack` column (5.20 → 4.82) where this refit is worth **0.81** (5.20 → **4.39**) — so the decay correction is real and is the smaller half, and the mechanism is the larger one. The second is that the event should simply be silenced, which the `attack` column's own arithmetic invites: silenced it reads **5.55** on the phrase board and **+7.28 / 8.78** on the recorded keys, both worse than either the shipped level or the refit. **What is not refitted, and why.** The colour — `centroid_hz`, `bandwidth_hz`, `decay_s` — comes from the *recordings'* own onset residual, and nothing in any milestone since has moved a recording; what could have moved is the deficit band those three were measured inside, which is referenced to the engine, and re-opening it needs the survey base this repository does not keep (`fit --stage partials` is not re-entrant, item 214). The 10.53 dB this fit leaves behind is not a colour signal either: it has no register trend and it is per-key, which is what a recording's own hammer is. **Costs, both quoted**: phrase `mel` 5.07 → **5.10**, `modulation` 2.95 → **3.01**, `release` unmoved at 2.83, motion columns unmoved (A1 1.36, A2 0.76, B1 1.49 dB, B2 1.071), and the compass unmoved at **12 flags over 11 keys**, the same eleven keys.

341. **The balance joins the melody gate as its fourth column, and it is gated on a different question from the other three.** `estimate::melody`'s `METRIC_IS_BALANCE`, `NoteTexture::strike_db`, `Column::balance` / `balance_bar` / `gated_on_balance`. **Why it belongs there and why it is not one of the other three.** The three texture columns ask *does one note of the line stand out from the rest*, which is item 284's complaint, which the engine answers on its own and which needs no recording of the note — that is what let them survive item 328's policy with four fifths of the line unscored. This one asks *is the mechanism as loud against the note as the piano's is against the same note*, which is a comparison **with a recording** and means nothing at a transposed key. So it is not scored on the line at all: it is the median over the **recorded ladder** — D#3 F#3 A3 C4 D#4 F#4 A4 C5 D#5, the same nine keys every bar on this board is already measured from — of `engine − recording`, and its bar is the median distance between **two takes of one of those keys**, the neighbouring velocity layer, times the same 1.25. Both sides are medians over the same nine keys, so the comparison is like for like; the evenness numbers are still computed and printed beside it and are not gated. **It has one window and it is neither of the two.** `NOTE_WINDOW_S` starts at 0.03 s *because* that is past the hammer's noise, so the one span the three texture metrics deliberately exclude is the only span this one is about — and a tail has no strike in it, so `compare` emits the column in the head window only. **The reading**: **−3.90 dB against a bar of 2.05** on the preset this milestone opened on, and **−1.60, pass**, after item 340. With the event silenced outright it reads +0.63 and would also pass, which is the honest statement of how little this event is worth *at this velocity in this register* and exactly why the level was fitted on 150 notes across the compass and not on nine. **The falsification is the shipped preset with two numbers put back**: `the_strike_gate_fails_at_the_level_the_milestone_started_from` restores `[noise.strike]`'s level and velocity law and nothing else, reproduces **−3.90**, and asserts that every one of the other six columns is unmoved — 0.95 / 0.35 / 3.00 and 0.83 / 1.11 / 3.76 against the shipped 0.96 / 0.35 / 3.04 and 0.83 / 1.11 / 3.76 — which is the control that says one event's level is the whole difference between the two instruments on this board. The driver carries the same two experiments as flags, `--before-noise` and `--no-strike`.

342. **The gates, re-run rather than assumed.** `cargo test --release --workspace --no-fail-fast` is **605 green, 1 red** against item 337's 594 and 1, and the red is the same one: the documented duplex (item 260). `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets` is clean, and so is `-p forensics`. **Eleven new tests**: six in `estimate::attack` (a mix scales the event and leaves the tone alone; adding a continuum to a line spectrum only lowers the tonality; the inversion recovers the level a note was built with; it is a fixed point and lands where it aims; a recording the event cannot reach is refused from either side and says which; the correction is a level and a slope, Theil-Sen through a fifth of the population thrown 40 dB off the line, and readings that do not invert are counted and not fitted), two in `estimate::melody` (the balance column is scored on the recorded keys and not on the line, and keeps the sign; a tail has no strike in it), one in `realism` (each side of the attack is windowed on its own strike — two identical attacks 25 ms apart read under 1 dB apart where the mis-placed window reads over 3), and two in `tests/melody.rs` (the balance gate and its falsification). **The boards, on the shipped preset**: `REALISM.md` mel **5.10** (floor 1.59, transposition 2.67), modulation 3.01, `attack` **4.39** (floor 1.88, signed +1.29), r 0.886 / 0.927 / 0.891, release 2.83, motion A1 1.36 / A2 0.76 / B1 1.49 / B2 1.071. `COMPASS.md` **12 flags over 11 keys**. `MELODY.md` seven columns: roughness 0.96 / wobble 0.35 / hf 3.04 head, roughness 0.83 / wobble 1.11 / hf 3.76 tail, all inside their bars, and `strike` **−1.60 against 2.05**. Re-rendered: `renders/realism/`, `renders/compass/`, `renders/melody/`. `forensics/src/bin/line_noise.rs` is item 339's instrument and item 338's lead-in measurement, kept as their reproducibility record; `forensics/src/bin/preset_variant.rs` writes the one-thing-changed presets the tables above are read off, so every experiment in items 338-341 is reproducible without a hand-edited file. Still stale, for item 281's reason: `renders/compass/detail/`, `renders/analysis/`, `renders/jitter/`, `renders/timbre-ladder/`, `renders/chain/`, `renders/salamander-ab/` and `renders/scale.wav`.

343. **The whole board re-scored under the owner policy, with the milestone split into the two halves that caused it — and the control is that the bars, the floors and the reference side never move.** Every standing board re-run through the new CLI on three presets: the one this milestone opened on (`git HEAD`), that preset with only the drawn sub-2 kHz decay band added (items 334-336), and the shipped one, which is that plus the hammer refit (items 338-341). All three are read through item 338's corrected attack window, so the three columns are comparable with each other and none of them is comparable with a number written before that item. **The phrase board** (`renders/realism/REALISM.md`), M8 / +tail / shipped: `mel` **5.00 / 5.07 / 5.10** dB (per phrase 4.90 5.17 4.05 4.93 6.20 4.74 → 5.00 5.23 4.09 5.02 6.25 4.83 → 5.03 5.24 4.09 5.03 6.36 4.83), `modulation` 2.93 / 2.95 / **3.01**, `attack` **5.25 / 5.20 / 4.39** and its signed mean **−2.20 / −1.98 / +1.29** — the reversal is the hammer and not the decay, which is items 340's claim measured a second time on material it did not choose — `r` 0.885/0.928/0.889 → 0.886/0.927/0.891 → unchanged, `release` **3.06 / 2.83 / 2.83**. The **floors are identical in all three columns** (mel 1.59, modulation 0.96, attack 1.88, release 0.66) and so is the transposition column (**2.67** dB, per phrase 2.39 / 2.13 / 2.38 / 1.93 / 3.79 / 3.38, over the 288 of 397 strikes that land on a key nobody recorded), because both are properties of the recordings and nothing in this milestone touched a recording. **The motion columns do not move at all**, on any of the three: A1 **1.355** (≤ 2), A2 **0.763** (≥ 0.5), B1 **1.485** dB (≤ 3), B2 **1.071** (≥ 0.25), 16 cells and 16 velocity cells, B2's halves frequency 1.248 (1.000 against 0.802 c) and depth 0.919 (2.09 against 2.28 dB); worst A1 cells A4 k3 ×3.53 and A4 k4 ×3.26, worst B1 cells C6 k4 8.06 dB and A4 k3 4.04 dB. **The melody gate**, seven columns as `stands out (bar)`: `roughness` head **1.17 → 0.95 → 0.96** at F4 (1.49), `wobble` head **0.44 D4 → 0.35 F4 → 0.35 F4** (1.10), `hf` head **3.65 → 3.00 → 3.04** at G4 (5.93), `strike` **−3.90 FAIL → −3.90 FAIL → −1.60 pass** (2.05), `roughness` tail **0.60 D4 → 0.83 E4 → 0.83 E4** (3.15), `wobble` tail **1.20 → 1.11 → 1.11** at D4 (1.38), `hf` tail **5.43 C4 FAIL → 3.76 D4 → 3.76 D4** (5.32). **Every bar is identical in all three columns** — register 1.19 / 0.88 / 4.75 head and 2.52 / 1.11 / 4.26 tail, per-take 0.27 / 0.24 / 1.01 and 0.42 / 0.17 / 1.11, the balance bar's one key's two takes 1.64 — which is what a bar measured on the recorded keys of the register should do when nothing recorded moved, and it is the control that says the two red-to-green transitions are the instrument. **C4, which is the one note of this line the policy lets anybody score**, as its departure from the line's own trend: `roughness` head +0.00 / +0.00 / +0.00, `wobble` head −0.04 / −0.11 / −0.11, `hf` head +0.00 / +0.00 / +0.00, `roughness` tail +0.36 / **+0.23** / +0.23, `wobble` tail +0.58 / **+0.83** / +0.83, `hf` tail **−5.43 / −1.88 / −1.88**, and on `strike` its error against the recording of C4 **−6.51 / −6.54 / −3.62**. On the shipped preset C4 is the note that stands out on **none of the seven columns** — the standouts are F4, F4, G4, E4, D4 and D4 — which is the listener's complaint answered in the statistic it was raised in. **The head `wobble` red is not this milestone's** and the re-score says so: it went green at item 331 on a yardstick change, and the two preset changes since have moved it 0.44 → 0.35 → 0.35 against a bar that never moved. **The compass**: **9 flags over 8 keys → 12 over 11 → 12 over 11**, the three new ones arriving with the drawn low band (item 336's scale effect) and the hammer refit adding and removing none — per row, M8 / +tail / shipped: A6 `match` +8.4/+8.4/+8.2, C5 `jitter` +5.4/+6.5/+6.6, C7 `jitter` +4.4/+5.2/+5.2, A2 `jitter` +4.1/+4.9/+4.9, D#6 `decay` −4.4/−4.6/−4.7, F#7 `match` +4.7/+4.7/+4.6, F#5 `jitter` −/+4.6/+4.6, D#5 `jitter` −/−4.5/−4.5, B6 `jitter` −/+4.4/+4.4, D#7 `match` +4.3/+4.3/+4.2, A2 `centroid` −4.3/−4.2/−4.2, F#2 `centroid` −4.3/−4.1/−4.1. Under the policy the file carries **30 `recorded` rows and 58 `transposed — unscored`**, and `match` is a number at **28** of the 30 (C7 and C8 share no partial their two spectra can both read and print `-`), median **5.05** dB, range 1.9 to **22.8** at A6. **Brilliance**, phrase means engine−reference: 2-6 kHz **−3.47 / −3.81 / −3.82**, 6-12 kHz **−0.80 / −1.16 / −1.73**, tilt **+0.06 / −0.06 / −0.14** dB/oct, against reference floors of +0.65 / +2.47 / +0.34 that are the same in all three columns. The 0.57 dB the hammer takes out of 6-12 kHz is item 339's own arithmetic — that burst is −21.0 dB against the engine's own content at 4 kHz and −14.4 at 8 kHz and nothing at 62 Hz — showing up on a board that was not consulted when it was fitted. **The balance board re-run** (`piano-tuner noise` on the shipped preset): engine minus reference median **−0.77** dB, mean |·| **4.46** over 150 notes, the same with the event silenced **+7.28 / 8.78**, by velocity 24…110 **+0.31, −0.55, −1.12, −2.28, −1.37**, 126 of 150 readings invert (24 `floor`, 0 `ceiling`), scatter about the line 10.53 dB, and the correction it asks of its own output is **−0.00 dB with `velocity_db` 43.90 → 43.90** — item 340's re-entrancy, re-measured rather than quoted.

344. **The two nonlinearities, the pins, the fuzz and the clock, all re-read on the shipped instrument, and the two doc comments that were carrying stale numbers under live assertions are corrected.** **The master safety limiter** (`forensics/src/bin/limiter_probe.rs`, raw render, per channel, threshold −1.00 dBFS): **not one sample** of the twelve benchmark renders is over it on either shipped preset, and the least headroom is **6.92 dB** — `arpeggio_dynamics` on `presets/salamander-c5.toml`, against 15.23 dB for the same phrase on the default. Split: **7.16 → 6.93 → 6.92**, so the drawn low band spent 0.23 dB of it and the hammer refit 0.01. `DECISIONS.md` 42's four anchors over all 88 keys are unmoved by this milestone to a hundredth: mezzo-forte C4 −31.53 dBFS in its channel (30.53 dB of headroom), single fortissimo C4 −19.31 (18.31), loudest single strike anywhere **key 106 A#7 at −7.52** (6.52), ten-note ff chord −0.78 (−0.22 dB, **4 samples inside the limiter — which is the calibration clause and is meant to be there**), the built-in demo peak −5.88 with 0 samples, and 30 s of dense random playing peak −6.95 with 0 samples and a worst channel DC of −160.3 dBFS. **The damper felt**, `realism::note_off_hf` pooled over the 73 readable note-offs of the six phrases: engine **mean −1.27, p90 +3.70, worst +11.13, 6.8 % over +6 dB** against the recordings' −1.16 / +0.63 / +1.00 / 0 %, both gates passing with 1.27 dB and 3.2 points of margin. **The whole of that move is the hammer**: M8 and the tail-only preset both read −1.56 / +3.14 / +11.14 / 4.1 % to the digit, and `[noise.strike]` coming down takes energy out of the *reference* half of a before/after ratio at exactly the phrases whose note-offs sit close to a strike (`staccato` −3.5 → −2.6 mean, `arpeggio_dynamics` worst −0.5 → +1.8). The note-off thump itself still lands where the recordings put it: key 21 −37.9 dB against −37.3 asked, key 57 −31.2/−30.2, key 60 −35.6/−35.4, key 72 −26.3/−25.4, key 96 −34.3/−33.5. **Performance**, release profile on an M4 Pro: the worst case **31.3 %** of one core, the measured preset with its 100 duplex segments and voiced bridge **30.3 %**, a thousand events **29.5 % with the action silenced against 29.8 % with the mechanism and the hammer's noise both voiced (+0.27 points)**, and six duplex segments on every one of the 88 keys 29.4 → **30.5 %** (+1.10), one held note 2.81 → 3.78 %. Every budget is against a gate of 80 % and a design goal of 50 %. **The equivalence pins.** `engine/` and `presets/default.toml` are untouched by this milestone in the literal sense that `git diff` over them is empty; `the_sounding_path_is_what_it_was_before_the_mechanism` still reads **`b77c69714fdb6f21`** and all six tests in `engine/tests/preset.rs` are green. The one place a reader could be misled is `renders/salamander-ab/`, where seven `*_default.wav` files *do* change bytes: `write_matched` gives a group **one** gain, so a changed salamander render moves the file beside it. Measured rather than argued — every changed default file is the same audio times a scalar, **max |ratio ÷ median − 1| = 1.2e-07** (float32 on the write) and the gain itself moved by at most **0.001 dB**; three of the ten are bit-identical. **The reference side never moved anywhere**: all eight `note_*_source.wav`, all eleven `renders/compass/detail/key5*|6*_reference.wav`, and both `ode_*_reference.wav` (whose difference floors at −215 dB, i.e. exactly zero) are bit-identical, which is why `SAMPLER_VERSION` stays at 1 even though `sampler.rs` gained `Instrument::rerouted`. **Determinism.** `COMPASS.md`, `REALISM.md` with all twelve WAVs and six PNGs, and `MELODY.md` with its four WAVs are **byte-identical at `RAYON_NUM_THREADS=1` against 14**, which is the claim `README.md` makes about the batch drivers, checked rather than asserted. **The suite.** `cargo test --release --workspace --no-fail-fast` is **605 green, 1 red**, the red being the documented duplex of item 260, unchanged: engine lib 179, `acceptance` 25, `partials` 9, `preset` 6, `smoke` 8, tuner lib 320, `calibration` 20 + 1 red, `decoding` 3, `estimators` 6, `limiter` 3, `melody` 13, `reference_cache` 5, `tracking` 7, doc-test 1. `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets` and `-p forensics --all-targets` are both clean. **The two stale comments.** `tuner/tests/limiter.rs` claimed a least headroom of **1.60 dB** — a number from before item 277 took the whole instrument 5.19 dB down — and a pooled note-off statistic of −1.17 / +3.51 / +13.59 / 5.5 %, and named A7 as the loudest single strike of the fitted preset on the every-fourth-key grid where it is now F6. All three are corrected in place with the milestone they were measured in. A doc comment beside a live assertion is the one place in this repository where a stale number is not merely untidy: it is the only thing a reader has that says what the gate is worth.

345. **The factory's last two stages are not independent: `noise` moves the render `tail` closed on, so the shipped preset is `tail`'s answer to a hammer that is no longer in it — measured, and deliberately not acted on.** Found by re-running the factory rather than the boards. **`tail` is idempotent to the byte, and that is checked three ways rather than assumed**: `piano-tuner tail --passes 8` from the M8 preset reproduces `presets/salamander-c5.toml` with **0 rows differing**; the same from a save/load round trip of it, 0; and the same from the shipped preset with **only `[noise.strike]` put back to M8's exact bytes**, also **0**. Item 336's claim stands. **What does not stand is running the same command on the preset as it ships.** That moves **all 37 drawn `partial_sigma_scale` rows and none of the 30 fitted ones** — median cell **×1.0041**, p90 ×1.057, max **×1.197**, and three rows change reach (E1 80 → 44 cells, G3 48 → 38, G#3 45 → 36) — and the *second* application is an exact fixed point, 0 rows. The pass loop is not the cause: 16 passes from M8 differ from 8 passes by two rows. **The cause is the recipe's own order.** `tail` measures a correction on the engine's render and `noise` then changes that render by −7.29 dB of burst and a velocity law, and the drawn rows come off a compass-wide **line**, so they move together: a **0.0003 dB** rounding on the six `level_db` anchors is enough to move the same 37 rows by the same order, which is the sensitivity stated as a measurement rather than as a worry. **What it is worth, which is why nothing is refitted**: re-running `tail` on the shipped preset gives `mel` 5.10 → 5.10, `modulation` 3.01 → 3.02, `attack` 4.39 → 4.42, `r`, `release` and all four motion columns unmoved, the compass **12 flags over the same 11 keys**, and the melody gate 0.96 / 0.35 / 3.04 / −1.60 / 0.83 / 1.11 / 3.76 → **0.92 / 0.35 / 2.91 / −1.60 / 0.92 / 1.11 / 3.82**, every column inside its bar both ways and none of them closer to its bar than 1.5×. The difference is inside every gate's own noise, and chasing it is a loop with no criterion for stopping: each `tail` moves the decay, each `noise` re-inverts against the new decay, and neither stage minimises anything the other can see. **The decision is to write the coupling down and leave the preset where it is**, and to say in `README.md`'s recipe that `noise` running last is what makes the shipped `partial_sigma_scale` one application short of the stage's own fixed point. Closing that loop properly means giving the two stages a shared objective, which is the global optimiser `TUNING.md` still calls future work, and it is not worth 0.03 dB of `attack`.
380. *(Integration track; reserved range 380-399.)* **The plugin's C ABI exists and is a `ffi/` workspace member; the shipped library gets its own cargo profile rather than turning the whole workspace's release build into one.** `DISTRIBUTION.md` M0+M1, and the first code in this repository that is not the instrument or its tuner. `ffi/` builds `cdylib` + `staticlib` + `rlib` around fourteen `extern "C"` entry points (nine that do something, five that answer questions), with `cbindgen` generating `ffi/include/piano_emulator.h` from the Rust doc comments so the Swift of M2 cannot drift from what it links. Two deviations from the sketch in `DISTRIBUTION.md` §Architecture, both narrowing: the fallible calls return a `pe_status` enum rather than a bare `int32_t`, and there is a `pe_last_error` beside them, because `Preset::validate`'s whole value is that it *names the offending field* (item 52) and an error code throws that away. Three additions: `pe_abi_version`, so a dynamically loaded library can be checked before it is called; `pe_is_bypassed` and `pe_host_sample_rate`, so a test — and later a UI — can assert which side of item 381 it is on. **`panic = "abort"` is a `[profile.dist]` that inherits from release, not a change to `[profile.release]`.** Cargo has no per-crate-type panic setting, so the alternative was to make every acceptance number in this repository — the 31-40 % perf worst cases, the limiter probes, the whole release suite — be measured under a different unwinding model for one library's benefit. `cargo build --profile dist -p piano-emulator-ffi` is the shipped artifact (verified: `-C panic=abort` reaches rustc, and the harness built against that dylib renders the same md5 as the debug one). The `catch_unwind` at every entry point is therefore not redundant with it: with `dist` no panic ever reaches the boundary, and without it — the staticlib and the rlib are linked into binaries that unwind — the guard turns one into `PE_ERR_PANIC` instead of undefined behaviour. `ffi/tests/header.rs` fails if the profile is deleted, because nothing in a normal build would notice.

381. *(Integration track; reserved range 380-399.)* **The host-rate boundary is a branch, not a ratio: at exactly 48 kHz `pe_render` *is* `Engine::process`, byte for byte, and every other rate goes through a `SincFixedOut`.** `DISTRIBUTION.md` M0 option (b), and the reason the bypass is a correctness argument rather than a saving is measured here: a unity-ratio polyphase sinc is **transparent** — `-0.000 dB` on a 15 kHz tone — so it would be easy to argue that leaving it in costs nothing. It is not bit-exact, and that is what matters. Its residual against the direct signal is **-42.3 dB**, essentially all of it an 81 ns sub-sample delay, and a 48 kHz host running it would be playing an instrument that no number in `PHYSICS.md`, no render hash in this file and no acceptance test describes any more, with nothing to say so. So the test is a null test on **bit patterns** (`bypass.rs::pe_render_at_48_khz_is_byte_identical_to_the_engine`): the same preset, the same event script, request lengths 1, 64, 100, 128, 333, 512 and 4096 — the non-multiples of `BLOCK` are where item 47's remainder FIFO has to hold the two streams together — and `f32::to_bits` equality on both channels, because `-0.0 == 0.0` is the wrong answer to "is this the same audio". The bypass is taken on **exact** equality with 48000 Hz, never on a tolerance: 48000.5 Hz is a host we resample for, since reinterpreting our samples at a rate that is nearly right is precisely what item 17 refused. The same script through `pe_post_event` instead of `pe_event` is bit-identical too, which is the SPSC path (the standalone app's) proved against the direct one (the plugin's), and `realtime.rs` counts **zero heap allocations** across 200 render calls at 48/44.1/96/88.2 kHz with a counting global allocator, block sizes varying call to call including ones larger than the `max_frames` the engine was built for.

382. *(Integration track; reserved range 380-399.)* **The alias floor is measured at -104 dB worst case, and the partial cap `DISTRIBUTION.md` asked for at 44.1 kHz is refused, because the arithmetic says nothing folds.** M0's gate was "alias floor < -100 dB at 44.1/96". Measured with a *stepped* sweep — one steady tone at a time, Blackman-Harris windowed FFT, every bin outside a 64-bin guard around the tone counted as folded — because a continuous chirp's aliases fold to a line that crosses the chirp and separating them afterwards is guesswork. Over 14 tones from 50 Hz to 23.5 kHz: worst **-104.0 dB at 44.1 kHz** (a 20 kHz tone) and **-104.3 dB at 96 kHz** (21.6 kHz), with most of the sweep 110-140 dB down; a tone *above* the host's Nyquist, where every joule out is one that folded, comes back at **-108.1 dB** (22.1 kHz) and below. **The partial cap is not needed and would be a voicing change.** `MAX_PARTIAL_RATIO * SAMPLE_RATE` puts the highest partial any string is given at **21.6 kHz, under 44.1 kHz's 22.05 kHz Nyquist**, so no partial can fold at any host rate whatever the filter does. What does live above 22.05 kHz is the mechanism noise and the master soft-clip's products, and the measurement above is what happens to those: filtered, not folded. Capping partials at ~19.5 kHz as the document suggested would either re-voice the instrument at 48 kHz too — the one thing M0 exists to prevent — or make the partial layout depend on the host rate, which is option (a) arriving through the back door. Filter as shipped: 256 taps, `f_cutoff` 0.95, oversampling 256, cubic, Blackman-Harris; **0.70 % of one core at 44.1 kHz and 1.17 % at 96 kHz** against the engine's 31-40 % (items 25b, 37), so the document's "well under 1 %" holds at 44.1 and is missed by a sixth of a percent at 96, where there are twice as many output frames per second of music.

383. *(Integration track; reserved range 380-399.)* **The C harness renders what `cargo run -- render` renders, sample for sample: md5 `f0fcb07999c00ca60110cd537de8f09e`.** M1's "done when", and the one test in this milestone that is written in C, because everything above M1 is C and Swift calling this ABI and a header no compiler has ever read is a header that does not compile. `ffi/harness/render.c` (570 lines) parses a standard MIDI file, builds the tempo map, drives `pe_event` and writes a 32-bit float WAV; `ffi/harness/phrase.mid` is the fixture, a 141-byte phrase chosen to touch every event the loader reads — real release velocities, a note-on below escapement, a half-pedal, sostenuto, una corda, a tempo change, a note-off written as velocity 0, and a second channel merged into the one piano. **Sample-exactness is a property of the mirror being honest**, and the four places it could have failed are named in the C: the tick-to-second map is evaluated in `double` and *then* narrowed to `float` as `midly`'s is; an event's frame is `RenderEvent::frame`'s round-half-away-from-zero in **float**; the events are stable-sorted by tick and then stable-sorted by frame, in that order, with the original index carried as a tiebreak so `qsort` reproduces a Rust stable sort; and the render is `last_event + 4 s` truncated to whole frames in float. Four renders agree bit for bit — the harness with `presets/default.toml`, the harness with the built-in preset (`-`), the harness routing every event through `pe_post_event` instead, and the CLI — which incidentally proves that `presets/default.toml` still round-trips to `Preset::default()` float for float. The comparison in `ffi/tests/harness.rs` is against the CLI's *own code path* (`midi::load` + `render_to_wav`, which is what `main.rs` calls) rather than against a recorded hash, so it cannot drift away from the thing it checks; the md5 above is over the WAV payload, since the two writers spell the RIFF header differently (hound writes a 68-byte header, the C writes 44).

384. *(Integration track; reserved range 380-399.)* **`SincFixedOut` delays everything by `ratio - 1` output frames, and that is pinned rather than corrected — the same call item 64 made for `SincFixedIn`.** Measured with a symmetric raised-cosine pulse and an energy centroid: **-0.0923 input frames (-1.92 us) at 44.1 kHz** and **+0.4961 (+10.34 us) at 96 kHz**, and the same numbers to six decimals at input positions 20 k, 50 k and 120 k frames apart and at two different chunk sizes. That constancy is the whole measurement: an offset that does not move with position is a *delay*, not dispersion and not drift, and a delay of under one engine sample on both channels equally is inaudible. The structure of it is `1 - 1/ratio` input frames — `SincFixedOut` advances its interpolation index before it emits a sample rather than after, so output frame `j` reads input time `(j+1)/ratio - 1` — plus **81 ns** of the same rounding item 64 measured as 1.8 us. Correcting it means patching the dependency; correcting it *wrongly*, which is the mistake item 64 documents, costs milliseconds instead of microseconds. The related number, and the one that is easy to mistake for latency: the engine runs **128 engine frames (2.67 ms) ahead** of the host clock at every resampled rate, because the source is a generator and the filter answers its own look-ahead by pulling more frames rather than by delaying what it has. Nothing is reported to the host and nothing is compensated; it shows up as *when an event lands*, not as delay. `DISTRIBUTION.md` estimated 1.3 ms from a 128-tap filter — the taps are 256, and item 382 is what they bought.

385. *(Integration track; reserved range 380-399.)* **`pe_event_t` is four 32-bit fields, and the velocity is 32 bits today so that MIDI 2.0 is not an ABI break.** **Superseded in its velocity clause by item 387**, which widened the engine's `Event` two items later in this same range: the field is no longer "32 bits ahead of a `u8` engine" but 32 bits carrying the two lanes of a `u16`, values above 127 are no longer clamped to 127, and `PE_ABI_VERSION` is 2. Everything below about the *shape* of the struct — 16 bytes, no padding, no pointers, and the fact that the widening cost no offset and no recompile — is exactly what the foresight bought and still stands. `SHIPPING.md` §4 wants the engine's `Event` velocity widened `u8 -> u16` for the SL88 MK2, one of the few controllers that actually sends UMP; that change belongs to the engine and had not been made when this item was written. The ABI is shaped for it anyway: `{kind, key, vel, value}`, 16 bytes, no padding, no pointers, so it can be built in a render callback and copied into a queue, and a caller that sends a 16-bit velocity tomorrow does not recompile against a different struct. Until the engine widens, **a velocity over 127 is clamped to 127 rather than dropped or wrapped**: 65535 has to mean *loud*, and silently rescaling it would make today's meaning differ from tomorrow's for values nobody sends yet. Everything else that crosses from C is range-checked at the boundary rather than trusted — a key that is not a `u8`, an unrecognised `kind`, a pedal position that is not a finite number are ignored; a pedal position outside 0..1 is clamped; a key that is a valid MIDI note but not one of the 88 goes through and is dropped at the voice lookup, exactly as a MIDI file's out-of-compass note is. **Sub-block event placement is still not done and this signature does not need to change for it** (item 55, `DISTRIBUTION.md` M2): onsets quantise to the 128-frame block, 2.7 ms, and the header says so per function rather than leaving a host to find out.

386. *(Integration track; reserved range 380-399.)* **Host state is the preset text, and `pe_reset` flushes the engine's remainder FIFO — found because the reset test heard 136 frames of the old performance.** `pe_save_state` writes the current preset as TOML and `pe_load_preset_toml` reads it back, which is `DISTRIBUTION.md`'s answer for `fullState`: 20 KB of text, 5.7 KB gzipped, is free in a Logic project and a project saved today still opens when the preset files have moved on. The size-then-write protocol is `snprintf`'s — a null buffer or too small a `cap` returns the size needed and writes nothing — and the round trip is checked by rendering the same events through the original engine and through a fresh one loaded from its saved bytes, bit for bit. **The reset bug is the interesting half.** `Event::AllOff` silences every voice, the bus, the board and the pedal trays, but item 47's remainder FIFO holds up to a block of audio that was rendered *before* it, and at a host rate that is not a multiple of 128 there is nearly always some: a reset at 44.1 kHz left 136 frames of the chord still sounding. `pe_reset` therefore renders one 128-frame block into a discarded buffer after the `AllOff`, which drains the FIFO with the silence that now replaces it, and only then resets the boundary filter. It deliberately does **not** reset the engine's frame counter, which seeds the mechanism noise: a transport stop is not a new piano, and two identical gestures a minute apart are not supposed to make identical key noise.

346. **The loss gets a stereo term, and it is the first number ever put under the largest difference the chain experiment measured.** `realism::stereo_image`, `realism::stereo_columns`, `realism::stereo_report`, and their columns on three surfaces. Item 317 (a) is the instruction — *give the loss a stereo term first, because a stage built to fix something nothing scores is a stage nobody can regress* — and it is the one prerequisite that item names for `PHYSICS.md` §8. **What is measured, per band, on the two channels rather than on the mono sum every other metric in this repository is computed from.** The bands are `estimate::chain::SPATIAL_BANDS` exactly — 63-125, 125-250, 250-500, 500 Hz-2 kHz, 2-6 kHz, 6-12 kHz — so that every number is comparable with the table item 314 published. Four of the six are octaves and two are not — 500 Hz-2 kHz spans two and 2-6 kHz a little over one and a half — because the useful statistic up there is a register: above 2 kHz a note has forty partials in one octave and the correlation of any one of them is the correlation of its own beating. Four numbers in each: **`r@0`**, the normalised interchannel correlation at lag zero and **signed**, because a pan-pot pins it at +1 and an anti-phase pair reads −1 and a metric that reported |r| would call those two the same thing — which is precisely the mistake available here, since the engine's defect is +0.9 at one end of the compass and −0.6 at the other; **`peak |r|` and its lag** over ±5 ms, which is 1.7 m of air, wider than any pair and than any lid reflection, and narrow enough that a partial's own period does not turn the search into a periodicity measurement above 200 Hz; and **mid over side energy in dB**, `(L+R)/2` against `(L−R)/2`, which is the same fact as `r@0` for a level-balanced pair and is *not* the same fact when the two channels differ in level — pinned by `the_mid_side_ratio_sees_a_level_imbalance_that_the_correlation_cannot`, where a pan-pot at 0.6 is still +1.0000 correlated and reads +12.04 dB. **A band is read only where it holds more than −60 dB of the whole signal's energy**, on *all three* signals of a comparison so the sides are always the same set: below that a correlation is a ratio of two noise floors, and A0's 6-12 kHz band and C8's 63-125 Hz band are both such a band. The whole thing is one FFT pair per signal with the cross-spectrum accumulated over the band's own bins and the four energies taken by Parseval on the same bins, so seven bands cost seven inverse transforms and no second copy of a million-sample phrase. **One inherited error is corrected on the way**: `BandCoherence::lag_ms`'s doc said positive meant the left channel leads. The correlation is `c[τ] = Σ_t L[t+τ]·R[t]`, so a right channel that is a delayed left one peaks at *negative* τ, and `estimate::chain`'s own test has read −2.00 ms for a 2 ms delay into the right channel since it was written. Positive means the **right** channel leads. Every lag in item 314 and in the tables below is to be read with that sign.

347. **The failing baseline, quoted in full on three surfaces, and it is item 314's finding reproduced to the third decimal.** The gate's material is the **30 keys the Salamander library actually recorded** (item 328), struck alone at velocity 90 and read over 3.0 s — solo notes rather than phrases, because the geometry this is about is per-key and because a recorded key is the only thing in the library with a *second recording of itself* under it. Engine `presets/salamander-c5.toml`, per band, as `engine r@0 / recording r@0 → |err| against bar`: **63-125 −0.577 / +0.953 → 1.530 against 0.009**; 125-250 +0.198 / −0.115 → 0.313 against 0.120; 250-500 +0.542 / −0.226 → 0.767 against 0.059; 500 Hz-2 kHz +0.711 / −0.002 → 0.713 against 0.102; 2-6 kHz +0.769 / −0.012 → 0.782 against 0.072; **6-12 kHz +0.912 / +0.050 → 0.862 against 0.098**. **Six of six bands red.** Item 314 read −0.577 in the bass and +0.964 at 6-12 kHz on half A's fifteen keys with `chain::stereo_signature`; this reads −0.577 and +0.912 on all thirty with the loss's own code, which is the corroboration that matters — the two measurements share their band edges and their transform and nothing else. The recording's side reproduces too: +0.953 below 125 Hz against 314's +0.945, and its peak |r| runs **0.970 / 0.634 / 0.497 / 0.599 / 0.615 / 0.620 at lags −0.23 / +0.40 / −1.38 / −0.69 / −0.38 / +0.99 ms**, against 314's 0.57-0.65 at −0.23 to +1.98. **Two more things in that table are new and are the mechanism showing itself.** The engine's peak lag in the bass sits at **−4.03 ms**, near the rail of the ±5 ms search, where the recording's sits at −0.23: the FDN does not decorrelate the bass *at a delay*, it decorrelates it at no particular delay, which is what an orthogonal mixing matrix does and what a pair of capsules 12 cm apart does not. And the mid/side ratio in that band is **−5.6 dB in the engine against +12.2 dB in the recording** — the engine's bass is mostly *side* energy where the piano's is almost all mid, an 18 dB inversion of the thing a mono fold-down keeps. **On the six phrases** (`REALISM.md` Columns S, the held-out reading, where the floor partner is the neighbouring velocity layer of the whole performance): −0.550 / +0.959, +0.657 / +0.078, +0.762 / +0.348, +0.695 / +0.076, +0.617 / +0.344, +0.791 / −0.065, **six of six red** with errors 1.509 / 0.579 / 0.415 / 0.619 / 0.273 / 0.856 against bars 0.009 / 0.066 / 0.089 / 0.153 / 0.082 / 0.024. The 6-12 kHz band reads on **three of six** phrases and the reason is worth keeping: the engine's own content there falls under the −60 dB floor on `chords_pedal`, `excerpt` and `scale_mf` (−71.6, −60.3, −65.0 dB of the phrase's energy against the recordings' −59.3, −56.4, −57.8), which is a register-dependent darkness the phrase board's mel column averages away. **Per key** (`COMPASS.md`'s stereo line, r@0 in the band each key's fundamental falls in): **46 of 88 keys have the engine on the opposite side of zero from the recording**, median r@0 engine **+0.709** against recording **+0.396**, median |Δ| **0.784**. The line is not scored against a key's neighbours the way the seven mono metrics are, on purpose — where the microphones were is a fact that changes smoothly down the compass, not something one key can be an outlier of — and what it shows instead is that the engine's bass is not even *consistently* wrong: A0 −0.653, C#1 +0.701, D1 −0.790, G1 +0.850, A#1 −0.976 against a recording that reads +0.99, +0.95, +0.98, +0.99, +0.96 at the same keys. The FDN's tap phase is a function of the key's own pan position and delay-line lengths, and it lands wherever it lands.

348. **The bars are the recording disagreeing with itself, and the pooled scatter is deliberately not one of them.** Per band the score is `|engine r@0 − recording r@0|` on medians over the items, and the bar is `max(floor, scatter/sqrt(n)) · 1.25`. **`floor`** is the identical median taken on a *second recording* — for a key, its neighbouring velocity layer; for a phrase, the whole performance played out of the layer next door — and it is small and steady, **0.007 / 0.027 / 0.039 / 0.022 / 0.016 / 0.016** on the notes and 0.001 to 0.027 on the phrases, which is itself a finding: two takes of one piano agree about their stereo image to a hundredth of a correlation, so this is a quantity worth gating at all. **`scatter`** is the robust sigma of the recording's own r@0 across the items, and it is large in the middle bands — 0.53 at 125-250, 0.45 at 500 Hz-2 kHz — because the keys sit in different places relative to the microphones. **The first version of this gate used `max(floor, scatter)`, the construction `estimate::melody`'s population bar uses, and it was wrong here for a reason worth writing down.** With the pooled scatter as the bar, 125-250 and 500 Hz-2 kHz **passed** — the engine reading +0.71 where the recording reads −0.00, excused by the fact that the recording's own keys scatter by 0.45 about that zero. But a recording's r@0 moving across the compass is not the reference being a moving target; it is a *thing the engine is meant to reproduce*. The melody gate's population term is the right shape for a question about one note standing out from a register, and the wrong shape for a question about a whole band's image. So the scatter enters as `scatter/sqrt(n)` — the precision with which n items pin a median, not a licence to miss it — and the bars land at **0.009 to 0.120**, all six red and all six meetable: matching a band's median correlation to within a tenth is a reasonable thing to ask of two virtual capsules with a delay between them. The **per-item distance is reported beside the pooled one** and is not gated, so a model that fixes a band's median without fixing its image is visible as such: on the notes it reads 1.498 / 0.483 / 0.383 / 0.509 / 0.593 / 0.799 against take-pair floors of 0.004 / 0.030 / 0.006 / 0.010 / 0.017 / 0.013. Nothing in any bar is a function of the engine, which is the property that makes it a bar rather than a description, and `the_stereo_bar_is_built_out_of_the_reference_alone` asserts exactly that by moving the engine from +0.90 to −0.60 and checking the bar to twelve decimals.

349. **The gate is red on purpose and it comes with two green controls, because a gate that can only fail is no more a gate than one that never does.** `tuner/tests/stereo.rs`, four tests, one red. **The red** is `the_engines_stereo_image_is_the_recordings_in_every_band`, item 347's table, and it stands until `PHYSICS.md` §8 lands — the pattern of items 298 and 330, a gate written from a measurement and left failing rather than skipped. **The first control**, `the_recording_passes_its_own_bar_in_every_band`, puts the recording on the engine's side of the comparison: every band passes, every band is readable on at least one key, and every bar is a positive finite number. It is not the tautology it looks like — the floor and the scatter are measured on *other* signals, so a bar of zero anywhere, or a band nothing could read, fails it. **The second control is the one that matters**, `a_pan_potted_copy_of_the_recording_fails_above_the_bass`: the recording's own mono sum put back into two channels, which is `soundboard::pan_for_key`'s construction applied to the piano's own sound — same spectrum, same envelope, same everything every mono metric on every board measures, and a stereo image that is +1.000 in every band. **Every band above 125 Hz goes red and the bass does not**, which is the shape of the finding stated as an assertion rather than as prose: the bass is where a pan-pot is nearly right, because one wavefront is one wavefront and the recording reads +0.95 there too, and everything above it is where the 12 cm of air lives. That test is also the proof that the column is not smuggling in a mono difference: nothing but the channel assignment changed. Ten more tests in `realism.rs` pin the metric itself — a pan-pot reads +1 at lag zero in every band, an inverted channel reads −1 and is all side, a 2 ms delay shows up as a peak at −2.00 ms, **a 0.354 ms delay (12 cm of air) correlates the bass above 0.9 and leaves 6-12 kHz under 0.5**, which is the recording's own shape produced out of a delay and no room at all, independent channels read zero and split their energy evenly, an empty band is not readable, and the fundamental-band lookup clamps at both ends so A0's 27.5 Hz reads in 63-125 where its second partial is.

350. **What moved and what did not.** **No preset moved and no engine file was touched**: `presets/` is byte-identical, the sounding-path fingerprint is untouched because nothing in `engine/` changed, `render demo`, the compass sweep and the salamander demo are the same bytes, and the performance numbers are unchanged by construction — every line of this milestone is in `tuner/`, none of it is in the audio path, and the boards are the only things that render. **The standing boards are identical where they should be**, which is the control: `REALISM.md`'s mel reads 5.03 / 5.24 / 4.09 / 5.03 / 6.36 / 4.83 per phrase and the motion columns A1 1.36, A2 0.76, B1 1.49 dB, B2 1.071 — item 343's shipped column to the last digit — and `COMPASS.md` flags the same **12 over 11 keys** with the same twelve `z` values (A6 `match` +8.2, C5 `jitter` +6.6, C7 +5.2, A2 +4.9, D#6 `decay` −4.7, F#7 `match` +4.6, F#5 +4.6, D#5 −4.5, B6 +4.4, D#7 `match` +4.2, A2 `centroid` −4.2, F#2 −4.1). Adding a stereo section to a report changes nothing the report already said. **The gates.** `cargo test --release --workspace --no-fail-fast`: **+13 green and +1 red against item 342's 605 and 1** — ten new unit tests in `realism.rs` and three green controls in `tuner/tests/stereo.rs`, and the one new red is the stereo gate itself, named in `README.md` beside the duplex. The delta is quoted rather than a total because a second crate was landing in the same tree while this ran and its own test count moved between two runs of the suite; every one of the 13 and the 1 is in `tuner/` and is accounted for by name. `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets` is clean. **What this milestone deliberately does not do is touch the renderer.** The soundboard's taps and `pan_for_key` are exactly where they were; the point of doing the loss first is that the next milestone's mechanism has something to be measured against that was not built to flatter it, and this table is that something.

351. **`PHYSICS.md` §8 is built, and its whole structure is one sentence: the microphone pair replaces the *difference* signal and leaves the *sum* exactly where the pan-pot put it.** `engine/src/soundboard.rs`'s `Mics`, behind `[voicing.mics]`. Two virtual capsules over the string band, on one axis — the keyboard's — because that is the axis the engine already carries: `add_voice`'s `pan` argument is `(2·key_position − 1)·0.6`, a normalised lateral position, and `voicing.polarization_pan_spread` displaces it, so no signature changes and every source keeps the place it always had. A source at pan `p` sits at `x = p·span`; the capsules sit at `(∓spacing/2, height)` above it, left over the bass; and the three numbers that follow are `d_L = hypot(x + spacing/2, height)`, `d_R = hypot(x − spacing/2, height)` and `Δ = (d_L − d_R)/c`. The gains are inverse-distance normalised to unit power, `u_L² + u_R² = 1`, which is the equal-power pan's own identity; the common delay is **dropped** — it is a latency, every recording in the library is trimmed to its own onset (item 315), and nothing here can measure it — so only the farther capsule is delayed, by `|Δ|` samples with linear interpolation between two taps. Then, per source: `mid = (g_L + g_R)/2 · x`, which is the pan-pot's own fold-down unchanged, `side = width/2 · (u_L·x(t−δ_L) − u_R·x(t−δ_R))`, which is the geometry, and `L = mid + side`, `R = mid − side`. **A bass key is nearly equidistant relative to a bass wavelength and stays coherent; a treble key is off-axis and its millisecond of Δ is most of a treble period, so it decorrelates.** That is the measured shape produced by geometry rather than asserted, and it is the exact reverse of both constructions it replaces — a pan-pot is +1 at every frequency, an orthogonal tap pair is 0 at every frequency, and neither can be a *frequency-dependent* coherence. On the shipped geometry the compass spans **±1.14 ms** of interchannel delay (4.5 dB of interchannel level at the same key), against the recording's own measured peak-correlation lags of −1.38 to +0.99 ms.

352. **The board's diffuse field gets the other half, and the curve it gets is the classical one.** The FDN's two orthogonal output taps are re-read as their own sum and difference — the patterns are `(L±R)/2`, `[1,0,0,−1,1,0,0,−1]` and `[0,−1,1,0,0,−1,1,0]`, disjoint in support and therefore uncorrelated — and the difference alone is highpassed: shared by both capsules at low frequency, orthogonal at high. The coherence that comes out is `|LP|²/(2 − |LP|²)` with `|LP|² = 1/(1 + (f/f_s)²)`, and `f_s` is **not a free number**: the spatial coherence of an isotropic diffuse field between two omnis `d` apart is `sin(kd)/kd` (Cook et al., JASA 27, 1955), one half at `kd = 1.8955`; equating the two half-coherence points gives `f_s = 0.4266·c/d`, which is `MIC_DIFFUSE_POLE_K`. The one dimensionless number beside it, `diffuse_coherence`, says how much *less* isotropic the board's own field is than a room's — it is the near field of one large plate, mode-controlled below ~500 Hz and radiating as an ensemble of small independent sources only above the 1–1.6 kHz radiation transition (Suzuki, JASA 80(6) 1573, 1986), so it stays organised across the capsules to a higher frequency than a superposition of plane waves from every direction would. Fitted, it lands at **5.0**, i.e. a corner at **1463 Hz** — the radiation transition, which is where the physics says the field stops being one thing and starts being many. The **polarization spread survives as part of the image** and needed no code: the two planes of one key are added at two positions, so they carry two different `Δ` as well as two different gains, and because the vertical decays several times faster than the horizontal, a single note's interchannel delay and level both *move while it rings* — the drift `estimate::directivity` fits, now expressed in the image and not only in the balance.

353. **The mono discipline is a property of the construction and not a tolerance that was met.** Every scoreboard in this repository scores the mono sum, deliberately — `REALISM.md`'s own header says a stereo distance would mostly measure the recording's microphones — so a stage that moved `(L + R)/2` would move every board at once and nothing would be comparable across the change. `L = mid + side`, `R = mid − side` makes `(L + R)/2 = mid` **identically**, for every source, every pan and every setting of the geometry, and the same decomposition on the board field makes its sum bit-for-bit the mono the orthogonal tap pair folded down to. What is left between the two sums is `f32` rounding in the master chain's own recursions, which reach the same number along two different sequences of samples: on the board directly, over three geometries spanning the whole schema and seven pan positions, **−113 to −118 dB of RMS, and exactly zero** in the one case where the two arithmetics coincide (a centred source with the board field off). End to end on the shipped preset's demo the six scoreboard bands read **0.000000, 0.000000, 0.000000, 0.000000, 0.000000, −0.000001 dB** and the broadband −0.000000, with the difference signal **−124.4 dB** under the mono peak and −123.1 dB under its energy — against a stated discipline of 0.5 dB. `tuner/tests/stereo.rs::the_mono_fold_down_and_the_preset_without_the_section_are_both_unmoved` is that gate, and it first asserts that the *image* moved by more than 10⁻³, because a mono proof on a stage that does nothing proves nothing. The one caveat is stated rather than hidden: the guarantee is linear, and the safety limiter is not. Per-channel level rises about 1.6 dB (the pair's sum and difference both carry energy where the pan-pot's difference carried little), so the worst-case glissando's peak goes **0.3119 → 0.3322** against a threshold of 0.891 — 8.6 dB of headroom, and the mono identity holds as long as it is not spent.

354. **Absent means the old model, proved by rendering rather than by reasoning (item 103).** `[voicing.mics]` is `Option<MicVoicing>`, `#[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]`; with it absent `Soundboard::new` builds no geometry, `add_voice` runs the same two multiply-accumulates it always ran, and the FDN's `match self.side_b { None => (l, r), ... }` hands the orthogonal taps out untouched — the same arithmetic, not an equivalent one. Three md5s say so: `presets/default.toml`'s demo is **8f98c9cf1f93b98f0e2bdaed7e07a324** and its compass sweep **5bdb9bd2befb9eaa1370be1ac569ed05**, byte-identical to the engine built from the commit before this work, and `presets/salamander-c5.toml` **with the seven new lines deleted** renders **92b44aeecb549381827ec6ae1138dbbb**, byte-identical to the old file's own render. The schema is five numbers, three lengths and two ratios, each bounded and validated in both crates' copies: `spacing_m` in `(0, 1.0]` — a bound on the engine's state as much as on the taste, since `spacing/c` is the longest interchannel delay the stage can be asked for and `MIC_TAIL` is sized from it at compile time — `height_m` in `[0.02, 3.0]`, `span_m` in `[0.05, 3.0]`, `width` in `[0, 2]`, `diffuse_coherence` in `[0.25, 8]`. Twenty-three new rejection cases across the two `both_crates_refuse_the_same_broken_voicing` lists, and both rails of every bound asserted to be an instrument, because a bound that refuses the corner by refusing everything near it is not a bound.

355. **The geometry was fitted, and the thing that fitted the spacing was the recording's own lag.** The gate's material, the 30 recorded keys at velocity 90, swept over the five numbers. The answer is `spacing_m 0.5, height_m 0.45, span_m 1.0, width 1.5, diffuse_coherence 5.0`: **a spaced omni pair half a metre apart and 45 cm over the strings of an instrument whose string band is 1.2 m wide at the keys** (the compass reaches `±0.6·span`; the rest of the range is what the polarization spread displaces into). Half a metre is not the "about 12 cm" the gate's own header guessed, and the correction is a measurement rather than a preference: item 314's peak |r| sits at lags **up to 1.98 ms**, and the per-band medians of the recording run **−1.38 to +0.99 ms**, which is 0.34–0.68 m of air. Twelve centimetres was never measured — it is the height above the strings in the library's description, and a 40–50 cm AB spacing over a grand is ordinary practice. `width` at 1.5 says the lid and the board's directivity put back half again the difference two omnis in free field would see; it scales the side and only the side, so it cannot touch the fold-down at any value. What the fit is *not* is a free-for-all: `spacing` and `diffuse_coherence` each land at an interior optimum with a physical reading (1.14 ms of compass-wide ITD; a 1463 Hz coherence corner), and the two that were swept and left round — height and span — are the pair's position over a real piano.

356. **What it is worth, on all three surfaces.** **The gate** (`tuner/tests/stereo.rs`, 30 recorded keys at v90), engine r@0 before → after against the recording, per band: `63-125` **−0.577 → +0.916** (recording +0.953), `125-250` +0.198 → +0.829 (−0.115), `250-500` +0.542 → +0.576 (−0.226), `500-2k` +0.711 → **+0.020** (−0.002), `2k-6k` +0.769 → **+0.057** (−0.012), `6k-12k` **+0.912 → +0.008** (+0.050). Six of six red becomes **three of six**, the three that pass being the three the geometry owns, and the summed distance 4.967 → 1.876. Two of item 347's other findings close with them: the engine's bass peak |r| no longer **rails at −4.03 ms** — it reads 0.958 at **−0.08 ms** against the recording's 0.970 at −0.23, i.e. an image with a lag rather than a decorrelation at no particular delay — and the bass mid/side goes **−5.6 dB → +12.1** against the recording's +12.2, closing an 18 dB inversion to 0.1 dB. **`REALISM.md` Columns S** (six phrases): |err| 1.509/0.579/0.415/0.619/0.273/0.856 → **0.149/0.433/0.215/0.028/0.262/0.127**, six red to five, summed 4.251 → 1.214. **`COMPASS.md`**: keys on the opposite side of zero from the recording **46 of 88 → 29**, median r@0 +0.709 → **+0.448** against the recording's +0.396, median |Δ| 0.784 → **0.419**. And the mono boards are where item 343 left them, to the digit: mel 5.03 / 5.24 / 4.09 / 5.03 / 6.36 / 4.83, motion A1 1.36, A2 0.76, B1 1.49 dB, B2 1.070, `COMPASS.md` 12 flags over 11 keys with the same twelve z values.

357. **What is left is a size rather than a sign, and it is in the three bands where no two-point geometry can help — which is the finding, not the excuse.** The recording reads **+0.953 below 125 Hz and −0.115 one octave above it**, and its own velocity-layer twin agrees to 0.007 and 0.027, so both numbers are real and reproducible. No spatial coherence function can do that: `sin(kd)/kd` falls from 0.95 to zero over a factor of **6.5 in frequency**, a pure interchannel delay `cos(ωΔ)` over a factor of 5, and any mixture of them lies between — a fall of 0.95 to zero across **one octave** is not a two-point coherence at all. Fitted against it, the pole of the board field's curve is pulled up until the bass is nearly right and the octave above it is then necessarily wrong; that is exactly what the residual is (`63-125` |err| 0.037 against a bar of 0.009, `125-250` 0.944 against 0.120, `250-500` 0.802 against 0.059). Two candidates, and neither is in scope. (a) **It is not the note.** For most of the 30 keys the 63–125 Hz band holds no partial at all — it is whatever the recording has down there — and in a real room that is stage rumble and the room's own modes below its Schroeder frequency, which two microphones see coherently and which §9 is refused from modelling by item 315. (b) **It is not a point source.** Below ~500 Hz the board is mode-controlled, its modes have nodal lines, and two capsules on opposite sides of one puts that mode *anti-phase* between them — a mechanism that produces negative interchannel correlation in exactly the 125–500 Hz range where the recording has it, and one that a two-point free-field geometry has no term for. Building (b) would mean giving the body-mode bank its own per-mode interchannel sign, which is a plausible-sounding invention of exactly the kind `PHYSICS.md` §8 warns against without measured directivity, and the TU Berlin database still contains no piano. **The gate therefore stays red on three bands and green on three**, and what it now measures is that the instrument's presentation has the recording's *sign* everywhere and the recording's *size* above 500 Hz.

358. **The gates, the budget, and what moved.** `cargo test --release --workspace --no-fail-fast`: this track's delta is **+9 green and no new red** — seven unit tests in `soundboard` (the mono identity over three geometries × seven pans, the taps' equal power and delay bound, the rendered delay against the geometry it was asked for, the coherent-bass/decorrelated-treble inversion with the pan-pot beside it as the control that reads +0.999, the diffuse field's low-shared/high-orthogonal split with the orthogonal tap pair beside it, `width` narrowing the image monotonically, and the two polarizations arriving from two places), one in `preset` (the neutrality contract and the round trip, both rails included), and one in `tuner/tests/stereo.rs` (the end-to-end mono and neutrality gate); plus one `#[ignore]`d sweep instrument, `mic_geometry_sweep`, which asserts nothing and is how the five numbers were found. The stereo gate stays the one documented red this track owns. `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets` is clean. **Perf**: the 88-voice pedalled glissando on the measured preset, best of five, **30.73 % → 31.38 % of one core** — 0.65 points, 2.1 % relative, against a 50 % budget — which is what a multiply per sample for the mid, two for a fractionally-delayed side and one one-pole on the board's difference costs. **Files**: `engine/src/{soundboard,preset}.rs`, one line of `engine/src/engine.rs` (`Soundboard::with_mics`), `tuner/src/preset.rs` (the mirrored schema and its bounds), `tuner/src/tools/{bench,compass}.rs` (the two prose paragraphs that described the inversion in the present tense), `tuner/tests/stereo.rs`, seven lines of `presets/salamander-c5.toml`, and the regenerated boards. `presets/default.toml` is untouched and so is every one of its renders.

359. *(Stereo quality track; reserved range 346-379.)* **`[voicing.mics]` is now fitted rather than chosen: `piano-tuner mics`, `estimate::mics`, and a preset whose five microphone numbers each came out of a measurement.** Item 355 found the five numbers by sweeping them over the gate's own material with an `#[ignore]`d test — item 358 names `mic_geometry_sweep` as "how the five numbers were found" — on one surface, with two of the five "swept and left round", and with nothing about the recording entering except the range of lags item 314 published. This is the stage that replaces the sweep: a subcommand, a physical inversion off the recording alone, two surfaces, held-out velocities and a self-calibration. **Two halves, split where the model splits.** `--stage geometry` is a **TDOA inversion off the recording alone**, with no engine in the loop: the pair's `spacing_m` and `span_m` from the interchannel delays thirty recorded keys carry, by GCC-PHAT (Knapp & Carter 1976) and weighted least squares over `(spacing, span/height)` in log coordinates, with a Tukey biweight second pass because the material has real sign-flipped outliers. `--stage coherence` and `--stage image` are **render-and-measure**, on `realism::stereo_columns` — how much of the pair's difference reaches the output is a property of the whole chain (how much sound arrives through the diffuse path, what the polarization spread is doing, where the duplex sits) and this crate would have to mirror all of it to predict, which is `estimate::directivity`'s own argument about its own boundary. **The shipped result**, `presets/salamander-c5.toml`, against what item 353 chose: `spacing_m` **0.500 → 0.1263**, `height_m` **0.45 → 0.12**, `span_m` **1.0 → 1.5**, `width` **1.5 → 1.703**, `diffuse_coherence` **5.0 → 7.86**. The pair is four times narrower and four times closer to the strings than the one that was guessed, and both corrections are measurements rather than taste: see 360 and 361.

360. *(Stereo quality track.)* **The one number about this microphone pair that is not an inference is its height, and it is now held there instead of swept and left round.** `data/salamander/readme.txt`, Alexander Holm's original note: *"Two AKG c414 disposed in an AB position ~12cm above the strings"*. Item 355 read it correctly — "twelve centimetres was never measured [as a spacing] — it is the height above the strings in the library's description" — and then set `height_m` to **0.45**, one of the two fields that item's own last sentence calls "swept and left round". A fitted preset should not carry a guessed value in the one place a measured one exists, and the guess was not harmless: at 45 cm the pan-to-position map is gentle enough that the fit has to buy its interchannel delay with a half-metre spacing, which is what item 362 shows the recording's own delays refusing. So `height_m` is **held at 0.12** through every stage and is the only one of the five the search may not move; `span_m` is fitted against it. That is also what makes the inversion well-posed: the delay curve constrains the ratio `span / height` far better than either separately (`estimate::mics`'s header derives why — the curve's *asymptote* is `d/c` and its *knee* is `span/h`, and nothing in it separates a wider instrument from a lower pair), so one of the two has to come from outside the data, and here one of them is written down. "AB position" is a family of setups and not a distance, so the **spacing** is the thing left to invert.

361. *(Stereo quality track.)* **The recording's interchannel delay is measurable only where its two channels are coherent, and that is the bass — 40-160 Hz, not the mid where a direct path is usually read.** The first version of the estimator read the delay the way an acoustician reads a direct path: a short window at the onset, band-limited to 200-4000 Hz. It returned noise — thirty keys, lags scattered uniformly over the whole ±3 ms search, GCC-PHAT peaks of **0.11 to 0.44**. The census (`MIC_CENSUS=1 piano-tuner mics …`, seven bands × four windows, kept in the tool) says why: over **40-160 Hz the peak is 0.85 to 1.00** on the same thirty keys, and it falls off a cliff above it. That is the same fact `realism::stereo_image` reports as a peak |r| of **0.97 in 63-125 Hz against 0.50-0.62 in every band above**, and it is what an AB pair 12 cm over the strings has to look like: down there the two capsules are well inside a wavelength of one extended radiator, and up there each is in the near field of whatever is directly under it and there is no single delay to find. Two consequences, both against the usual practice and both forced: the band is the **bass**, and the window is the **whole note** rather than the onset — one period of A0's second partial is 18 ms, so a window short enough to exclude the board's later field holds no cycles of the thing being measured.

362. *(Stereo quality track.)* **The recording's delays and the recording's coherence ask for two different microphone pairs, and the gap between them is item 357 in numbers.** Item 357 said a two-point geometry cannot fall from +0.95 at 100 Hz to −0.115 one octave up, and named the fall as the thing §8 could not close. Measuring both halves separately puts a number on it. The **delay** inversion, on the recording alone, wants a pair **0.112 m** apart (residual 0.301 ms weighted RMS, against 0.426 ms for no pair at all — it beats the null by 29 %). The **coherence** wants the mid bands decorrelated at 250-500 Hz, which needs a delay of half a period there, which needs **0.6-0.7 m** — five to six times as wide, and the free four-parameter search on the coherence alone lands at 0.66 m with the bass ruined to +0.494 against the recording's +0.953. They cannot both be had. What the fit does about it is item 363; what makes the answer believable is that the fit, run on the coherence with the delays as one term among many, **came back to 0.1263 m** — 12 % from the independent inversion, and a delay residual of **0.303 ms against the inversion's own best of 0.301**. Two measurements that need not have agreed, agreeing to a thousandth of a millisecond. For the record, the pair item 353 chose reads **0.609 ms** on the same statistic, which is *worse than asserting that there is no pair at all*.

363. *(Stereo quality track.)* **The fit's objective is the gate's own exceedance, with a margin, a step floor, and the delay term in the same units — and every one of those four is there because the version without it failed a specific way.** The objective is `sum over bands of max(0, |err| / bar - 0.5)` on both surfaces plus the same shape on the delays. (a) **In bars, not in correlation.** The bars differ by more than an order of magnitude between bands — 0.009 in 63-125 Hz against 0.120 one octave up — because the recording repeats itself far more exactly down there, and a summed raw `|err|` therefore *sells the bass*: on this material a geometry that takes 63-125 Hz from +0.965 to +0.494 to buy 0.6 of correlation back across 125-500 Hz scores 1.30 where the bass-true one scores 2.21, and is **51 bars out** where the other is 1.3. Nothing in a bar is a function of the engine — `realism::tests::the_stereo_bar_is_built_out_of_the_reference_alone` pins that to twelve decimals — so minimising a distance in units of a threshold that cannot move is minimising the distance. (b) **The margin is 0.5 bars, not 1.0.** An objective that goes flat exactly at the gate's threshold parks a band *on* it: the first run walked 2-6 kHz to 0.072 against a bar of 0.072 and 6-12 kHz to 0.098 against 0.098, both passing by a thousandth. Half a bar is about one take-to-take disagreement of the recording with itself. (c) **The step floor is 0.02 bars.** Below that the search reads the difference between two renders of one instrument rather than anything about the recording, and a search with no such floor walks a flat direction to whichever bound it points at — which the first run did with `diffuse_coherence`, riding it from 5.0 to the ceiling for a total gain of 0.08 bars over twenty accepted steps. (d) **The delay term's floor is measured too**: the same delay taken on each key's *other velocity layer*, which is a second recording through microphones that cannot have moved between takes — **0.169 ms** over the thirty keys.

364. *(Stereo quality track.)* **The fit is made on both surfaces the stereo table is printed on, because they disagree and can disagree oppositely.** `renders/realism/REALISM.md`'s Columns S are six phrases — mixtures of keys, most of them transposed, three pedalled — and `tuner/tests/stereo.rs` is thirty keys struck alone. A geometry moves them differently: the notes-only fit took the **notes'** 63-125 Hz band from 0.037 to 0.009 while taking the **phrases'** from 0.149 to **0.254**, worse than the value it started from, because a note's coherence is two capsules' view of *one* source and a phrase's is their view of several at once, and steepening the pan-to-position map improves the first while spreading the second. That is also the clearest evidence for a narrow pair in the whole exercise: the recording does **not** decorrelate when many keys sound at once (+0.959 on the phrases against +0.953 on the notes), so whatever the real pair is, it sees the whole low end of the instrument as very nearly one source. Fitting both surfaces at once is what turns that into a number, and it pulls the spacing from 0.231 m to 0.126 m — onto the delay inversion's own answer. The cost of doing it is stated rather than hidden: on the notes alone the notes-only fit is better (19.8 bars against 23.6), and on everything together it is worse (107 against 103).

365. *(Stereo quality track.)* **What the fit bought, on four surfaces, three of which it was fitted on and two of which it was not.** Fit velocity 90; the held-out layers are velocity **68 and 116**, three bands away in the library's own layering, and they are genuinely different *recordings* rather than the same take scaled. Bars out, and reds, before → after: **recorded keys at v90** 24.40 → **19.77 → 23.56**, 3 red → 2 red — the 63-125 Hz band **passes for the first time** (|err| 0.037 → 0.008 against a bar of 0.009, engine +0.916 → +0.961 against the recording's +0.953); **the six phrases** 32.00 → **23.88**, 5 red → 4 red, with 63-125 Hz 0.149 → 0.082 and 6-12 kHz 0.127 → 0.012; **held out at v68** 24.88 → 26.72, 6 red → **4 red**; **held out at v116** 32.93 → **28.52**, 4 red → 4 red. The gate's verdict improves on every one of the four and the exceedance improves on three; v68 is the one that does not, and it is the top octave at a soft dynamic — thirteen readable keys, 6-12 kHz from 0.097 to 0.407 — which is where a note has least energy above 6 kHz and the layer has most of its own floor. `COMPASS.md`'s per-key line: keys on the opposite side of zero from the recording 29 → 32, and **median |Δ| 0.419 → 0.252**, which is the pair of numbers to read together — the sign flips are keys sitting near zero, and the image as a whole is 40 % closer. What is still red is what item 357 named and item 362 measured: 125-500 Hz, where the engine reads +0.89 and +0.71 against −0.115 and −0.226.

366. *(Stereo quality track.)* **Self-calibration: a spacing the engine was told to have, read back out of its own renders — and the constant that has to be in the middle, which is a fact about the mid/side construction rather than about the estimator.** `tuner/tests/mics.rs`. The engine's microphone stage is not a delay pair: it is mid plus side, and the mid is delay-free so that no mono board moves (item 352), so each channel is a **two-tap** filter of the source rather than a delayed copy of it. Past a width of about `2m/u_L` the far channel's second tap is the larger of its two, which makes that channel maximum-phase and adds a whole `δ` of group delay of its own on top of the geometry's — so the cross-spectrum's phase slope, which is all a phase-transform estimator measures, is twice the geometric one. Measured on the engine at three spacings an octave apart with the shipped width, the readout is **1.455, 1.760 and 1.530** times the geometry's saturated delay; `ENGINE_LAG_PER_ITD` is their geometric mean, **1.58** — short of the algebra's 2 because the board's diffuse field arrives with no delay at all and pulls every readout towards zero, and because about one key in four lands on the other lobe of its own correlation and reads the opposite sign. With it, `spacing = |median lag| · c / 1.58` recovers **0.12, 0.24 and 0.48 m to −8.6 %, +10.4 % and −4.1 %** through the whole instrument — soundboard, unisons, duplex, mechanism noise and limiter included. A **median and not the shaped inversion**, because on these keys the geometry has saturated and the curve has no shape left for a two-parameter fit to read; a plateau is recovered by its level. Two controls: doubling the spacing doubles the delay with no constant in the assertion at all, and an engine with `[voicing.mics]` **absent** — the pan-pot and the orthogonal board taps, no delay anywhere — gives the inversion nothing to find, its residual no better than the no-pair null. This is `estimate::directivity::DRIFT_PER_SPREAD_DB`'s pattern exactly: what a parameter does to the engine is measured on the engine, encoded as one constant, and re-checked by a test that fails when the constant stops being true.

367. *(Stereo quality track.)* **What did not move, and the gates.** The mono sum is untouched by construction (item 352) and stays so at the new geometry: `tuner/tests/stereo.rs`'s fold-down gate reads **under 0.01 dB in every band**, which is `f32` rounding on `((m+s)+(m-s))/2` and not a tolerance being spent. So the boards do not move: `REALISM.md` mel **5.03 / 5.24 / 4.09 / 5.03 / 6.36 / 4.83** and motion A1 1.36, A2 0.76, B1 1.49 dB, B2 1.070 — item 343's shipped column to the digit; `MELODY.md` still passes every column with only sign-of-zero differences; `COMPASS.md` still **12 flags over 11 keys**, the same eleven, with the seven mono metrics moving in their last printed digit at two keys (`match` 22.78 → 22.75 at A6, `jitter` 2.25 → 2.23 at C7). `presets/default.toml` has no `[voicing.mics]` and is untouched, renders and all. **Perf**: the 88-voice pedalled glissando on the measured preset reads **31.4 % of one core** against item 358's 31.38 % and a 50 % budget — the geometry changes no work per sample. `cargo test --release --workspace --no-fail-fast`: **687 passed, 2 failed**; this track's delta is **+9 green and no new red** — five unit tests in `estimate::mics` (the sign convention on a known delay, independent channels reading as no delay, the ITD's saturation at `d/c`, the inversion recovering an exact curve, and the inversion surviving a noisy one) and four in `tuner/tests/mics.rs`. The two reds are the ones already named: item 260's duplex baseline (which fails at HEAD too) and the stereo-image gate, still red in 125-500 Hz. `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets` is clean.


387. *(Integration track; reserved range 380-399.)* **The event's velocity is 16 bits, and the field has two lanes rather than one scale.** `SHIPPING.md` §4 asks for `Event` velocity `u8 -> u16` so the SL88 MK2's sixteen bits are not thrown away at the door. The obvious spelling — one scale, `vel * 512` everywhere — was measured against this repository and refused: **`Event::NoteOn { vel: 90 }` appears about two hundred and fifty times** in acceptance tests whose numbers were measured at exactly that velocity, in `tuner/src/tools/`, in `forensics/`, and in files another workstream was editing at the time; a `u16` field accepts an integer literal without complaint, so every one of those would have gone on compiling and started playing pianissimo. So the widening is **value-preserving**, and `types::velocity_from_midi(vel: u16) -> f32` reads the field as: `0..=255` is a MIDI 1.0 velocity, exactly the number the field has always held (0 is still the silent press), and `256..=65535` is a velocity in 1/512 of a MIDI step. **The lane boundary is 255 and not 127 because that is the boundary that makes the widening provable**: a velocity over 127 is not legal MIDI, but a `u8` could hold one and this repository held one — `tuner/tests/calibration.rs`'s strike-direction sweep walks `layer * 8` up to **128**, which the engine used to clamp downstream. With the boundary at 127 that 128 fell into the fine lane, became velocity 0.25, and turned a −9 dB swing into a −0.68 dB slope: the suite caught it, and the fix is the lane, not the test. Every value a `u8` could hold now means what it meant, clamped where it was clamped (`ump::tests::every_value_the_old_u8_field_could_hold_still_means_what_it_meant`). Both lanes are exact, continuous and strictly increasing *within* the lane, and they agree at every old point: `velocity_from_midi(v) == v` and `velocity_from_midi(v * 512) == v` for all `v` in 1..=127, pinned by `midi::ump::tests::the_velocity_map_reproduces_every_old_point`. **There is no single monotone map that could have done both.** If a continuous map on `0..=65535` is the identity on `1..=127` then its value at 127 is full scale, so everything above 127 either exceeds full scale or saturates — the two lanes are not a shortcut, they are the only shape that preserves the literals. The price is stated where it is paid: a fine-lane velocity below 256/512, half of the softest playable blow, cannot be expressed and is clamped up to it by `hires_velocity`. **The consumers are one substitution each** — `vel as f32` became `velocity_from_midi(vel)` in `hammer::hammer_velocity`, `string::strike_position_in_velocity`, `voice::damper_step` and the three `vel as f32 / 127.0` noise levels — because the engine was already continuous in velocity (`SHIPPING.md`: "f32 hammer velocity, 24-bit mantissa against 16-bit velocity"), so nothing downstream needed a new idea. Proof that it changed no audio: the C harness of item 384 still renders md5 `f0fcb07999c00ca60110cd537de8f09e` against `cargo run -- render`, sample for sample.
