388. *(Integration track; reserved range 380-399.)* **Cross-partition edits the widening forced, listed for review.** Changing a type in `types.rs` reaches every construction site in the workspace, and this milestone ran beside another one with a strict file split, so every touch outside the integration track's own files is named here rather than left to a diff. **In files the split reserved to the other track**, all of them type-only: `engine/src/voice.rs` — `note_on`, `key_down`, `note_off` and `damper_step` take `u16`, three `vel as f32 / 127.0` became `velocity_from_midi(vel)`, one test helper's parameter; `engine/src/string.rs` — `set_strike`, `strike_velocity`, the cached `velocity` field, `StrikeMix::at` and `strike_position_in_velocity` take `u16` (and `NOMINAL_STRIKE_VELOCITY` is widened at its single use with `u16::from`, so `preset.rs` and `noise.rs` are untouched), plus two test literals `1u8 -> 1u16`; `engine/src/calibrate.rs` — one `const REFERENCE_VELOCITY: u8 -> u16`; `tuner/src/tools/` — **twenty-six `u16::from(...)` wraps across twelve files** (`ab.rs`, `bench.rs`, `brilliance.rs`, `chain.rs`, `compass.rs`, `fit/motion.rs`, `fit/partials.rs`, `noise.rs`, `residuals.rs`, `score.rs`, `sympathetic.rs`, `tail.rs`), each at an `Event::NoteOn`/`NoteOff` whose velocity is a `u8` constant that also indexes a 7-bit sample layer and therefore must stay `u8`. **In files the split reserved to nobody**: `engine/src/{engine,render,hammer,lib}.rs`, `engine/tests/*`, `forensics/src/bin/*` (twenty-eight wraps across seventeen files), `tuner/src/sampler.rs` (the bridge to the sampler's own event type gains `to_layer_velocity`, which rounds to 7 bits because a sample library's `lovel..=hivel` is 7-bit in every format there is), `tuner/tests/{calibration,limiter,stereo}.rs`. **One accident to declare:** a `cargo fmt --all` was run on a tree that had never been rustfmt-clean, and it reformatted files belonging to the other track. Undoing it was done by proof rather than by eye, in two passes: thirty-one files whose whole change was formatting were restored to `HEAD` (rustfmt of the `HEAD` text equals the working text, so nothing else was in them), and twenty-four more — `voice.rs`, `string.rs`, `hammer.rs`, `calibrate.rs` and twenty of the `tuner/src/tools/` and `forensics/` files — were **rebuilt from `HEAD` plus this milestone's own edit**, keeping the rebuilt text only when rustfmt of it reproduced the working file exactly. `engine/src/voice.rs` went from 88 changed lines to 22 that way, `engine/src/string.rs` from 151 to 24. What is left carries formatting that is not this milestone's work, in the files where the other track had uncommitted changes of its own and the two could not be separated — `engine/src/{engine,preset,soundboard}.rs`, `tuner/src/{preset,realism,sampler}.rs`, `tuner/src/estimate/chain.rs`, `tuner/src/tools/{bench,chain,compass}.rs` and five under `forensics/src/bin/`. None of it changes what any of them does. The lesson is the obvious one: in a shared tree, format the files you edited, never `--all`.

389. *(Integration track; reserved range 380-399.)* **Live MIDI is one input port asking for MIDI 2.0, and the OS does the rest.** `piano-emulator --midi-in [name]` opens `MIDIInputPortCreateWithProtocol(kMIDIProtocol_2_0)` — `coremidi` 0.9.2's `Client::input_port_with_protocol`, which is real and complete in that crate, along with `virtual_destination_with_protocol` and the `EventList`/`EventPacket` UMP types — connects to every source or to the ones a case-insensitive name matches, and publishes a virtual destination named "Piano Emulator" so other applications can play it. **`DISTRIBUTION.md`'s "we do not implement MIDI-CI" turned out to be stronger than it reads: we do not implement a MIDI 1.0 path either.** Measured on a Core MIDI loopback in this repository (`engine/tests/live_midi.rs`), a MIDI 1.0 source's `90 3C 5A` arrives at a 2.0 port as `40903C00 B4D30000` — message type 4, velocity 46 291 — and 46 291 is exactly the specification's own min-center-max up-scaling of 90. So the parser is UMP-first: `midi::ump::parse_ump` reads message types 2 and 4 and skips everything else *by length*, and `midi::ump::midi1_velocity_of` is the piecewise-linear inverse of that up-scaling, exact at all 128 points. That exactness is the whole design: a nearby scale such as `v16 * 127 / 65535` turns velocity 90 into 89.7 and makes a keyboard play a different note from the same note in a `.mid` file. The MIDI 1.0 byte parser (`ump::Midi1Stream`, running status, real-time bytes interleaved, sysex cancelling status) is kept as the fallback for a Core MIDI that will not give us a protocol port, and as the parser a non-Apple backend would need. Two semantic differences between the protocols are written into the code because the specification writes them there: a MIDI 1.0 note on at velocity 0 is a **note off** (running status), and a MIDI 2.0 note on at velocity 0 is a **silent press** — `PHYSICS.md` §6's damper lift, which no MIDI 1.0 controller can express at all.

390. *(Integration track; reserved range 380-399.)* **CC 64 is slewed over 15 ms in the adapter, and the REPL and the keyboard share the queue behind a mutex on the producer end.** The slew is `DISTRIBUTION.md`'s own mitigation for 7-bit half-pedalling, and it is a *rate* limiter rather than a smoother: full travel takes `Slew::TRAVEL_S` = 15 ms, a tenth of the travel takes a tenth of that, and the pedal lands exactly on its target rather than approaching it — the end points are physical, and an exponential smoother never quite reaches "dampers on strings". It is stepped at one engine block (2.67 ms), because item 55 applies an event at the start of the next block and two positions inside one block are one position with the first thrown away. **The queue's contract is unchanged and that is what needed care**: `rtrb`'s producer is single-threaded, and live play has three producers — the terminal, Core MIDI's receive thread, and the slew's ticker. `midi::EventInput` is an `Arc<Mutex<EventSender>>`; it serialises those three and nothing else. The audio thread holds the *consumer*, never locks, and cannot be starved by a lock it does not take, so `DECISIONS.md` 55's contract and the allocation-free render path are exactly as they were. A full queue drops rather than blocks: the ring holds far more events than a keyboard can produce between two audio callbacks, so a full one means the audio thread has stopped.

391. *(Integration track; reserved range 380-399.)* **The loopback test is real, and what it cannot reach is named.** A Core MIDI **virtual source created inside the test process** can be connected to the real input port and played, in an ordinary `cargo test`, with no hardware and no entitlement — this was uncertain when the milestone started and it is now six passing tests in `engine/tests/live_midi.rs`. They pin the OS's own behaviour from the outside: the up-scaling table for eight velocities, that a MIDI 2.0 velocity between two 7-bit points arrives between them and stays there, that both protocols spell the same note the same way, that CC 64 arrives as a monotone ramp ending exactly at 1.0, and — through `Engine::process`, the call the audio callback makes — that a note played on a virtual keyboard makes the engine sound. The file skips *loudly*, printing why, if Core MIDI will not give the process a client. **What no test here can reach**, and what `README.md`'s live-input section carries as a manual step: that an SL88 MK2 negotiates MIDI 2.0 with macOS at all, and that its sixteen-bit velocities are as fine as its data sheet says — `DISTRIBUTION.md`'s verdict already notes that Yamaha, the most candid vendor, senses 10 bits of the 16. The ABI moved with the engine: `PE_MAX_VELOCITY` is 65 535 with the same two lanes, and `PE_ABI_VERSION` is **2**, because the meaning of values above 255 changed even though every value at or below it plays exactly what it played under version 1. **The gates.** `cargo test --release --workspace --no-fail-fast`: **676 passed, 2 failed**, and the delta this milestone owns is **+22 green and no new red** — eleven unit tests in `midi::ump`, four on the pedal slew, one on the two-producer merge, and the six Core MIDI loopback tests. The two reds are the ones that were already named: item 260's duplex baseline, and the stereo-image gate the quality track landed deliberately red. `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets` is silent. The proof that none of it changed the instrument is item 384's C harness, which still renders md5 `f0fcb07999c00ca60110cd537de8f09e` against `cargo run -- render`, sample for sample.

368. *(Stereo quality track; reserved range 346-379.)* **The stereo gate is green, and what closed it is not a microphone.** Item 346's gate went red in all six bands, item 358 took it to two red and item 365 to two red with the bass passing for the first time; what stayed red was **125-500 Hz**, and item 357 argued from the model that nothing in `PHYSICS.md` §8 could ever close it — the recording reads **+0.953 below 125 Hz and −0.115 one octave above**, and `sin(kd)/kd`, a pure interchannel delay, or any mixture of the two cannot fall from +0.95 through zero across a single octave. That argument was right and this milestone does not contradict it. What it does is take item 357's own second candidate — "the board's mode-controlled nodal lines below 500 Hz putting a mode anti-phase across the capsules", which that item declined to build because "without measured directivity [it] would be exactly the invention §8 warns against" — and **measure the directivity first**. `piano-tuner mics --stage profile` and `realism::stereo_profile` are that measurement; items 369-370 are what it says and what was built from it; 371-373 are the fit, its objective and the four surfaces; 374-375 are neutrality and the new board; 376-377 are the gates and what is still not right. `presets/salamander-c5.toml` gains three numbers, `presets/default.toml` gains nothing and renders bit for bit what it did, and **no mono board moves at all** — the section is still mid plus side and still replaces only the side.

369. *(Stereo quality track.)* **Six bands are enough to score a coherence and not enough to see one: the recording's interchannel image, at sixth-octave resolution, is a three-regime plate.** `realism::stereo_profile` is `stereo_image`'s arithmetic with the band list generated instead of tabulated — 40 Hz to 16 kHz, six bands to the octave, one FFT pair, and no lag search (in a sixth-octave band the zero-lag value is `M − S` in closed form and every other lag is the signal's own periodicity). Run over the same thirty recorded keys at v90 the gate uses, the recording's **median `r0`** reads: `+0.97` at 80 Hz, `+0.98` at 90, `+0.95` at 101 and 113, **`+0.94` at 127** — and then **`+0.30` at 143, `+0.07` at 160, `−0.53` at 180, `−0.49` at 202, `−0.36` at 226, `−0.47` at 254** — and then back through zero: `+0.45` at 285, and inside **±0.25 of zero at every one of the eighteen points from 320 Hz to 8 kHz but one** — 508 Hz reads −0.34, a second and much shallower dip that is measured, stated and not modelled. The mid-over-side ratio says the same thing on an independent statistic: `+12 dB` under 130 Hz, **`−2.4` to `−4.0` dB over 180-254**, and inside ±1.7 dB above 300. **It is a measurement and not a curiosity of one take**: the same thirty keys' *other velocity layer* — a second recording through microphones that cannot have moved — reproduces the whole curve, `+0.945` at 127, `+0.21` at 143, `−0.06` at 160, `−0.42` at 180, `−0.44` at 254, `+0.45` at 285. Read as three regimes of a radiating plate this is textbook and the frequencies are a grand's: below its first modes the board and its air load are **one radiator** and two capsules 12 cm apart see one pressure; from about 140 Hz the board is **mode-controlled**, individual modes with a small number of nodal lines cross the string band, the pair straddles one, and the two capsules see the same mode *in opposition*; above about 300-500 Hz **modal overlap** sets in, many modes sum with unrelated phases, and there is no organised sign left to see. What no two-point geometry has is the middle one, and that is exactly the band item 357 could not close.

370. *(Stereo quality track.)* **`[voicing.mics.modal]`: the mode-controlled band, as two edges and a lift, and why each of the three is the shape it is.** `soundboard::ModalLobe`. The FDN's two orthogonal output taps are already the third regime exactly — their *difference* is a signal uncorrelated with their sum, which is `r0 = 0` — and [`Mics`] already highpasses that difference for the diffuse field's coherence. This adds the second regime on top of the same signal: `side = highpass(s) + lift · butterworth_band(s)`. **(a) The lift is above one and has to be.** When mid and side are uncorrelated — which is what an orthogonal tap pair makes them — `r0 = (M − S)/(M + S)` identically, so a mid/side ratio *is* a coherence and a filter that sets the ratio sets the coherence. A negative `r0` therefore means **more difference than sum**, which is what the recording has (−3.5 dB of mid over side at 200 Hz, on both takes) and what a nodal line between two capsules produces. It costs the mono fold-down nothing at any value, because `(L+R)/2` is the mid and the mid is never touched — and that is also why the recording's own mono sum is missing that energy, and why every mono board in this repository is unmoved by the whole section. **(b) The lower edge is eighth-order, and the order is a measurement.** Item 369's curve falls from `r0 = +0.940` at 127 Hz to `−0.529` at 180. Read as a side-over-mid *amplitude* that is 0.176 to 1.80 over half an octave: **40 dB per octave**. Nothing shallower reaches it, and the shape has to be maximally flat above the corner as well as steep below it — four **identical** Butterworth sections are also 48 dB/octave and were built first, and they fail, because an identical cascade is still 0.46 of its passband gain a fifth above its corner where a true Butterworth is at 0.97. Measured through the instrument, the identical cascade could not take the 125-250 Hz band below **+0.39** at any edge that left 63-125 Hz intact; the staggered-`Q` eighth-order takes it to **−0.18**. The upper edge is fourth-order, which is all the measured return to coherence asks for — from 320 Hz to 8 kHz the profile has no second edge in it to resolve, only eighteen points inside ±0.25 of zero and the one −0.34 at 508. **(c) The `Q`s are Butterworth and are not free**: `1/(2 cos((2k+1)π/16))` and `1/(2 cos((2k+1)π/8))`, maximally flat, no ripple. The lift is the gain of a *band*, not of a resonance — a resonance would put the whole effect at one frequency, and what the measurement shows is a region.

371. *(Stereo quality track.)* **The three numbers are fitted, on the same objective and the same two surfaces as item 363's, and the fit had to be taught to finish.** `piano-tuner mics --stage modal`, `MODAL_KNOBS`. A separate stage from `image` rather than three more axes on it, for item 362's reason: the geometry is pinned by a measurement the engine is not in — the recording's own interchannel delays — and the band is not, so letting the search trade one against the other would spend the pinned half to buy the free one, which is precisely what the free four-parameter coherence fit did when it walked the spacing to 0.66 m. **Two things had to change about the search itself, and both were found by a better point being visibly nearby.** (a) **A coarse grid first.** Started at item 369's own reading of the profile — 165 Hz where the curve crosses zero going down, 270 where it comes back, and a lift of 1.5 from the depth in between — the compass walks the band *wider and weaker*, to 208-875 Hz at a lift of 1.67 and **29.66 bars out**, and stops; a narrower, stronger band scores **24.18** on the same objective. The two are not on one slope, because the lower edge and the lift trade almost exactly against each other, and a search that moves one axis at a time reads the ridge between the basins as a wall. Sixty-four log-spaced cells cost a tenth of the compass they precede and remove the question. (b) **Compass, simplex, compass again, until neither moves it.** One round stopped at 202-252 Hz ×3.48 and 20.47 bars, and a point a third of a semitone away — 205-245 Hz ×3.80 — scores **20.37** and takes the gate from one red to none. The surface has small steps in it, because a band enters or leaves the readable set as the engine's own levels move, and the two searches fail on steps in opposite ways: the compass walks past a diagonal valley, the simplex reads a step as curvature and contracts onto it. A fit that leaves a better point that close to the one it reports has not finished, and `SEARCH_ROUNDS` and `modal_refine` — a *diagonal* pattern search, all twenty-six neighbours at four step sizes, a maximal positive basis in the sense of Torczon 1997 — are the fix. (c) **Relaxed first, then constrained** (item 372): the ten-bar red-band penalty is larger than the whole continuous variation of the objective, so under it the surface is dominated by an integer count of reds and the coarse grid can no longer tell one basin from another — run that way round from the start, the grid picks 281-570 Hz ×1.7 and the compass sits down at **40.97 bars**. Item 363's own objective finds the basin; the constraint then decides where in it to stop.

372. *(Stereo quality track.)* **The two surfaces disagree by more than either's own floor, and the fit now says which one is authoritative.** The recording's 250-500 Hz band reads **−0.226** on thirty solo recorded keys and **+0.348** on six phrases, against take-to-take floors of 0.039 and 0.018. One coherence curve cannot produce both aggregates unless the engine's *within-band* energy distribution matches the recording's, which it does not exactly, so a summed objective splits the difference — and measured, it splits it almost evenly: at its own optimum (a band of 212-225 Hz at a lift of 4.81, 19.99 bars out) the notes are **1.13** bars out in that band and the phrases **0.91**, which is a good balance and still a **red gate**, because the two bars differ — 0.059 against 0.089 — and one threshold falls on each side of an equal split. `RED_BAND_PENALTY` charges ten bars for every red band of the recorded-keys surface, and the three reasons that surface is the authority are not "because it is the gate": it is the only one made of keys the library **recorded**, with no resampling anywhere in it (item 328's rule for every other fitted quantity here); it is **thirty** items against six and spans the whole compass; and it is **one source at a time**, which is what a per-source geometry is about — item 364's own sentence. Item 364's objection to notes-only fitting was that it made the phrases *worse than they started*, and that objection does not apply: the phrase surface improves from 23.88 to 18.37 under the constraint, against 18.04 without it. **The price is 0.33 bars on the phrase board, stated rather than hidden**, and what it buys is that **both** surfaces' 250-500 Hz columns pass at once, which neither the unconstrained optimum nor the capsule pair alone manages. Two structural things the penalty needed, both found by watching it fail: the **grid keeps item 363's objective**, because ten bars per red turns the surface into an integer count that no coarse grid can read (run the other way round, the grid picks 281-570 Hz ×1.7 and the compass sits down at 40.97 bars); and the constrained pass **starts at the best point the relaxed pass saw pass the gate**, not at the relaxed optimum, because the relaxed optimum is *infeasible* and every point within ten per cent of it on all three axes is red — the ordinary failure of a penalty method started outside the feasible set, and the ordinary fix. Noticing which candidates were feasible costs nothing: the relaxed objective has already computed the gate's columns for every one of them.

373. *(Stereo quality track.)* **What it bought, on four surfaces, two of which the fit never saw.** Fit velocity 90; the held-out layers are **68 and 116**, three bands away in the library's own layering and genuinely different recordings rather than the same take scaled. Bars out and reds, item 367's shipped instrument → this one:

| surface | reds before | reds after | bars out before | bars out after |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| **the gate** — 30 recorded keys, v90 | 2 | **0** | 23.56 | **0.85** |
| the six phrases (`REALISM.md` Columns S) | 4 | 3 | 23.88 | 18.37 |
| held out, v68 | 4 | 3 | 26.75 | **6.01** |
| held out, v116 | 4 | 4 | 28.51 | **5.26** |

Band by band on the gate's own material, engine `r@0` before → after against the recording's (bar in brackets): `63-125` +0.961 → **+0.953** against +0.953 (0.009) · `125-250` +0.893 → **−0.221** against −0.115 (0.120) · `250-500` +0.710 → **−0.169** against −0.226 (0.059) · `500-2k` +0.053 → +0.042 against −0.002 (0.102) · `2k-6k` −0.024 → −0.024 against −0.012 (0.072) · `6k-12k` +0.089 → +0.055 against +0.041 (0.096). Summed |err| **2.058 → 0.232**. `COMPASS.md`'s per-key line: keys on the opposite side of zero from the recording 32 → **31** (46 when the gate was written), median |Δ| 0.252 → **0.248** (0.784 when the gate was written) — the pair of numbers item 365 says to read together.

374. *(Stereo quality track.)* **The mono sum, the neutrality contract, the level and the cost — all four re-measured rather than inherited.** (a) **The mono sum is untouched by construction and stays so at a lift of nearly five.** `soundboard::ModalLobe` adds to the *side* and only to the side, so `(L+R)/2` is the mid and the mid is not a function of any of the three numbers. Asserted three ways: `soundboard::tests::the_lift_moves_the_board_field_and_nothing_else` renders the board at lifts 0.0/1.0/2.4/4.0 and the four mono sums differ by −118.7, −120.5 and −120.2 dB peak, which is `f32` rounding in the master chain's own recursions; `the_microphone_pair_leaves_the_mono_sum_exactly_where_the_pan_pot_put_it` now carries a fourth geometry row with the band at **both** rails of its schema (40 Hz to 2 kHz at a lift of 6) and still reads under −100 dB peak; and end to end on the shipped demo, `tuner/tests/stereo.rs`'s fold-down gate reads **0.0000 dB** band-wise against a stated discipline of 0.5 dB. (b) **`presets/default.toml` is bit-identical**, and so is the measured preset with the section deleted — md5 of the demo render `8f98c9cf1f93b98f0e2bdaed7e07a324` for the default, `5bdb9bd2befb9eaa1370be1ac569ed05` for its compass sweep, and `92b44aeecb549381827ec6ae1138dbbb` for `salamander-c5.toml` with the whole `[voicing.mics]` section removed: item 352's own three numbers, unmoved. With only `[voicing.mics.modal]` removed it renders `bddf1973a977e39db6623f2d24478416`, which is the shipped preset of item 367 to the byte. (c) **Level.** An anti-phase band is energy the mono sum loses, so the per-channel level rises where the band is: on the shipped demo the per-channel RMS goes **−32.414 → −32.033 dBFS**, +0.38 dB, and the peak **0.7088 → 0.7096** against a limiter threshold of 0.891. The loudest single strike of the measured preset is key 89 at velocity 127, **5.90 dB under the threshold**, exactly where `tuner/tests/limiter.rs` read it before. (d) **Perf**: the 88-voice pedalled glissando on the measured preset reads **31.4 % of one core** against item 367's 31.4 % and a 50 % budget — six biquads on one mono signal per sample is nothing next to 88 voices.

375. *(Stereo quality track.)* **`renders/stereo/STEREO.md`: the one board in this repository that is not a mono sum, and the first one that can be *listened* to for the thing it scores.** `piano-tuner stereo`. Two pieces of music — the Ode melody line alone and unpedalled, and the pedalled chord phrase where the board rings longest — through four things: the pan-pot the engine had before item 351, the capsule pair alone (`[voicing.mics.modal]` deleted), the shipped preset, and the Salamander recording of the same music. **The three engine takes are matched to one gain and not to three**, because they share a mono sum sample for sample: everything audible between them is side energy, which is the subject, and a per-take normalisation would remove the finding. The recording is matched separately, for `tools::ab`'s reason — somebody else's microphones twelve centimetres above somebody else's strings are not comparable to a rendered level. Beside the audio the document prints, per take, the interchannel correlation in the six scoreboard bands, the **mid-over-side ratio** (the statistic a fold-down loses, and the one that reads negative where the recording's difference beats its sum), the peak |r| and the lag it sits at, and the **sixth-octave curve** those six numbers summarise — which is where the reader can check the engine's realised shape against the recording's rather than trust the label on the section. What the two pieces read at lag zero, per take, in the band the finding is about (`125-250` / `250-500`): the **pan-pot** `+0.589 / +0.794` on the melody line and `+0.732 / +0.709` on the chords; the **pair alone** `+0.802 / +0.656` and `+0.837 / +0.649`; the **shipped preset** `+0.106 / +0.395` and `+0.470 / +0.267`; the **recording** `−0.272 / +0.551` and `−0.004 / +0.487`. Two pieces of music are two items and not a bar — `tuner/tests/stereo.rs` is where the thirty-key bar is — but the ordering is the milestone in one line: the pan-pot is the furthest from the recording on both, the pair alone is next, and the shipped preset is the only one on the recording's side of anything.

376. *(Stereo quality track.)* **The gates, and the boards that did not move.** `cargo test --release --workspace --no-fail-fast`: **694 passed, 1 failed, 1 ignored**, against item 367's 687/2/1. **This track's delta is +6 green and the documented stereo red turning green** — three unit tests in `soundboard` (the band is anti-phase and only there; the lower edge is as steep as the recording's own and maximally flat above its corner; the lift moves the board field and nothing else), two in `realism` (the profile and the six bands are one measurement at two resolutions; a side larger than its mid reads as a negative lobe), and one control in `tuner/tests/stereo.rs` (`the_capsule_pair_without_the_mode_controlled_band_fails_in_the_middle`, which asserts that the two bands the new section carries are red without it, red on the *positive* side, and that the four it does not carry pass without it). Fifteen new rejection cases across the two crates' preset schemas — both edges at both rails, a lift over its ceiling, a negative lift, three NaNs, and the two that matter: a **crossed** band and one whose edges coincide, either of which would realise a notch where the section names a lobe. The one red is the one already named: item 260's duplex baseline. `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets` is clean.

**The boards did not move, because the mono sum did not.** `REALISM.md` mel **5.03 / 5.24 / 4.09 / 5.03 / 6.36 / 4.83** and motion **A1 1.36, A2 0.76, B1 1.49 dB, B2 1.070** — item 343's shipped column to the digit, three milestones running. `MELODY.md` passes every column, `head` 0.96 / 0.35 / 3.04 against bars of 1.49 / 1.10 / 5.93 and `tail` 0.83 / 1.11 / 3.76 against 3.15 / 1.38 / 5.32. `COMPASS.md` is still **12 flags over 11 keys**, the same eleven and the same twelve metrics, with one mono number moving in its last printed digit (A6 `match` 22.75 → 22.76). What *did* move is the two stereo columns those documents carry, which is the point: `REALISM.md`'s Columns S go 4 red → **3**, with 250-500 Hz passing for the first time (0.206 → **0.069** against a bar of 0.089), and `COMPASS.md`'s per-key line goes from 32 keys on the wrong side of zero and a median |Δ| of 0.252 to **31 and 0.248** — and from item 346's own baseline, 46 keys and 0.784. `renders/chain/CHAIN.md`, the record of the experiment that started all of this, now reads its bare-engine interchannel mismatch at **0.169 against the 0.233 item 323 published**, on material none of this was fitted to.

377. *(Stereo quality track.)* **What is still not right, named rather than left for the next reader to find.** (a) **The fitted band is narrower than the regime it is named after.** A grand's mode-controlled region runs from its first nodal-line mode to modal overlap and that is at least an octave; the shipped band is **214.5 to 227.4 Hz**, a twelfth of an octave. The reason is not that the board's is: it is that the lobe supplies only what the *rest of the engine* does not already — the pair's geometric difference and the diffuse highpass carry part of the decorrelation over the same frequencies — so the fitted edges are the edges of the **missing** term and not of the regime. That is honest but it is also the thing to watch: a term whose edges are a residual can absorb an error that belongs somewhere else, and the check that it has not is `renders/stereo/STEREO.md`'s sixth-octave column, where the engine's realised curve is printed against the recording's rather than against a label — **and on the melody line it does not line up**: the recording's dip there sits at 143-202 Hz (`−0.45`, `−0.43`, `−0.45`, `−0.66`) and the engine's at 226-285 (`−0.48`, `−0.33`), about half an octave high, with the engine still at `+0.72` and `+0.82` where the recording has already turned over. The band **aggregates** are inside the recording's own take-to-take disagreement on all six bands of thirty recorded keys, which is what the gate asserts and what this milestone claims; the *curve* is right in shape and about half an octave off in place on this material, and that is the next thing to measure rather than something this item is entitled to round off. (b) **The recording has a second, shallower dip at 508 Hz (`r0 = −0.34`, and −0.28 on the other take) and the engine has no term for it.** One lobe was fitted because one is what a three-regime plate has; a second would be a second parameter set fitted to a single point of the curve, which is the invention item 357 warns against, and it is left unmodelled and written down here instead. (c) **The two surfaces still disagree at 125-250 Hz**: the phrase board's column is +0.486 against the recording's +0.078, |err| 0.408 against a bar of 0.066 and it is the largest thing left on either surface that this section could in principle move. Item 372 explains why it is not moved further — the same coherence curve is being asked for two different band aggregates — and the honest next step is not a wider lobe but a **level-matched** comparison inside each band, so that the two surfaces stop measuring the engine's own spectral balance as though it were its image. (d) The phrase board's 63-125 Hz and 2-6 kHz columns are red and neither is this section's: they predate it, they are unmoved by it to the digit, and the first of them is the one place the *capsule pair* still misses on phrases.

378. *(Stereo quality track.)* **The stereo gate was reading every note from two milliseconds in, and both halves of that were wrong.** `tuner/tests/stereo.rs` asked for `PREROLL_S = 0.05`, which is 2400 samples; the engine's block is 128 and an event takes effect at the head of the block that contains it, so the strike landed at sample **2304** and the window the image was read over began at **2400**. Ninety-six samples of every note — the first two milliseconds — were outside the window, and the window opened in the middle of a signal. Nothing said so, because the same constant was in `tools::mics` and the fit and the gate were therefore wrong together, which is the failure mode a shared constant is supposed to prevent and here caused. **The verdict depended on it.** At the gate's own preroll the six bands read `+0.953 / −0.221 / −0.169 / +0.042 / −0.024 / +0.055` and none were red. Struck at the head of a block — 0.08 s, 3840 samples, the window opening in silence at the strike — the same instrument reads `+0.936 / +0.204 / +0.218 / −0.073 / −0.039 / −0.078` and **three are red**: 63-125 Hz at 1.84 bars, 125-250 at 2.65 and 250-500 at 7.49. Prerolls of 0.03 s and 0.11 s are both 32 samples past a block boundary and give the same third answer, which is what says the variable is the alignment and not noise. **It is two independent errors.** (a) *A window that opens inside a note opens with a step, and a step is broadband.* The engine's 6-12 kHz band went from readable on 15 of the 30 recorded keys to readable on 29 and its median `r0` from `−0.078` to `+0.296` between the aligned window and the misaligned one — an entire column made of the window's own edge. Fading that edge over the same 96 samples puts it back: A0's band reads `−72.1 dB` aligned, `−56.3` with the step and `−66.9` with the step faded. (b) *A window that opens after the strike is missing the strike*, and fading does **not** undo that — F#4's 125-250 Hz reads `+0.204` aligned, `−0.222` two milliseconds in and `−0.273` two milliseconds in with the edge faded. That half is content, and it is item 379's subject. **The fix is that there is only one legal window.** The preroll is now `realism::STEREO_PREROLL_SAMPLES`, 3840 = 30 × 128, shared by the gate and the fit, with `const _: () = assert!(PREROLL % BLOCK == 0)` in both places and a debug assertion that the samples before the window are silent — the window begins at the strike, in silence, by construction rather than by choice. **And the choice is licensed by a control.** The engine's window starts at a sample the renderer picked; the recording's starts where `detect_onset` says, which is an estimate. `the_recordings_image_does_not_move_when_the_window_does` asserts the estimate cannot matter: over the same three placements the recording's six bands move by `0.000 / 0.003 / 0.002 / 0.012 / 0.000 / 0.013`, against bars of `0.009 / 0.120 / 0.059 / 0.102 / 0.072 / 0.122`. Averaging the gate over several placements — the other way to answer this — was refused: two of the three placements are measurements of a window edge, and an average that includes them is an average of one right answer and two wrong ones.

379. *(Stereo quality track.)* **The mode-controlled band was built out of the wrong signal, and once the window was honest it could not hide it.** With `DECISIONS.md` 378's alignment fixed, the shipped instrument was three bands red, and the band item 377 (a) had already flagged as suspicious — **214.5 to 227.4 Hz, a twelfth of an octave, at a lift of 4.83** — was the reason. **The mechanism was wrong twice.** `soundboard::ModalLobe` band-limited the FDN's *difference* and scaled it up. But (a) a nodal line is not an incoherent field: two capsules straddling one hear the same signal with opposite signs, which is a deterministic anti-phase copy of the sound and not a louder helping of what the two channels already disagreed about; and (b) a filter on the diffuse difference **cannot act during the strike** — the FDN's shortest line is 149 samples, so its difference is exactly zero for the first 3.1 ms of every note, and a 13 Hz-wide band takes 77 ms to build up on top of that. Measured per 10 ms frame in 125-250 Hz, C5's **first frame read `+9.9 dB` mid over side** — the band is *mono* through the strike — where the recording's own first frame reads `−1.6 dB` and its second `−11.6`. That is what made the gate's two mid bands a high-variance statistic and what flipped their sign when the window moved two milliseconds: they were an average of a mono click and an over-decorrelated ring, in a band where a treble key holds −15 to −30 dB of its energy. **The fix is that the lobe runs on the sum, on both paths.** `direct: side += lift · butterworth_band(mid)` and `board: side += lift · butterworth_band(l + r)`, so the term exists from the first sample of the note and is an anti-phase copy rather than an amplified disagreement. It costs the mono fold-down nothing, because it is added to the side and the side cancels — `the_mono_fold_down_and_the_preset_without_the_section_are_both_unmoved` still reads 0.0000 dB and the absent-section render is still bit-identical. `lift` stops needing a model to interpret: it *is* the side-over-mid amplitude the band carries, so the recording's measured `−3.5 dB` mid/side ratio is `10^(3.5/20) = 1.5` read straight off, which is what `tools::mics`'s `PROFILE_LOBE_LIFT` had been asserting all along without the mechanism to make it true. `soundboard::tests::the_mode_controlled_band_reaches_the_direct_path` is the regression: with the board muted entirely the pair must still oppose inside the band, which the old form could not do at any setting because it had no term on that path. **And the fit had to be restructured to match**, because the change makes two of its stages inseparable: inside the band the side is now `width/2 · geometry + lift · sum`, one signal built out of two knobs, so `piano-tuner mics --stage band` moves `width`, `diffuse_coherence` and the band's three numbers together where `coherence` then `modal` moved them one after the other — and the old order fitted the trims with **no band present at all**, which is a fit to an instrument that was not going to ship (it sat down at 46.65 bars where the joint stage's own starting point is 27.24). **What it bought**, at the aligned window, on the thirty recorded keys: the mode-controlled band moved from **214.5-227.4 Hz at a lift of 4.83** — a twelfth of an octave, which item 377 (a) had already named as the thing to watch — to **229.4-307.4 Hz at 2.12**, four times as wide and at a lift that is now a plain side-over-mid amplitude, with `width` 1.703 → 1.626 and `diffuse_coherence` 7.860 → 3.474 (off its rail, which item 363's own `SEARCH_EPSILON` note says a fitted preset should not carry). The thirty recorded keys go from **3 red, 23.36 bars out, summed |err| 2.043** on the capsule pair alone to **0 red, 0.37 bars out, summed |err| 0.203**, and no band is parked on its threshold: the six margins are 0.89, 0.49, 0.51, 0.29, 0.50 and 0.34 of a bar. The two **held-out velocity layers**, never fitted, go from 3 red and 21.50 bars to 1 red and 2.15 at v68, and from 4 red and 30.15 to 2 red and 2.80 at v116; both remaining reds are 6-12 kHz on ten and twenty-one readable keys. The **phrase** surface goes from 4 red and 23.88 bars to 3 red and 17.25, and the trade inside that is stated rather than buried: 125-250 Hz — item 377 (c)'s "largest thing left on either surface" — closes from |err| 0.737 against a bar of 0.066 to **0.020**, and 250-500 Hz goes the other way, from 0.206 to 0.650, because the two surfaces disagree about that band by more than either's own floor and item 372's argument about which one is authoritative has not changed. The geometry is untouched by all of this and so is its residual against the recording's own delays, 0.303 ms. **What it did not buy.** The engine's own image still moves with the window where the recording's does not — the recording's six bands move by `0.000 / 0.003 / 0.002 / 0.012 / 0.000 / 0.013` over the same three placements and the engine's mid bands by 0.377 and 0.336 — and the mechanism above is not what is left. What is left is the *envelope*: in 125-250 Hz a treble key's engine energy falls 10 dB between the first 10 ms frame and the second and 17 by the sixth, while the recording's **rises** 5-11 dB over frames 2-4 and is still above its first frame's level at 110 ms. (The mid is the mono sum and the whole section leaves it alone, so those are the same numbers before and after the refit.) The engine's low-frequency response to a treble strike is a click where the recording's is a field, and that is a soundboard decay rather than an image: fixing it moves the mono sum and therefore every board in this repository, which is a different milestone and not this one. Item 377 (a)'s other open note is also unchanged and is worth restating rather than quietly dropping: on the melody line of `renders/stereo/STEREO.md` the recording's sixth-octave dip sits at 143-202 Hz (`−0.45`, `−0.43`, `−0.45`, `−0.66`) and the engine's now at 226-254 (`−0.36`, `−0.70`), still about half an octave high — the band aggregates the gate asserts are inside the recording's own take-to-take disagreement, and the curve is right in shape and off in place. All of this is written here with its numbers rather than rounded off, and the gate is at least honest about which window it reads, because there is now only one it can.

392. *(Per-channel fidelity track; reserved range 392-399.)* **Every fidelity gate in this repository scores a mono sum, the one that does not scores a correlation, and between them they left a whole dimension unscored — a listener found it three times before any number did.** The listener's three notes on `renders/stereo/`: the C4 of the melody line stands out, the hammer noise is too loud again, and the reference chords have more brilliance. The hypothesis handed to the forensics was that the virtual pair's *delays* comb each channel and that its side construction lifts broadband content against tonal; `forensics/src/bin/stereo_channels.rs` tested both and **refuted both**, and found one cause under all three. **The pair geometry is exonerated**: analytically its per-channel comb is 0.4-8.0 dB deep with its first notch at 2.7-12.1 kHz, and the realised `|L/R|` fine structure over 1-10 kHz is 22.0-26.3 dB peak-to-trough against the *recording's own* 19.1-25.8 — inside a real AB pair's range. **The side lift does not raise broadband content either**: on the soprano line the strike burst over the note reads −57.45 dB in L and −57.15 in R against −56.41 mono, a net −0.4 dB against a pan-pot, and the largest per-band excursion of channel-pair over mono is +0.8 dB at 2-6 kHz. What is left is `soundboard::ModalLobe`, and it is one line: `side = s + lift·butterworth_band(mid)`, so per frequency **`L = m(1+g)` and `R = m(1−g)` with `g = lift·|B|`**, and the *pair* energy is `2(1+g²)` — **up to +6.18 dB of acoustic energy the mono sum does not contain**, manufactured, with nothing pushing back on `lift` because nothing scored it. Item 370's selling point — "it costs the mono fold-down nothing at any value" — is exactly what made it non-physical: two real capsules straddling a nodal line carry `2p²` *and their mono sum is zero*. The three complaints, in the forensics' own numbers. **(a) C4.** The fitted band's gain peaks at **261.7 Hz; C4 is 261.63 Hz**, half a cent away. Measured through the whole chain, key struck alone at v90, pair-over-mono energy: **C4 +6.42 dB, D4 +4.42, E4 +5.50, F4 +2.41, G4 +3.07** — C4 the loudest note of the line by 0.9 dB over the next and 4.0 over F4 — and adjacent semitones differ by up to **6.7 dB** (B3 +6.74 against A#3 +1.92). All four melody columns are *shape* statistics on the mono fold-down; **the gate has no loudness column at all**, and loudness is what "stands out" means. **(b) The hammers.** The burst is untouched; what moved is the denominator. Attack tonality per channel, engine − recording, over the line's five pitches: **mono −4.30 dB, L +5.05, R −2.60** — and the R column tracks the lobe's R-channel attenuation of that note's fundamental *monotonically* (−2.21, −4.20, −11.08, −20.67, −12.08 dB): the more the lobe nulls the fundamental in one speaker, the noisier that note reads there. **F4 loses 20.7 dB of its own fundamental from the right channel** while the burst is untouched. **(c) The chords.** Brilliance as a 2-6 kHz over 250-500 Hz tilt, engine − reference: **`dmono` is −8.15 dB and identical across the engine, the pair alone and the pan-pot** — which is the control that says the mono deficit is pre-existing (item 343's standing −3.82 dB at 2-6 kHz) and the mic section is innocent of it — while on top of that the lobe adds **−4.95 dB of L-channel brilliance loss**, and it is not a treble loss: 2-6 kHz is −33.39 dB on both takes' L, and the whole of it is 250-500 Hz going **up**, +1.56 against the pair's −3.39. Two more facts worth recording: the deep per-channel notches an earlier verifier called "informational" at −26 dB are **not the delays** but the lobe's unity-gain crossings at **213.0 and 359.6 Hz**, where `1 − g = 0` nulls one channel outright; and the twelfth-order cascade rotates phase by hundreds of degrees inside a 4.5-semitone band, so **which channel is boosted flips with pitch** — C4 L +0.70 / R +9.43 against F4 L +7.17 / R −9.61, the flip landing between D4 and D#4, in the middle of the tune. A nodal line is a frequency-independent sign, not a minimum-phase rotation.

393. *(Per-channel fidelity track.)* **The repair is a phase diffuser on the lobe, four allpass sections, and the recording is what says so.** `soundboard::MIC_MODAL_DIFFUSION`, `soundboard::Allpass1`. **(a) What the recording actually does in the band where its two channels disagree.** `forensics/src/bin/channel_fidelity.rs --stage evidence`, thirty recorded keys at v90, sixth-octave: the median `| |L| − |R| |` of the *recording* reads **2.17 dB at 101 Hz, 2.87 at 127, 2.46 at 143, 3.01 at 160, 3.75 at 180, 2.65 at 202, 4.50 at 226, 3.61 at 254, 3.72 at 285** and 2.0-4.0 all the way to 806 Hz. Its `r0` over that same span goes **+0.954, +0.940, +0.301, +0.065, −0.529, −0.486, −0.364, −0.470, +0.448** and its mid/side ratio +12.2 down to −4.0 dB and back. **The band where the recording's two channels disagree most is not a band where they differ in level at all** — the interchannel level difference is the same 2.5-4.5 dB there as where `r0` is +0.95. An in-phase inversion cannot be that: it produces `20 log10|(1+g)/(1−g)|`, eleven decibels at the fitted lift and unbounded at `g = 1`. **(b) Quadrature is the obvious answer and cannot be built.** `F = ±jg` gives `|L| = |R|` identically, and a causal rational filter whose phase sits at −90° across a band does not exist — phase is monotone through −90° and cannot plateau. Fitted numerically over the shipped band, the best single allpass takes the worst-case interchannel difference from 17.8 dB to 11.2 and no further, and **its optimum is not a fixed multiple of the band**: it moves from 0.6x to 5.1x the centre as the band moves, so it would be a fourth fitted number pretending to be a constant. **(c) What is built instead is what item 377 asked for**: a decorrelation whose target is the recording's own comb statistics rather than zero. Four first-order allpass sections at 1.2x, 1.99x, 3.31x and 5.5x the band's geometric centre. They are **unity-magnitude**, so `|F|` — the whole of what `PHYSICS.md` §8 was fitted on — is untouched, and `Re<L,R> = |m|² − |s|²` whatever the phase, so `r0` and the mid-over-side ratio do not move at fixed `lift`; what they change is `arg F`, so that `cos(arg F)` turns more than a full circle inside each of the scoreboard's bands and the interchannel difference at one frequency becomes a **draw** rather than a fixture. Measured on the shipped band against no diffusion: the interchannel level difference integrated over **125-250 Hz goes from −1.34 dB to +0.57 and over 250-500 Hz from +1.61 to +0.17**. **(d) Four, and all four above the band, because of the strike budget.** Phase rotation *is* group delay — `Δφ = 2π τ Δf`, there is no way to have one without the other — and item 379 established that this band must act on a note's first milliseconds. The band's own twelve poles already spend 6.3 ms at the centre. Eight sections straddling the centre spend 4.2 ms more and put `soundboard::tests::the_mode_controlled_band_reaches_the_direct_path` into the red at **+0.530** over the first 10 ms; four sections *above* the band buy the same rotation inside it for **1.6 ms**, because a section's group delay goes as `1/f` while its phase contribution inside the band does not vanish, and the test passes. That test is the constraint that picked the layout, and it is the reason the answer is four upward rather than eight straddling.

394. *(Per-channel fidelity track.)* **The permanent column: per-channel spectral fidelity, on both boards and on the melody gate, with bars out of the recording's own second take.** `realism::ChannelBand`, `channel_shape`, `channel_columns`, `channel_report`; `melody::pair_over_mono_db` and the `channel` metric; `tuner/tests/stereo.rs::each_loudspeaker_has_the_recordings_spectrum_where_the_mic_pair_acts` and `tuner/tests/melody.rs::the_two_loudspeakers_play_this_line_as_the_recording_does`; *Columns C* in `REALISM.md` and a new table in `STEREO.md`. **The statistic**, per band, per channel: `dev_L(b) = 10 log10(E_L(b)/E_L) − 10 log10(E_M(b)/E_M)` with `M = (L+R)/2`. Three properties and each is load-bearing. It is a **shape**: a gain on the take cancels and so does a gain on one channel, so it needs no level match and the output gain cannot pass or fail it. It is **exactly zero for a pan-potted single note**, because a pan-potted pair's two channels are its mono sum scaled — which makes it a pure measure of what a stereo stage did, referenced to the thing every other board already scores. And **the recording's own is not zero**, so the target is the recording's value and the bar is the recording disagreeing with itself — `max(floor, scatter/sqrt(n)) x 1.25`, `realism::stereo_columns`' construction exactly, never a function of the engine. On the melody line the same idea per note is `10 log10((E_L + E_R)/2 E_M)`, which is the pair-over-mono loudness item 392 measured; it is gated as a **balance**, like `strike`, because the recording has its own value at every note and the question is whether the engine's is the recording's. **One bar had to be given a second term.** The melody's balance bar was the take-to-take median alone, which is right for `strike` — two velocity layers of one key differ by 1.64 dB on attack tonality, because how much hammer noise a take has is a property of that take — and is *not* right for `channel`: the two layers are the same two microphones on the same key, so their interchannel ratio repeats to **0.03 dB**, and a bar of 0.04 dB is a bar on the recording's dither. The second term is `1.4826·MAD / sqrt(n)` over the recorded ladder — how well nine keys pin a median that moves across them — which is `StereoColumn::uncertainty` and item 348's argument, and the larger of the two governs, so **`strike` does not move at all**. **What the gate asserts, and what it does not.** The two bands the mode-controlled lobe acts in — 125-250 and 250-500 Hz — must pass outright, because the lobe is the only thing in the engine that deliberately puts a per-channel spectral difference there and it is what this milestone repaired. Every other band is asserted only against **the pan-pot's own value**: with no microphone section at all the engine is 5.68 dB out at 63-125 and 1.23 at 2-6 kHz, which are other milestones' jobs (item 343's brilliance board owns the second), and asserting them absolutely would be asserting a milestone that has not happened. Asserting that the microphone section does not make them worse is the same "must not have bought its two bands by spending the other four" rule the mode-controlled band already carries.

395. *(Per-channel fidelity track.)* **The diffuser of item 393 does move `r0`, its own source said it does not, and the refit that was supposed to absorb that has no feasible point — measured, not argued.** `soundboard::MIC_MODAL_DIFFUSION`. Item 393(c) reasoned that four unity-magnitude allpasses leave `r0` and the mid-over-side ratio alone "at fixed `lift`", because `Re<L, R> = |m|² − |s|²` whatever the phase between the two. **The identity is exact and the conclusion does not follow**, because `s` is not the lobe: it is `s_geo + F·m`, so `|s|² = |s_geo|² + |F m|² + 2 Re(s_geo* · F · m)`, and rotating `arg F` rotates that cross term. It would vanish only where the geometric side is orthogonal to the mid, and **on the direct path the geometric side is a delayed copy of the mid**, which is as far from orthogonal as a signal gets. Verified by rebuilding both states: with the diffuser removed the coherence gate's 125-250 band **passes**; with it, `r0` reads `+0.096` against the recording's `−0.115`, **0.211 out against a bar of 0.120**. The per-channel board's same band went **1.03 → 2.10** across the same edit, and the melody's per-note pair energy **−0.30 → −0.95 dB** against a bar of 0.91 — so of the two boards item 393 quoted, one improved (250-500: 2.63 → 1.35, still red) and three readings it did not quote turned red. Below the band's centre the rotation also flips which channel the lobe favours, which is how the engine came to read `R −2.22` where the recording reads `−0.12`. **The refit is therefore owed, and it does not exist.** Thirty-odd points were measured over `lo_hz`, `hi_hz`, `lift`, `width` and `diffuse_coherence` on the thirty recorded keys, and the feasible set is empty because a **third constraint** nobody had written down cuts across the other two: the fidelity boards want the band *lower* — at `218-300 Hz × 2.25` the coherence board is green in all six bands (125-250 goes 0.211 → **0.001**), the per-channel 250-500 goes 1.37 → **1.06**, and both pair-energy columns of item 396 come inside their bars — while `tuner/tests/mics.rs::the_estimator_reads_back_a_spacing_the_engine_was_given` wants it *higher*, because the lobe's own group delay biases the interchannel lag the geometry is read back from: below about `lo_hz = 225` the 0.12 m spacing reads back **0.092-0.095 m, −21 to −24 %**, against a tolerance of 20 %, and four lifts from 1.95 to 2.25 do not move that boundary. Nothing between the two is green on both. **`presets/salamander-c5.toml` is therefore unchanged**, the three reds item 393 left are still here, and what this item adds is the reason, the map, and the numbers to resume from rather than a repair.

396. *(Per-channel fidelity track.)* **Two dimensions the per-channel board could not see, which is how the first repair was graded green while a listener still heard it.** `realism::ChannelBand::pair_db`, `realism::ChannelColumn::{pair_balance, per_key_error}`, their asserts in `tuner/tests/stereo.rs`, and a `pair_excess` term in `tools::mics`' objective. **(a) Loudness.** `dev_L` and `dev_R` are *shapes* — each channel's band share against the same take's mono share — and a shape is blind to the thing item 392 convicted: the lobe carries `2(1 + g²)` of pair energy where the fold-down carries `1`, **up to +6.18 dB of it**, and moves no shape at all. `pair_db` is `10 log10((E_L + E_R) / 2 E_M)` per band — `estimate::melody::pair_over_mono_db` per band rather than per note — and it costs nothing, because `channel_band` already had all three energies. It is gated as a **balance**, signed, against the recording's own value, in the two bands the lobe acts in and against the pan-pot elsewhere: item 394's rule for the shape half, applied to the half item 394 did not have. The recording's own is **+3.54 dB at 125-250 and +3.88 at 250-500**, repeating between its two takes to 0.2-0.5 dB; a pan-potted engine's is **exactly zero** in every band, which is what makes it a pure measure of what a stereo stage did. It is **red on the shipped preset** — 125-250 at **−0.83 against a bar of 0.49** — which is the fourth reading item 393 moved and did not report. **(b) The key, not the median.** `per_key_error` was documented as "reported and not gated", and a board right on the median and wrong at every key is exactly the C4-shaped failure at key granularity. It is gated now, in the same two bands, and **against `scatter` — the recording's own key-to-key sigma — rather than against `per_key_floor`**. The floor is the recording against its own second take, **0.11-0.19 dB**: the same key through the same two capsules twice. No microphone model with three fitted numbers can reproduce which plate mode falls where at thirty separate keys, and a bar that demanded it would be a gate nobody could pass and therefore no gate at all; the recording's key-to-key spread is a real ceiling and a measured one, and the engine sits under it in both bands (4.67 against 6.31, 4.15 against 5.99). **What stays unscored, named rather than fixed**: the interchannel *phase* trajectory the diffuser manufactures — −329° of cross-spectrum rotation over 140-560 Hz at C4 against its own recording's +7° — which nothing gates because the recording's own scatter runs to +264° at D#4, so a bar built from it would be ±370° and vacuous; and the residual comb, engine p90 of `| |L| − |R| |` over 100-900 Hz at **7.3-11.0 dB against the real pair's 3.7-6.8** on the same six keys, unbiased now but still about twice as combed as the recording.

397. *(Per-channel fidelity track.)* **Four repairs for the lobe's per-channel bias, built and measured, and what refuses each — including the one that turns every fidelity board green and is still not shippable.** **(i) Diffuse the *composed* side.** `diffuse(s_geo + F·m)` rather than `s_geo + diffuse(F·m)`. It is magnitude-exact — `|A(s_geo + F m)| = |s_geo + F m|` — so every energy `PHYSICS.md` §8 was fitted on returns to its *undiffused* value while the interchannel level split, which is `2 Re(m · s*)` and not an energy, still turns. Measured, at `226-306 Hz × 2.15`: per-channel **125-250 2.10 → 0.86** and **250-500 1.37 → 0.53**, the coherence board green in **all six** bands, the melody line's pair energy **−0.95 → +0.01 dB**, the per-key and pair-energy columns of item 396 green, `soundboard`'s own 25 unit tests green, and the strike budget slightly *better* (−0.0221 → −0.0360). Every fidelity gate in the repository green at once. **It is refused because it rotates the geometric side, which is where the pair's arrival times live**: `the_estimator_reads_back_a_spacing_the_engine_was_given` then recovers none of the three spacings it is given. A pair whose delay cue has been diffused away is not a pair, and the geometry stage is fitted to that cue. A *band-limited* version — the rotation confined to the lobe's own neighbourhood by a **magnitude-exact** complementary split, so the geometry's arrivals pass through untouched — is the open avenue, and a one-pole complement is not magnitude-exact and is not it. **(ii) More sections.** The bias is a residue of too little rotation, and eight first-order sections above the band take 125-250 to **0.70**. They put `the_mode_controlled_band_reaches_the_direct_path` at **+0.336** where it must be negative. That budget is not nearly spent but **exhausted**: the shipped four measure **−0.0221**, so item 393(d)'s "four is what the strike budget allows" is exactly right and there is no room at all. **(iii) Second-order sections.** A first-order allpass ends at −180°, so `cos(arg)` ends at −1 and its rotation reaches every frequency above its corner; a second-order one ends at −360°, where `cos(arg)` is +1 again, so it is asymptotically the identity on *both* sides and its rotation is **local**. Built (`Allpass2`, Regalia-Mitra form, mirror-image coefficients so unity magnitude is exact in the arithmetic): with the composed side it takes the coherence board green in all six bands *and* passes `the_per_channel_column_sees_the_mode_controlled_band_and_only_it`, which the first-order composed form fails at 1.59 dB against 0.97. It over-rotates the 125-250 split to **2.42 dB out** at every layout tried, from `(1.2×, Q0.7)` to `(3.0×, Q1.6)`, two sections and three and four. **(iv) Move the diffuser onto the pair's own side path**, so that deleting `[voicing.mics.modal]` does not delete it and the locality control isolates the band again: the same delay objection as (i), one layer up — `soundboard::tests::the_rendered_delay_is_the_one_the_geometry_asks_for` fails outright, because a diffuser on a *bandless* pair corrupts the arrival times with nothing bought in return. **One thing this milestone did land unaided**: `estimate::melody::tests::a_tail_has_no_strike_in_it` had been red since item 394 — it asserted `METRICS.len() - 1` columns in the tail window, and item 394 added `channel`, a *second* balance metric, so the tail drops two. The count is now derived from `METRIC_IS_BALANCE` and the test asserts the rule (no balance metric survives the tail window) instead of a number that goes stale whenever a metric is added. **The baseline this milestone opened on was 696 green and *two* red, not one.**
396. **The M12 half-repair is reverted; the per-channel work it produced ships without it.** The forensics (392) stand: the modal lobe's in-phase mid/side lift manufactures up to +6.18 dB of per-channel energy the mono sum does not contain, its gain peak sits half a cent from C4's fundamental, and its unity-gain crossings null one loudspeaker outright — the mechanism behind all three of the listener's stereo-render complaints, invisible to every mono board by construction. The attempted allpass-diffusion repair improved the named defects but introduced its own regressions through the side/mid cross term (393-395 record them), and the parameter refit was proven infeasible: fidelity wants the lobe band lower, the spacing-readback test wants it higher (a bias of OUR estimator's lag reading under the lobe's group delay, not physics), and the feasible set is empty. engine/src/soundboard.rs is reverted to its pre-M12 state; kept are the per-channel fidelity columns and gates (the gate `each_loudspeaker_has_the_recordings_spectrum_where_the_mic_pair_acts` is the project's third documented red, failing on the shipped engine by design until the mechanism milestone lands), the L/R noise reading, the forensic instrument, and the 2.87x parallelized fit tooling. The mechanism milestone's mandate, from the evidence: an energy-conserving directivity (Givens rotation — a real nodal line costs the mono sum, and the REFERENCE's mono carries the same nodal behaviour, so mono boards must be re-scored against it rather than assumed frozen), and group-delay compensation in the spacing estimator so the false constraint dissolves.

398. *(Mechanism track; reserved range 398-409.)* **The engine's renders are content-keyed on disk, and the yardstick fit is 8.3x faster the second time it is asked.** `piano_tuner::renders`, `cache::engine_dir`, `tuner/tests/engine_cache.rs`, and the render paths of `tools::mics`, `tuner/tests/stereo.rs` and `tuner/tests/mics.rs`. **What was already true and what was missing.** `cache::` held the *reference* side — the Salamander recordings played by `sampler` — because that side does not move when the engine does; the engine side, which is where every stage-2 fit spends its wall time, was recomputed on every invocation and on every revisit inside one invocation. **The key is the whole of the input**, in the discipline item 284's reference cache set: the preset's own TOML bytes (`Preset::to_toml`, the only description both crates agree on), the **engine**, and the material. The engine has no version number to read, so it is fingerprinted by *what it sounds like* — `engine_fingerprint` renders two short probes through this crate's own render path and hashes their samples, one through the pan-pot branch and one through a full `[voicing.mics]` with a mode-controlled band, so a change to **either** branch of `soundboard` misses the whole cache by construction. That is `tuner/tests/calibration.rs`'s `corpus_base` applied to audio instead of trajectories, and it is the reason there is no refresh flag: an entry is either the answer to exactly this question or it is not read. **Bit-identical, proved rather than asserted**: entries are 32-bit float WAV, and `a_cached_engine_render_is_the_render_it_replaces` renders one note both ways and compares samples; `anything_that_would_change_the_render_lands_on_a_different_entry` moves the preset, the key, the velocity, the duration and the preroll one at a time and counts six entries. **Measured, on the throughput yardstick item 392 built for exactly this** (`mics --stage grid`, a fixed 81 candidates x 36 renders): **174.5 s cold against 21.1 s warm**, 2.15 s per candidate against 0.26, 17 renders/s against 138 — **8.3x**, on top of item 392's 2.87x from parallelism. **What is *not* keyed, and why the honest answer is the whole preset.** A per-key slice was considered and refused on a measurement rather than out of caution: `Engine::new` builds a `ResonanceBus` from the whole preset and the top octave's strings have **no dampers**, so one note's render is not a function of that note's own entries. What a whole-preset key still buys for an incremental fit is the useful half — a search that revisits a candidate, and a per-key bisection that builds one preset per step, so every key it is not moving is a hit on the next round. Entries are large (1.9 MB each, 5.5 GB for one grid), so the directory carries a **budget** — 32 GB, `ENGINE_CACHE_MB` — pruned oldest-first when a handle opens; eviction costs a re-render and can never return a wrong answer, which is the same argument that licenses the cache in the first place.

399. *(Mechanism track.)* **The mode-controlled band is a rotation now, it is exactly energy-conserving at every frequency, and the identity that makes it so needs no design.** `soundboard::ModalRotation`, `band_allpasses`, `Biquad::{allpass, allpass1}`, `MIC_MODAL_PROTOTYPE`; `[voicing.mics.modal]`'s `lift` becomes `rotation_deg` and `quadrature_hz`. **The defect.** Both previous versions left `(L + R)/2` bit-identical at every setting and advertised it: no scoreboard moved when the section did. Item 392 is what that cost — `side = s + lift·B(mid)` makes `L = m(1+g)` and `R = m(1−g)`, so the *pair* carries `2(1+g²)` where the sum carries `1`, **up to +6.18 dB of acoustic energy the mono sum does not contain**. Two capsules straddling a nodal line carry `2p²` *and their sum is zero*: the energy in the two loudspeakers and the energy missing from their sum are the same energy. **The mechanism** is therefore `mid' = cos(t)·mid`, `side' = side + sin(t)·mid` — the mid is turned into the side rather than copied into it — so the fold-down pays `−20 log10 cos t` inside the band, which is exactly the `pair_db` the pair gains. `the_mode_controlled_band_costs_the_mono_sum_exactly_the_energy_the_pair_gains` asserts both halves end to end through the instrument, at the band's centre and two octaves either side. **The realisation is an identity, not a filter design.** Build `C` and `S` out of a bandpass `B` as `1 − (1−cos t)B` and `sin(t)B` and the energy is conserved only where `|B|` is 0 or 1 — everywhere else, which is most of a band, the pair gains or loses. Build them out of **two allpasses** as `C = cos²(t/2)A_a + sin²(t/2)A_b` and `S = sin(t)(A_a − A_b)/2` and `|C|² + |S|² = (c+s)² = 1` follows from `|A| = 1` alone, at every frequency, **for any two allpasses**: the design's only remaining job is the *shape*, and it is the classical allpass decomposition of an odd-order Butterworth pair (Regalia, Mitra & Vaidyanathan 1988) through Constantinides' lowpass-to-bandpass transformation. `the_mode_controlled_band_is_a_rotation_and_conserves_the_pairs_energy` sweeps 400 log-spaced frequencies over five bands and five angles including both rails and reads the worst deviation at **under 1e-5**. **Why the geometry survives it, which is what refused all four of item 397's repairs.** Outside the band `A_b → A_a`, so `C → A_a` and `S → 0`: **both paths carry the same allpass and the pair's differential is exactly one**, so the interchannel arrival times the whole geometry stage is fitted to pass through untouched — `the_rotation_opens_the_image_and_leaves_the_arrivals_alone` reads the delay back with a band in the way and gets the geometry's own. That is item 397's named open avenue, "a magnitude-exact complementary split", built. **The order is a measurement**: `MIC_MODAL_PROTOTYPE = 3`, an order-six band at 18 dB/octave. Five was tried and its pole partition is wrong — the shape is not monotone, reading 0.42 of its angle an octave under the band where three reads 0.03 — and it is left at three rather than debugged because three is what the boards close on. **And the piece a rotation alone still cannot do.** A rotation gives `|L|/|R| = (cos t + sin t)/(cos t − sin t)`: **+21.2 dB** at the band centre at 50 degrees, unbounded at 45, against the recording's own `| |L| − |R| |` of 2.5-4.5 dB *everywhere*, no larger where its channels anti-correlate than where they agree (item 393(a)). What equalises them is a **phase** between what the band moves into the side and what it leaves in the mid, and `S` may carry a further allpass of its own without disturbing `|C|² + |S|² = 1` at all, because `|D| = 1`. One first-order section through −90 degrees at `quadrature_hz` x the band's centre takes the centre from **+21.16 dB to 0.00**. Item 393(b) refused this shape because a causal filter cannot *hold* −90 degrees across a band; over the 0.6-octave band it is asked for, it holds −67 to −114 degrees, and where it is centred decides which half of the band favours which loudspeaker — which the recording has an answer to, since its own `dev_L`/`dev_R` split changes sign between 125-250 and 250-500. So it is a fitted number rather than a constant.

400. *(Mechanism track.)* **The spacing-readback constraint that emptied item 395's feasible set was our estimator's, and it is gone: the reading is corrected exactly, the bar did not move, and the calibration constant is now a property of the geometry alone.** `estimate::mics::{ModalStage, interchannel_lag_through, ENGINE_LAG_PER_ITD}`, `soundboard::ModalResponse`, `renders::ModalRotationStage`, and `tuner/tests/mics.rs::undoing_the_mode_controlled_band_returns_the_reading_to_the_geometrys_own`. **What item 395 measured and what it charged it to.** Below about `lo_hz = 225` the 0.12 m spacing read back **0.092-0.095 m, −21 to −24 %** against a tolerance of 20 %, and four lifts did not move the boundary; the conclusion was that the fidelity boards' preferred band was out of reach and the feasible set empty. It was the **estimator** that could not go below 225 Hz. Any stage between the geometry and the two channels that is not *common* to both moves a phase-transform delay reading, and the correction is not a group-delay subtraction: measured on the engine's own renders, three first-order allpasses on the side path with `tau` of 0.96, 1.40 and 1.57 ms move the reading by 312, 818 and 537 microseconds — not by their group delay, not monotonically in it — while the *same* allpass on both channels moves it by **0.0**. **What is built is the stage's own arithmetic run backwards**, per bin, over exactly the bins the delay is read from: `soundboard::modal_rotation_undo` returns `(A_a/C, S/C)`, both bounded because `|C|` is one outside the band and `cos(t)` inside it and never zero, and `A_a·mid = (A_a/C)·m'`, `A_a·side = s' − (S/C)·m'` recovers the geometry's pair up to the common allpass no interchannel measurement can see. It lives in the engine because there is one design of the filter and it is `soundboard`'s; `estimate::mics` takes it as a caller-supplied trait so the tuner's *library* half still links without the engine. **Measured, at four bands including two under the 225 Hz item 395 could not pass:** uncorrected bias −13, −21, −111 and **−379 microseconds** at 229-307, 218-300, 180-260 and 160-240 Hz; corrected, **−0 microseconds at every one of them**. **`ENGINE_LAG_PER_ITD` is re-derived and it stops depending on the voicing.** `|median lag|·c/d` over the eleven bass keys at three spacings an octave apart reads **1.332, 1.382 and 1.389** with the stage undone — a spread of **±2.1 %** where the same three spread ±3.5 % under the shipped lobe and ±8.3 % under a lower one — so the constant is **1.368**, their geometric mean, where it was 1.58 measured *under a particular lobe*. The three spacings now read back at −2.6 %, +1.0 % and +1.5 % against the **unchanged** 20 % tolerance: the fix is in the reading, not the bar, and the test prints its three numbers whether it passes or not so the calibration is re-derivable rather than only visible in a failure.

401. *(Mechanism track.)* **What the refit bought, what it did not, and the one red that is left with its reason.** `presets/salamander-c5.toml`'s `[voicing.mics]` refitted by `piano-tuner mics --stage band` over six knobs — `width`, `diffuse_coherence`, and the band's `lo_hz`, `hi_hz`, `rotation_deg`, `quadrature_hz` — on the same two surfaces and against the same bars as item 363. **The coherence gate is green in all six bands for the first time.** `the_engines_stereo_image_is_the_recordings_in_every_band` on the thirty recorded keys: **0 red, 1.25 bars out** against the shipped preset's 2 red and 15.13, with 125-250 at **−0.062 against the recording's −0.115** (bar 0.120) where the in-phase lobe read +0.564, and 250-500 at **−0.172 against −0.226** (bar 0.059) where it read +0.365. Both held-out velocity layers, which no fit saw, go **4 red to 2** — v68 from 15.33 bars out to 1.95, v116 from 20.00 to 3.03. **Both `pair_db` columns of item 396 are green**: 125-250 reads **−0.09 dB against a bar of 0.49** where the shipped preset read −2.02, and 250-500 **−0.16 against 0.38** where it read −1.72, which is the energy half of item 392 closed. **The mono fold-down did not pay for it**, on the column built to catch that: `ChannelColumn::mono_balance` on the same thirty keys reads **+0.26, −0.13, +0.83, +0.03** dB in the four bands under 2 kHz, all inside their bars, so the rotation's `−20 log10 cos(θ)` — 6.5 dB at the band's own centre — costs the *octave-band* fold-down a few tenths, because the band is 0.96 octaves wide inside two scoreboard bands that are one octave each and a piano note's energy in either is mostly elsewhere. That is a measurement and not a reprieve: the sixth-octave cost is real and it is what the boards in `renders/` will show. **The gate that is still red is the per-channel *shape* in 125-250 Hz, at 2.34 dB against a bar of 1.15**, and the reason is the one item 393(b) named and this milestone only halved. The rotation gives `|L|/|R| = (cos θ + sin θ)/(cos θ − sin θ)` — **+21.2 dB** at 50 degrees — and `quadrature_hz` takes that to **0.00 at its own corner** and to ±11 dB half an octave either side, because a causal first-order allpass passes through −90 degrees rather than sitting there. The fit puts the corner at **1.109x the band's centre**, which lands the equal-level point at 249 Hz, in between the two bands, and 125-250 then reads **L +3.32 / R −2.46 against the recording's +1.40 / −0.12**: the right *sign* on both channels, twice the size. The other three reds on that board are outside the lobe entirely — 63-125 at 1.21/0.50 and 500-2k's pair energy at +0.86/0.35 are the pan-pot's and the geometry's, they are what a preset with no microphone section at all reads, and the gate asserts them only against that. **What is named rather than fixed.** (a) `diffuse_coherence` finished at **7.9, its search rail**, which by item 363's own rule means the material wanted more of it than the model can give and the number should not be read as a measurement. (b) The phrase surface disagrees more than it did: 250-500 goes from +0.321 to **−0.131 against the recording's +0.348**, four of six red, which is item 372's two-surface disagreement widened by a stage that is fitted on notes. (c) The quadrature is one first-order section at one corner; the recording's own `dev_L`/`dev_R` sign change between the two bands is a *shape* over frequency, and matching it needs the phase to be −90 over a range rather than at a point — which is where the next order of this mechanism goes. **Budget.** The band fit was time-boxed at one hour of wall clock and used 52 minutes: 81 grid cells, four relaxed rounds, two constrained rounds and the diagonal refinement, **75,000 engine renders**, of which 7 % were cache hits only because the cache was pruned by hand mid-run to keep the disk under control — which is what `PRUNE_EVERY` now does by itself.

402. *(Mechanism track.)* **The gates, in full, including the three this milestone turned red and did not close.** `cargo test --release --workspace --no-fail-fast`: **701 green, 5 red**, against a baseline of 698 and 2. **Green, and new**: the coherence gate `the_engines_stereo_image_is_the_recordings_in_every_band` in all six bands for the first time (item 401); `undoing_the_mode_controlled_band_returns_the_reading_to_the_geometrys_own` and `the_estimator_reads_back_a_spacing_the_engine_was_given` at −2.6/+1.0/+1.5 % against the unchanged 20 % (item 400); `the_mode_controlled_band_is_a_rotation_and_conserves_the_pairs_energy`, `the_rotation_happens_inside_the_declared_band_and_not_outside_it`, `the_mode_controlled_band_costs_the_mono_sum_exactly_the_energy_the_pair_gains` and `the_rotation_opens_the_image_and_leaves_the_arrivals_alone` (item 399); and the three of `tests/engine_cache.rs` (item 398). **Re-pinned, with the measurement**: `the_mono_fold_down_and_the_preset_without_the_section_are_both_unmoved` becomes `the_mono_fold_down_moves_only_where_the_nodal_line_is_and_by_what_it_costs`. Its old claim — the fold-down is the pan-pot's *with the band in* — is precisely what item 392 convicted, so it now asserts three things instead: the **geometry alone** still folds down to the pan-pot's own sum to `f32` rounding (0.006 dB worst band), the band costs the fold-down **only where it reaches** (worst 0.006 dB in a scoreboard band the rotation's own `|S|` never exceeds 0.05 in, against −5.05 dB at 125-250 and −1.82 at 250-500 where it does), and it **must** cost something there, because a nodal line that costs the sum nothing is manufacturing the pair energy it produces. The window that moved is stated in the test and its size is the rotation's own `−20 log10 cos θ`. **The two that were already red** are `each_loudspeaker_has_the_recordings_spectrum_where_the_mic_pair_acts` — this milestone's own acceptance, still red on the 125-250 *shape* at 2.34 dB against 1.15 for the reason item 401 gives, with its `pair_db` and `per_key` halves now green — and `a_known_duplex_comes_back_from_the_engines_own_render_of_it`, which was verified red on the pre-milestone tree by stashing this one and running it, so it is not this milestone's. **The three that are new, named rather than papered over.** (a) `the_capsule_pair_without_the_mode_controlled_band_fails_in_the_middle` fails on its *outside* clause, not its inside one: with the band deleted, 63-125 reads **0.0126 against a bar of 0.0092** where it must still pass, because the refit took `diffuse_coherence` to its rail and the rail is what carries that band. With the band in, the same column reads 0.009 and passes — so the instrument that ships is right there and the control's premise, "the band must not be what carries a band the pair already had", is now false by a third of a bar. That is a real finding about the fit, not about the mechanism, and the repair is a `diffuse_coherence` that is not at a rail. (b) `the_hammer_is_no_louder_against_the_note_than_the_pianos_is` reads the melody's `strike` balance at **−2.11 dB against a bar of 2.05** — three per cent over, where it was inside before. This is the rotation's mono cost reaching a mono board: `strike` is attack tonality on the fold-down, and the fold-down lost 5.05 dB in 125-250 Hz on the demo. The per-channel board's own `mono_balance` column does **not** see it (+0.26/−0.13/+0.83/+0.03 dB, all inside), because that column is an octave-band share over thirty single notes and this one is a 30 ms window over a five-note line.
