Both are true; the finer statistic is the one that binds, and it says the mandate's premise — that the reference's mono carries the nodal band's cost and the engine's therefore has room to pay it — is right in the octave and wrong in the third. (c) `the_tail_gate_fails_without_the_drawn_low_band_at_c4` is the same cause one control further out. **None of these were closed inside the milestone's budget and none of their bars were touched.**

403. *(Mechanism track.)* **The per-channel acceptance was not held red by the mechanism's arithmetic, it was held red by a rail in our own bounds — and the rail had been derived for a band four times wider than the one it was applied to.** `soundboard::MIC_MODAL_QUADRATURE`. Item 401 left `each_loudspeaker_has_the_recordings_spectrum_where_the_mic_pair_acts` red on the 125-250 Hz *shape* at **2.34 dB against a bar of 1.15** — engine `L +3.32 / R −2.46` against the recording's `+1.40 / −0.12` — and charged it to the mechanism: a rotation puts `|L|/|R| = (cos t + sin t)/(cos t − sin t)`, **+21.2 dB** at 50 degrees, and one first-order allpass through −90 degrees equalises that at a *point* rather than across a band. Every word of that is true and none of it was what was binding. **Where the split is actually zero.** The interchannel level difference vanishes exactly where `arg(S/C) = ±90 deg`, and `arg(S/C) = arg(w) + arg(D)` with `w = cos(t)·sin^2(phi/2) + j·sin(phi/2)cos(phi/2)` a function of the band's own branch-phase difference `phi`. So the quadrature section's corner decides **which half of the band favours which loudspeaker**, and the whole of 125-250 Hz sat on one side of it, because the fitted corner was at `1.109 x` the band's centre — **287 Hz, above the band**. Swept down on the gate's own material with nothing else moved: **2.34 at 1.109x, 1.98 at 0.7x, 1.52 at 0.5x** — and 0.5 was `MIC_MODAL_QUADRATURE`'s rail with the search held at 0.52, so item 401's 52-minute six-knob fit spent that axis against a boundary and reported the boundary's residual as the mechanism's. **The rail is not a constant and the arithmetic that produced it says so**: a first-order section reads `−2 atan(rho/q)` at an edge `rho = sqrt(hi/lo)` times the centre, and "not past −170 degrees by the top edge" is `q >= rho/tan(85 deg)` — **0.18** for the two-octave band the number was justified on, **0.12** for the 0.96-octave band that was fitted. **Below the rail the acceptance closes outright.** At `0.25x`, with the trims refitted around it (`width` 1.55, `diffuse_coherence` 2.4, rotation 65 deg): 125-250 shape **2.34 → 0.45 against 1.15**, its `pair_db` −0.09 → **−0.10 against 0.49**, 250-500 shape **0.98 against 1.09**, both `per_key` halves green, the coherence gate green in **all six** bands, and `the_capsule_pair_without_the_mode_controlled_band_fails_in_the_middle` green as well because the refit took `diffuse_coherence` off its rail — `cargo test -p piano-tuner --test stereo` **9 of 9**. That is the whole of item 402's stereo accounting turned green, and it is why this milestone did not end where item 396 did.

404. *(Mechanism track.)* **And it is still refused, because three gates in this repository want three different settings of the same two knobs, and the sweeps do not cross.** The state above turns `tuner/tests/stereo.rs` green and `tuner/tests/melody.rs` red, and the mandate's acceptance is both at once. Measured, each on the surface the gate itself reads, everything else held: **(a) `quadrature_hz` — the per-channel acceptance against the melody's `channel` balance.** Corner at `0.25 / 0.40 / 0.55 / 0.70 / 1.109 x` the centre: the 125-250 Hz shape reads **0.30 / 0.73 / 1.67 / 1.98 / 2.34** against a bar of **1.15**, and the melody line's pair-over-mono reads **−1.27 / −1.16 / −1.06 / −0.97 / −0.82** against a bar of **0.91**. One wants the corner under 0.45 and the other wants it over 0.8. **They do not cross.** `width` and `diffuse_coherence` do not rescue it: at the widest setting the geometry allows (1.85, 1.99) three stereo gates go red at once, and at the shipped width the melody column still reads −1.27. **(b) `rotation_deg` — the coherence board against the melody's `strike` balance.** At **45 / 55 / 62.1 / 65 degrees** the melody's attack-tonality balance reads **−1.58 / −2.01 / −2.11 / −2.15** against a bar of **2.05**, so the fold-down's own gate allows about 55 degrees; at 45 and 55 the coherence gate and the per-channel gate are both red, and the coherence board needs 62 or more. **The rotation's mono cost is what does it** — `−20 log10 cos(t)`, 6.6 dB at 62 degrees and 8.6 at 65 — and item 402(b) had already seen the first three per cent of it. **(c) `hi_hz` — the sixth-octave board against the coherence board.** The new board of item 405 reads, at an upper edge of **265 / 300 / 362 Hz**: **0 / 1 / 3** rows further from the recording than deleting the band altogether, while the coherence gate's 250-500 column reads **0.746 / 0.546 / 0.017** against a bar of 0.059. Nothing between them is inside on both, and the reason is a property of the filter: the recording's own pair-over-mono falls from **+5.66 dB at 252 Hz to +1.58 at 283** — *adjacent* sixth-octaves — and an order-six band at 18 dB per octave has no skirt that steep. **Three independent conflicts, three swept axes, no feasible point.** So `engine/src/soundboard.rs`, `engine/src/preset.rs` and `presets/salamander-c5.toml` are **reverted to the committed instrument**, exactly as item 396 reverted M12 and for a better-measured reason; the sounding path is byte-identical to the last commit, no render in `renders/` moves, and `each_loudspeaker_has_the_recordings_spectrum_where_the_mic_pair_acts` is the project's third documented red again.

405. *(Mechanism track.)* **What is kept, because it is what would have caught this before the milestone started: the per-channel board at a sixth of an octave, and the fold-down's real headroom measured against the band-deleted preset.** `realism::{STEREO_FINE_BANDS, ChannelShape::fine, channel_fine_columns}`, printed by `each_loudspeaker_has_the_recordings_spectrum_where_the_mic_pair_acts`. **The gap it closes.** `pair_db` and `mono_db` are shapes over frequency; a mode-controlled band is about an octave wide; the two scoreboard bands that contain it are an octave each. So a band whose angle is right at its bottom edge and eight decibels too large at its top averages into a column reading **−0.09 against a bar of 0.49** and calls itself green — which is exactly what item 401 shipped and item 402 graded. At a sixth of an octave on the same thirty renders, item 401's instrument reads **+10.35 dB of pair-over-mono at 283 Hz where the recording reads +1.58**, and a fold-down **7.2 dB below** the recording's in the same band. **The control is the same preset with `[voicing.mics.modal]` deleted, and it is the load-bearing half.** An absolute sixth-octave bar would be a gate nobody could pass — the engine's own fold-down is two to three decibels off the recording's at 100, 112, 126 and 713 Hz, in bands no microphone section touches, which is the voicing's business. The capsule pair alone is the right reference because this repository already proves what it is: its fold-down is the pan-pot's own to `f32` rounding, so **its distance from the recording is the headroom a nodal line has to spend**. Measured, per sixth-octave, with the band deleted: **+2.36 dB at 200 Hz, +2.70 at 224, +7.93 at 252, +0.78 at 283** — and a 65-degree rotation spends **8.6 dB** at its own centre. That is the mandate's enabling fact (a) measured on both sides at last: the reference's mono *does* carry the nodal band's cost, and the engine's mono has already inherited most of it through the mono fit, so the headroom left is **one to three decibels where the mechanism needs six to nine**. It runs out one sixth-octave before the band does, which is why 252 Hz is inside on the fold-down and 283 Hz is not.

406. *(Mechanism track.)* **What the next mechanism has to be, from the measurement rather than from a preference — and the gates as they now stand.** **(a) The angle has to be a function of frequency, and a Butterworth band cannot make it one.** `forensics/src/bin/mono_mechanism.rs --stage theta` inverts the Givens angle the recording's own `r0` asks for, per sixth-octave, against the engine's own geometric mid/side: **69.2 deg at 180 Hz, 57.8 at 202, 50.6 at 226, 45.0 at 254, 38.5 at 285**, and effectively nothing by 320 — a peak an octave *below* where item 401's flat top sat, and a fall of thirty degrees across three sixth-octaves. A constant angle over a band is the wrong **shape**, not merely the wrong size, and no setting of `(lo_hz, hi_hz, rotation_deg)` turns a flat top into that ramp. What would: the exactly energy-conserving *and* exactly level-balanced form is `C = (A + B)/2`, `S = (A − B)/2` for two allpasses — a fact, not a design: `|C|^2 + |S|^2 = 1` and `Re(C S*) = 0` follow from `|A| = |B| = 1` alone, and conversely any pair with both properties **is** such a decomposition. Its angle is *half the branch phase difference*, so `theta(f)` becomes a phase-difference design and the peak, the width and the two skirts are four handles instead of one. That also removes `quadrature_hz` entirely, and with it conflict 404(a), because the two loudspeakers then carry equal levels by construction. **(b) The fold-down needs the energy back before any of it will hold.** 404(b) and 405 are the same wall from two sides: the cost is real, the headroom is one to three decibels, and no filter design changes that. Giving it back is a **soundboard** milestone — a voicing that radiates more in 175-300 Hz so that the pair's own cancellation lands on the recording's mono rather than under it — and it moves every mono board in the repository, which is why it is named here rather than attempted inside a microphone milestone. **(c) The gates.** `cargo test --release --workspace --no-fail-fast`: **701 passed, 2 failed**, against the baseline of 698 and 2. The delta this milestone owns is **+3 green and no new red** — the three of `tuner/tests/engine_cache.rs`, item 398's content-keyed engine-render cache, which is mechanism-independent and is kept and wired back into `piano-tuner mics`. Its speed-up is **re-measured and smaller than item 398 claimed**: on the same `--stage grid` yardstick, **132.2 s cold against 92.7 s warm, 1.43x**, because only the *key* renders go through it and the six phrases per candidate — the long ones — are a different shape from `renders::NoteSpec` and are still rendered every time. The two reds are the two that were already named: item 260's duplex baseline, and this milestone's own acceptance. **What is reverted and what is kept**, so that nothing is half: `soundboard`, `preset` and `presets/salamander-c5.toml` are the committed instrument byte for byte (`renders/` is untouched and no re-render was needed); items 399's rotation, 400's estimator compensation and 401's refit are gone with them, and 400's finding stands on the record — the spacing-readback constraint of item 395 **was** our estimator's rather than physics, and it will be re-derivable the moment a mechanism needs it. Kept: the render cache (398), the sixth-octave board (405), the `mono_db`/`mono_balance` column it is built on, and `mono_mechanism`, which is the instrument that measured the headroom this milestone died of.

407. *(Mono-source track; reserved range 407-419.)* **The blocker item 406 named is real and the sign of its diagnosis is the other way round: the engine's mono is not hot in the nodal band because a fit folded a microphone artifact *into* the source, it is short of the headroom a nodal mechanism needs because a fit folded the artifact's *hole* into the source — and the +7.9 dB the sixth-octave board reads at 252 Hz is the sub-fundamental floor under the treble keys, not C4's source gain.** Four new sections of `forensics/src/bin/mono_mechanism.rs`, all on the thirty recorded keys at v90 that every stereo board uses: `keys` (per key, per sixth-octave, with each key's share of the pooled reference energy beside it), `report_halo` (both sides of the comparison at 1/24 octave, with the recording's own pair-over-mono in the same bands), `report_parts` (the same statistic through seven instruments that differ in one stage each) and `report_headroom` (what a mechanism needs against what the fitted knobs reach). **(a) The 252 Hz row is a median, and the median is not the band's own keys.** `ChannelColumn::mono_balance` is a median over thirty keys of a whole-take-normalised band share. Of the recording's pooled energy in the 238-267 Hz band, **C3 carries 42 % and C4 38 %**, and those two keys read **+1.4 and +4.2 dB** — while every key from D#3 upward reads **+4.7 to +16.8 dB carrying 1-2 % each** (A5 +11.1, C6 +9.4, F#6 +11.0, C7 +11.0, D#7 +11.2, A7 +11.5), and there are enough of them to set the median at +6.8 on the file's own span-normalised grid and +7.93 on the board's. A treble key has no partial within two octaves of 252 Hz; what is being compared there is the shape of its low-frequency floor. "Half a cent from C4" is where the sixth-octave grid falls, not where the defect is. **(b) At 1/24 octave, the hole is the recording's.** Pooled, level-matched on 100-810 Hz: at 252 Hz the *recording's* own mono share is **−31.56 dB** and the engine's is **−22.08**; at 126 Hz the recording's is **−16.75** and the engine's **−31.61**. The recording's pooled pair-over-mono at those same 1/24-octave points reads **+7.04 at 252, +7.41 at 238, +8.75 at 346, +10.32 at 400 and +9.92 at 183** — the two capsules carry the energy its mono sum does not, which is a nodal line and is exactly what item 392 convicted the *engine* of manufacturing. Where the recording's mono is not cancelled — 100-160, 291-317, 519-617 Hz — the engine tracks it to ±2 dB. **(c) Which stage carries it: none of the ones with a knob.** The same pooled 1/24-octave statistic through `soundboard.body_modes` flattened, `voicing.bridge.peaks` flattened, `board_mix` at zero, `voicing.resonance_coupling` at zero, `[noise.strike]` silenced, and all three of the last together. At 252 Hz: bare **+9.48**, no body modes +9.27, no bridge peaks +9.50, no sympathetic +9.48, no strike noise +9.48, no board path +7.78, **strings alone +7.78**. At 126 Hz: −14.87 / −10.92 / −14.93 / −14.88 / −14.87 / −6.14 / −6.16. The comb is the **strings' own direct radiated path**. The bridge backbone and peaks are the *resonance bus's* admittance (`resonance.rs`) and shape the sympathetic halo and the partials' decay, not the radiated spectrum — the ablation is what says so rather than a reading of the code; `soundboard.body_modes` exist only between 42 and 388 Hz and reach the output through `board_mix`'s 35 %, and they are visible only at 141-163 Hz (159 goes −4.30 to −15.13 when they are flattened) and nowhere else in the band.

408. *(Mono-source track.)* **The headroom, measured on both sides, and how far the fitted parameters reach into it: 1.7 dB of a 9.0 dB requirement at 180 Hz, and the wrong way at three of the other bands.** An energy-conserving mechanism costs the fold-down exactly the pair energy it adds, so the source must stand `REF pair − ENG(bare) pair` decibels **above** the recording's own mono *before* the mechanism, or the fold-down lands under the recording once it is. **Required against standing, per sixth-octave**, pooled and level-matched on the thirty keys: **180 Hz +8.97 required, +0.04 standing**; 202 **+5.47 / +0.64**; 226 **+3.96 / −0.30**; 254 **+3.02 / +3.76**; 285 +2.15 / +0.97; 403 +4.32 / +1.94; 508 +4.89 / −0.70. **Only 254 Hz has the headroom it needs**, by a surplus of 0.74 dB — the band item 405 and the mandate both read as the worst offender is the one band of the five that is already right. **What the knobs reach.** `soundboard.body_modes` between 140 and 400 Hz at two, four and eight times their gains, and at eight with `board_mix` opened from 0.35 to 0.6: at 180 Hz the standing +0.04 becomes **+0.93, +1.78, +1.30, −0.50** — the whole travel is **1.7 dB against 9.0**, and it turns back on itself past x4 because a resonator bank that large stops being a colouration of the drive and becomes the drive. At 202 Hz every setting moves **away** (+0.64 → +0.32 → −0.26 → −1.77 → −5.12), and so do 403 (+1.94 → −2.67) and 508 (−0.70 → −3.22). **And the one fitted parameter that *is* the radiated spectrum reaches no further.** `notes.partial_gains` is per key and per partial; lifting **every partial whose own frequency lands in 160-300 Hz by 9 dB** — the deficit 180 Hz asks for — moves 180 Hz to **+2.92**, 202 to +3.24, 226 to +3.45 and 254 to +5.35 (2.3 dB past its target), while taking 101 from −3.19 to **−4.39**, 143 from −1.00 to **−4.12** and 403 from +1.94 to **+0.56** through the renormalisation the level match performs. Nine decibels of partial gain buys **2.9 dB** of the band, because at 180 Hz about 40 % of the pooled reference energy is the treble keys' sub-fundamental floor and a per-partial table cannot reach a frequency where no partial is. **There is no fitted parameter in this engine that shapes the radiated response *between* a key's partials**, and that — not a filter design, not a rail in a bound — is what empties the feasible set.

409. *(Mono-source track.)* **So the mechanism is not started, and the arithmetic of the refusal is item 406's own table read against item 408's.** `mono_mechanism --stage theta`. The Givens angle inverted from the recording's `r0` against the engine's geometric mid/side is **69.2 deg at 180 Hz, 57.8 at 202, 50.6 at 226, 45.0 at 254, 38.6 at 285** and effectively nothing by 320, and its mono cost `−10 log10 cos^2 theta` is **+8.98, +5.47, +3.96, +3.02, +2.14 dB** — the same numbers as item 408's "required" column, which is the identity `cost = pair gained` closing on itself and is the check that both derivations are the same measurement. **Cost less headroom: +8.95, +4.84, +4.27, −0.74, +1.17.** Applied end to end to the engine's own rendered pairs and re-measured rather than predicted, the fold-down moves by **−7.17 dB at 180, −6.21 at 202, −4.12 at 226**, and the engine's mono then sits **−6.38, −5.20 and −4.26 dB under the recording's own**. **The successor form of item 406(a) does not change this and cannot.** `C = (A + B)/2`, `S = (A − B)/2` gives `|C| = |cos(Δφ/2)|`, so the fold-down pays `−20 log10 cos(theta)` whatever realises `theta`; what the form buys is that `theta` becomes a function of frequency and that `|L| = |R|` holds by construction, which dissolves item 404(a) — the `quadrature_hz` conflict, since there is no quadrature knob — and item 404(c) — the skirt, since a phase-difference design has four handles where a Butterworth band has one. **Item 404(b), the mono cost against the melody's `strike` balance, is untouched**, and it is now quantified at the frequency the recording actually asks for the angle rather than at a flat band's centre: 8.98 dB at 180 Hz where item 404(b) measured the fold-down's gate failing at 6.6.

410. *(Mono-source track.)* **What ships, what the next milestone is, and the gates.** **Ships:** nothing in `engine/`, `tuner/` or `presets/`. The sounding path is byte-identical to the last commit, no render in `renders/` moves and none was re-rendered, and the only file that changed is `forensics/src/bin/mono_mechanism.rs`, which is outside the workspace's default members. **The next milestone, from the measurement:** the direct path needs a *radiated response between the partials* — a fitted, minimum-phase colouration of the mono drive with sixth-octave resolution over 100-800 Hz — and the fit that writes it must fit the recording's mono **divided by the pair's own mono transfer**, not the recording's mono itself, or it will inherit the hole again exactly as `partial_gains` did. Both halves are measurable today: the transfer is `REF pair − ENG(bare) pair` per band (item 408's column) and the target is the recording's pooled level-matched mono share (`mono_mechanism`'s section 1). It is a soundboard milestone in the sense item 406(b) meant — it moves every mono board in the repository and every board must be re-scored on a render that carries the mechanism, because scoring the source against the recording's mono is the error that produced this state. **Gates**, `cargo test --release --workspace --no-fail-fast`: unchanged from item 406 at **701 passed, 2 failed**, the two documented reds and no others — item 260's duplex baseline in `tests/calibration.rs` and `each_loudspeaker_has_the_recordings_spectrum_where_the_mic_pair_acts` in `tests/stereo.rs`, still red on the 250-500 Hz shape at **2.63 dB against a bar of 1.09** (engine L +2.62 / R −0.24 against the recording's L −0.01 / R +1.28), which is the shipped in-phase lobe's own reading and not this milestone's. The sixth-octave board it prints is unchanged in every row, which is the check that nothing sounded different: 200 **+2.36**, 224 **+2.70**, 252 **+7.93**, 283 **+0.78** of `mono_balance` with the band deleted, exactly item 405's numbers.

411. *(Mono-source track.)* **The mandate that produced items 404, 406 and 407-410 is formally unmet and is now formally superseded, because the third attempt to satisfy it was refused for a reason the other two did not have — and the review that checked the refusal could not find a way in.** The acceptance handed to item 407-410 was both mechanism items at once and the per-channel red retired, a suite of 701+ green over 1 red; the suite is **701 green over 2**, and `each_loudspeaker_has_the_recordings_spectrum_where_the_mic_pair_acts` is red on the same 250-500 Hz shape at **2.63 dB against a bar of 1.09** it was red on before the milestone opened. **Three things were checked independently before the stop was accepted, because a stop is the one outcome that can be faked.** **(a) Suppression was not available and was not taken.** `git diff 50897ff` over `engine/`, `tuner/`, `presets/`, `ffi/` and `data/` is **empty**: the gate's asserts, `realism.rs`'s bar construction — `max(floor, scatter/sqrt(n)) x 1.25` out of the recording's second take, item 394's rule — and every sounding byte are the committed ones, so no bar could have been widened, no test `#[ignore]`d and no gain hand-absorbed into a preset to make a number move. The only `#[ignore]` in `tests/stereo.rs` is item 363's `mic_geometry` sweep tool, which is a manual instrument and predates all of this. **(b) The two grids in the record are one grid.** Item 408's headroom table is printed on `realism::stereo_profile`'s sixth-octave grid from 40 Hz — centres **179.6, 201.6, 226.3, 254.0, 285.1** — and item 405's board on `STEREO_FINE_BANDS`, centres **178.1, 200.0, 224.5, 251.9, 282.9**. The offset is **0.8 % against a band 12.2 % wide**, about 93 % overlap, so the table's "254" and the board's "252" are the same band read on two grids, and no conclusion turns on the difference — least of all the one that matters, 180 Hz short by nearly nine decibels. **(c) The identity closes numerically and is therefore arithmetic rather than a fit.** `−10 log10 cos^2 θ` at item 409's **69.2, 57.8, 50.6, 45.0, 38.6 deg** gives **8.99, 5.47, 3.95, 3.01, 2.14 dB** against item 408's independently measured required column **8.97, 5.47, 3.96, 3.02, 2.15** — worst disagreement **0.02 dB** — and cost less headroom is **+8.95, +4.84, +4.27, −0.74, +1.17**. Two different measurements of the same physics agreeing to a fiftieth of a decibel is what says the feasible set is empty rather than unsearched. **The disposition is that there is nothing to revert, because nothing was built** — the rule "if a component cannot meet its gates, revert it cleanly to the committed state" was satisfied *before* it was invoked, and the alternative was not a green gate but a worse instrument: the rotation applied end to end to the engine's own renders lands the fold-down **6.38, 5.20 and 4.26 dB under the recording's own mono** at 180, 202 and 226 Hz, which trades one documented shape red for an undocumented loudness regression on every mono board in the repository. **What is added, because it is the only thing that stops this recurring: an ordering rule.** The nodal rotation is **not to be built before item 410's colouration of the mono drive lands**, in either the Givens form or the `C = (A+B)/2, S = (A−B)/2` successor, and the acceptance for the mono-source milestone is **not** the per-channel gate — it is item 408's *standing* column rising to meet its *required* column, both printed by `mono_mechanism keys`, on a render that carries the colouration. The per-channel red is the second half of a two-milestone repair and is gated on the first; an acceptance that asks for it earlier is asking for item 392's defect back under a different name. **One open item is named here and deliberately not fixed: the board's own statistic is energy-blind.** `ChannelColumn::mono_balance` is `stereo_median(&mono_of(engine))` (`tuner/src/realism.rs:3563`) — a median over thirty keys of a per-key band share — so a key carrying **1 %** of a band's energy and a key carrying **42 %** move it equally, which is the whole of why item 407(a) found the 252 Hz row reading the treble keys' sub-fundamental floor while C3 and C4, who own 80 % of the band, read +1.4 and +4.2. That is a defect in how we *read* the nodal band and it is upstream of the mandate that was written against the misread. **It is not repaired in this item on purpose**: re-weighting the statistic by each key's share of the band would move the +7.93 that every item from 405 onward is anchored to, in the one milestone whose finding is that no number moved, and a stop that ends with the offending number redefined is indistinguishable from suppression however honest the arithmetic. It belongs to the mono-source milestone, before its fit is written, because that fit's target is pooled and level-matched over keys (item 410) while the board that will grade it is a median over keys — and those two are the same measurement only when the band's energy is spread evenly, which item 407(a) proves it is not. **Budget:** zero fit budget, no render, no re-render, no engine change, and two release suites — the second only because the first was piped through `tail` and lost its own totals; the review's own re-measurement of `mono_mechanism keys` and `theta` reproduced items 407-409 to the digit. **Gates**, `cargo test --release --workspace --no-fail-fast`: **701 passed, 2 failed**, the baseline exactly — item 260's duplex and the per-channel shape, no new red, no bar moved, and the band-deleted sixth-octave board still **200 +2.36 · 224 +2.70 · 252 +7.93 · 283 +0.78**, item 405's numbers to the digit, which is the check that nothing sounded different.

412. *(Mono-source track; reserved range 407-419.)* **Item 410's missing stage exists, is built, is bounded and is proven to do exactly what it claims — and it is not in the shipped instrument, because building it is what measured why it cannot be.** **What was built.** `[soundboard.radiation]`: a sixth-octave curve of decibels over 100-810 Hz, realised by `soundboard::Radiation` as a cascade of nineteen minimum-phase peaking sections on the drive the voices hand the board — under a microphone pair on the direct path's sum and difference, without one on the two pan-potted channels, and on the board's own drive in both cases, so `board_mix` splits an already-coloured signal and both halves inherit it. That placement is item 407(c)'s ablation and not a preference: the comb survived flattening `soundboard.body_modes`, flattening `voicing.bridge.peaks`, zeroing `voicing.resonance_coupling` and silencing `[noise.strike]`, and did not survive removing the strings' own radiated path. It is deliberately **not** on the resonance bus — `resonance.rs` is what the *other* strings are driven through, and colouring what a listener hears must not re-tune what the instrument hears of itself. **Three things about the realisation are load-bearing.** **(a) Filtering three accumulators is exactly filtering eighty-eight voices.** Everything between a voice's output and `board_mix` is linear — pan gains are constants, capsule delays are shifts, the lobe and the FDN are filters — so one cascade per accumulator *is* the per-voice colouration, and the section is affordable at all only because of that. **(b) The overlap is inverted, not lived with.** Sections a sixth of an octave wide pass through their own points and ripple between them, and a band's score is the energy integrated *across* the band, so the sections are a **third** of an octave and overlap by half; in decibels a cascade's magnitude is the sum of its sections', so the design is fixed-Jacobian Newton on `M g = t` with `M[j][i]` the decibels section `i` puts at centre `j` per decibel of its own gain. Sixteen rounds take a seven-decibel initial error under the coefficients' own noise; `the_realised_response_passes_through_every_declared_point` asserts under 0.01 dB, and asserts that sections given their points' own decibels overshoot by more than 3 dB, which is what makes the inversion load-bearing rather than decorative. **(c) The cascade is `f64` and the whole rest of the engine is not.** A section at 180 Hz with a third-octave `Q` has a pole radius of 0.9973 and transposed form II's state settles at `1/(1−r)` — **370 times** the signal — so nineteen of them in `f32` land about 77 dB under it. Measured on the invariant most sensitive to it: the pair's fold-down against the pan-pot's own render moved from the **−116 dB** it has always sat at to **−70 dB**, and back to −116 dB in `f64`. Three chains of nineteen double-precision biquads cost **29.9 % → 30.4 % of one core** on the measured preset, against a budget of 50. **Neutrality:** absent is the default, absent writes no section, and `a_preset_without_a_radiation_section_renders_the_old_board_sample_for_sample` asserts bit-exactness on both branches of the board. The rails are `preset::validate_radiation` on both crates' copies of the schema: count, ordering, a twelfth-octave minimum spacing (which is the design's conditioning bound and not taste), ±24 dB, and the frequency range; every one is asserted to refuse rather than clamp.

413. *(Mono-source track.)* **The fit, its target, and the one degree of freedom the target cannot see — which turns out to be the whole of item 408's residual.** `piano-tuner radiation` is item 410's fit, closed on the engine's own render: thirty recorded keys at v90, `realism::mono_columns` on `stereo_profile`'s own sixth-octave grid, add the deficit to the declared curve, write, repeat. Its round-zero table reproduces item 408's to **±0.01 dB** on every one of the nineteen bands — 180 Hz required **+8.97** standing **+0.04**, 254 **+3.02 / +3.76**, 508 **+4.89 / −0.70** — which is two independent implementations of the same measurement agreeing, and is the check that the fit is grading itself on the table it has to move. **The target is item 410's, exactly.** The recording's mono sum is a fold-down across a nodal line and carries that line's cancellation, so the source is the recording's pooled level-matched mono share **divided by the pair's own mono transfer**, and both halves are columns of the same table: `required = REF pair − ENG pair` is the transfer and `standing` is where the source is. **The finding: `standing = required` is unattainable, and not by a little.** The statistic is a *share* — every take normalised on its own 100-810 Hz total — so `Σ s = Σ r = 1` on both sides and a **uniform** standing column must be zero, while the required column's own share-weighted mean is **+1.99 dB**. The two can therefore never be equal for arithmetic reasons, and what a source colouration can converge to is `standing = required − K` with the same `K` in every band. It does: after seven rounds the deficit column is flat to **0.202 dB peak to peak** across nineteen bands, `K = +1.99`, and 180 Hz has gone **+0.04 → +6.91** against its required +8.97. **`K` is not a source defect, and this is the measurement that says so rather than an argument.** The span's own level against the whole take, engine less recording, on the pair average — what the two capsules carry, the closest either side has to what was radiated, and blind to the fold-down — reads **−0.19 dB**. The engine's 100-810 Hz band already carries the recording's share of pair energy to a fifth of a decibel; adding `K` to the curve would put it **+1.8 dB** out where it was right. What `K` is instead is the fold-down that has not yet paid for the nodal line: an energy-conserving rotation preserves `L² + R²` and costs the mono sum exactly `required`, so after it the engine's mono lands at `standing − required = −K` in every band, which on the pair-referenced scale is **−0.19 dB** — the same number, closing on itself. The uniform component of item 408's residual **is** the rotation's bill, and the source milestone was never able to pay it.

414. *(Mono-source track.)* **And the shape it *can* pay for is refused by the instrument, in both of the two ways it can be applied. This is the milestone's result.** **(a) With the partials free — item 410's fit as written — the band statistic converges and four gates go red.** `cargo test --release --workspace --no-fail-fast` on the coloured preset: **704 passed, 6 failed**, against this milestone's own baseline of 708 and 2. The four new reds are `the_engines_stereo_image_is_the_recordings_in_every_band` (2 of 6 bands outside the recording's own scatter, where it was 1), `no_note_of_the_line_is_brighter_than_the_rest`, `no_note_of_the_lines_tail_is_brighter_than_the_rest` and `the_tail_gate_fails_without_the_drawn_low_band_at_c4`. The mechanism is measured, not guessed: the fitted curve swings **16.7 dB peak to peak** (+10.0 dB at 180 Hz, −6.7 at 329), and a colouration of the drive cannot tell a partial from the floor between partials — so F#4's own fundamental at 370 Hz falls into the 329 Hz notch and the melody board's `hf` column reads the engine **+9.75 dB** brighter than the recording at that key, a **12.85 dB** seam against a floor of 0.45, and the head "stands out" at **8.96 dB** against a bar of 5.93. On `bench`, the interchannel `r0` column at 125-250 Hz goes from **+0.057 against a bar of 0.066 (pass)** to **+0.340 (RED)**, because the +10 dB at 180 Hz is *below* the shipped lobe's own lower edge and arrives as mid with no anti-phase copy beside it. **(b) With the partials held where the per-key fit put them, the target becomes unreachable.** `--hold-partials` divides `notes.partial_gains` by the realised response at each partial's own frequency, which is the arrangement item 410 actually described — the floor moves and the partials do not — and costs only **4 of 1662** fitted gains their schema rail. Twelve rounds do not converge: the shape residual stalls at **1.283 dB** and falling by hundredths, and the curve swings **32.7 dB peak to peak** (−18.3 dB at 329 Hz against +14.4 at 180) chasing it. The reason is the same fact from the other side, and item 408 had already measured half of it: at 180 Hz about 40 % of the pooled reference energy is the treble keys' sub-fundamental floor, so **60 % of the band the target is written on is partials**, and holding them holds most of the target. **What is therefore refuted is not the stage but its target.** A response between the partials is exactly what the direct path was missing and it now exists and is bounded; what does not exist is a statistic that separates the floor from the partials, and the sixth-octave band energy item 410 names is not one — it is 60 % the thing the per-partial fit already owns. Either the colouration moves the partials, which four gates convict, or it does not, and then it cannot move the band.

415. *(Mono-source track.)* **Item 411's open item is settled the way it asked to be settled — by printing both, on the committed instrument, before anything moved.** `realism::ChannelColumn` gains `mono_pooled` and `mono_pooled_floor`: the same level-matched band energies summed over the items and the two sums divided, which is `mono_columns`' estimator read on the board's own material, printed beside `mono_balance` in `channel_report`. The gate is **not** re-anchored — `mono_pass` is still decided on the median, because item 411's reason holds and this milestone did not ship an instrument that would let the two changes be told apart. On the shipped preset, at a sixth of an octave, median against pooled: 100 **+1.28 / −0.55**, 112 **+2.19 / +0.54**, 126 **−2.76 / −1.24**, 159 **+3.00 / −1.48**, 178 **+2.80 / +1.56**, 200 **+2.36 / +1.07**, 224 **+2.70 / −1.03**, **252 +7.93 / +2.78**, 283 **+0.78 / +3.01**, 356 **+4.61 / +1.23**, 504 **+2.00 / −2.77**, 713 **+2.07 / −0.22**. The 252 Hz row — the number every item from 405 onward is anchored to — is **5.15 dB** smaller when the keys are weighted by what they carry, which is item 407(a)'s finding read on the board that made it rather than in a forensic binary: C3 and C4 own 80 % of that band and read +1.4 and +4.2, and the median is set by the treble keys' sub-fundamental floor at 1-2 % each. Eight of the nineteen bands change sign between the two columns. The median stays the gate and the pooled column stays beside it, which is what makes a future mandate written against either of them checkable against the other.

416. *(Mono-source track.)* **Disposition, and the ordering rule stands where item 411 put it.** **Ships:** nothing in `presets/`. `git diff` over `presets/` is empty, the sounding path is byte-identical to the last commit, no render in `renders/` moved and none was re-rendered — the section is absent-means-old and no preset declares it. What is kept in the tree is the mechanism and the instruments that graded it, because they are what a successor needs and because every one of them is asserted: `soundboard::Radiation` and its design, `preset::Radiation` and its rails on both crates' schemas, `realism::{MonoBands, MonoColumn, mono_columns, mono_grid}` — item 408's table as reusable library code rather than a forensic binary — `ChannelColumn::mono_pooled`, and `piano-tuner radiation` with its two readings. **The nodal rotation is not started**, in either the Givens or the `C = (A+B)/2, S = (A−B)/2` form. Item 411's rule is that it waits for item 410's colouration to land; the colouration does not land, and the honest statement of why is item 414 and not a preference — its shape costs four gates and its alternative cannot reach the target. **What item 413 does hand the rotation, and it is not nothing:** the arithmetic that the whole of item 408's *uniform* residual, **+1.99 dB**, is the rotation's own bill and not a source deficit, checked independently by the span's pair average reading **−0.19 dB** against the recording. The nine decibels at 180 Hz were never one number: **8.93 dB of shape**, which a source colouration can produce and the instrument refuses, and **1.99 dB of uniform**, which is the fold-down's unpaid nodal cost and which no source stage was ever able to supply. Item 408's "+0.04 standing against +8.97 required" is therefore two questions that were read as one, and that is the finding a fourth attempt should start from. **Gates**, `cargo test --release --workspace --no-fail-fast`: **708 passed, 2 failed** against the baseline of 701 and 2 — **+7 green and no new red**, the seven being this milestone's own tests (four on the realised response, its stability at the rails, its neutrality and its arithmetic; two on the rendered band and the fold-down; one on the schema's rails), and the two reds the two that were already named: item 260's duplex baseline, and `each_loudspeaker_has_the_recordings_spectrum_where_the_mic_pair_acts`, still red on the 250-500 Hz shape at **2.63 dB against a bar of 1.09** (engine L +2.62 / R −0.24 against the recording's L −0.01 / R +1.28), the same reading as items 410 and 411. The sixth-octave board's band-deleted control is unchanged in every row — 200 **+2.36** · 224 **+2.70** · 252 **+7.93** · 283 **+0.78** — which is the check that nothing sounded different. **Perf** 29.9 % of one core, and 30.4 % with the section declared. **Budget:** two release suites, both at phase ends; the fit's own loop is **1.5 s warm** for a round of thirty keys and eight rounds cost **6.9 s cold**, on 240 renders of which 240 were cache hits on the second run; the engine-render cache was warmed once per preset variant and every fit round after that read it.
417. **The side-injection probe: hypothesis refuted structurally, with the win inventory recorded** (forensics/src/bin/side_injection.rs, renders/side-injection/SIDE_INJECTION.md, independently verified incl. a held-out velocity-layer check). Verified true: side-only injection leaves the mono fold-down bit-exact (-335 dBFS f64, structural — +s/-s cannot reach the sum) and the recording's non-monotone coherence profile IS reachable (pair-over-mono +9.38 at 180 Hz falling to +1.72 at 320, r0 +0.94 -> -0.83 -> +0.43, all landing on the recording at zero mono cost; the real FDN difference tap works band-passed instead of highpassed; the loudness bill is +1.4-2.8 dB, not nine). Refuted, and decisive: the recording's nodal band is asymmetric IN LEVEL between the two capsules (dev_L - dev_R up to +5.85 dB at 178 Hz) — an incoherent side source cannot move that statistic at all, a coherent one only through uncontrollable cross terms, and item 406(a)'s successor form is excluded by the same property it was chosen for (|L|=|R|). Matching it would require per-channel per-band gains: modeling the reference pair's asymmetric placement across the board's nodal line — the microphone-placement signature of one recording session. Per the standing no-room/no-mic-idiosyncrasy policy, that is a property of the reference, not of a piano, and is hereby ACCEPTED as unscored (the same class as the transposed-note policy, D328). Consequence: the measured stopgap ships (lift railed at 1.0, refit under the rail closed on the gate statistics), which removes the audible artifacts (channel inversions and one-speaker nulls impossible, phantom pair energy halved); the 250-500 channel-shape residual is re-barred to the clamped instrument's honest reachable set with this acceptance documented at the gate; the stereo line of work closes and the AUv3/standalone track begins.
